@@ 172 | Epic Series - 55 | Can Just Listening to Bhajans Heal Us? | 
A question from a reader:

“I understand bhakti… devotion… But people say even listening to Namasankeerthanam or bhajans has calming, healing effects.

How is that possible? Is it only belief… or is there something more happening?”

Very practical question !

First… observe your own experience.

When you hear loud noise… mind becomes restless. When you hear soft music… mind becomes calm. So clearly… sound affects us.

Now think of Namasankeerthanam…

Same music… but with divine names… repeated again and again… with rhythm… with feeling…

Something different happens. Let us understand this…

First… Mind

Our mind is always crowded…Past thoughts… future worries… continuous thinking…

But when one name… like “Rama… Rama… Rama…” repeats…Mind slowly gets one direction…..Less distraction… less noise…Like waves settling in water…..Not forcefully… very naturally.

Second… Body

When mind slows down… body follows.

* Breathing becomes deeper

* Heartbeat becomes slower

* Body starts relaxing

Without doing anything…system moves from stress → rest. This is why many people say: “I don’t know why… but I feel peaceful after bhajans.”

Third… Emotions

Namasankeerthanam is not dry music…It carries devotion… surrender… love…

As you listen…Heart becomes soft…Tension reduces…Sometimes… even tears come….Not sadness… but release.

Now let us go deeper…

Is this something new? Or did our ancestors know this? Let us take a simple example from our heritage…

In the Ramayana…

When Lord Rama was in the forest, sages and devotees constantly chanted his name.

Even in difficult situations… fear and uncertainty… they remained calm.

Why?

Because the Nama (divine name) itself became their strength…..Later, in our bhakti tradition…Saints like Tyagaraja, Purandara Dasa, Meera…

They did not just sing for art…They sang the name again and again.

And what happened?

Not only they experienced bliss…Even people around them felt peace….Same sound… same name… but powerful impact.

Now understand one simple truth: Namasankeerthanam works at two levels.

Level 1: Without bhakti

Even if you just listen…

* Mind gets focus

* Body relaxes

* Emotions settle

Like any soothing sound… but deeper.

Level 2: With bhakti

Now it becomes something more…

* You feel connected

* You feel supported

* You feel inner strength

It is not just calming…It becomes transforming.

So see the beauty…

Even if someone comes just for relaxation Namasankeerthanam helps.

And if someone comes with devotion…It lifts them to another level.

Now one small reflection…After a long, stressful day…

Try 10 minutes of simple nama chanting or listening.

Observe… Not analyse… just observe.

Mind will become lighter…

Body will become softer…

Heart will feel supported…

Thought for the Day

Divine name is not just for ritual… It is a medicine for the restless mind.

You may start by listening…

But slowly… it will start healing from within.

With bhakti… it elevates you.

Even without bhakti… it calms you.

That is the power of Nama.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

* At home

* At work

@@ 170 | Epic series - 53 | My mind is getting more negative as I grow older… How do I handle this? | 
My mind is getting more negative as I grow older… How do I handle this?

A middle-aged reader shares with quiet pain:

“I don’t know when this started…Earlier I used to be more relaxed…

Now my mind keeps going into negative thoughts…About health… children… money… future…

Small things affect me deeply…

I overthink… I react… and later I regret…It is slowly affecting my peace… and my relationships…

Can spirituality or satsang really help someone like me?”

Many of us can relate to this situation in our life . This phase is very real for many people in mid-life. Because at this stage…

* Responsibilities are high

* Energy is slightly lower

* Uncertainty about future increases

* And expectations from life are very different

So the mind becomes… overactive. Not peaceful… not stable… always thinking. Let us see some real-life situations you may relate to…

Scenario 1: Health worry

You feel a small pain in your body…Mind immediately says:

“What if this is something serious…?…….What if I become dependent on others…?”

Within minutes… fear builds up.

Scenario 2: Children

Your son/daughter doesn’t listen or behaves differently…

Mind says: “Where did I go wrong…?…..What if their life gets spoiled…?”

You feel hurt… anxious… sometimes even angry.

Scenario 3: Financial thoughts

You sit quietly… suddenly a thought comes: “Is what I have saved enough…?……What if something goes wrong later…?”

Even without any immediate problem…tension starts.

Scenario 4: Relationships

Someone close speaks a little harshly…Mind says: “They don’t respect me anymore…

After everything I have done…”

You withdraw… or react emotionally.

Now tell me honestly…

Are these thoughts 100% facts? Or are they fear projections?

This is the turning point…

Most of these are not realities…They are habitual thinking patterns……And if repeated daily…they become your mental environment.

Now a very practical and relatable tip…

The “Pause & Park” method

Next time a negative thought comes…Don’t go deep into it immediately.

Just tell your mind:

“Not now… I will think about this at 6 pm.”

Yes… literally schedule your worry. It may sound simple… but it works. Because…

* It breaks the immediate emotional reaction

* It gives you control over timing

* Many thoughts lose intensity by evening

By the time 6 pm comes…half the thoughts will not even feel important. This is how you slowly train the mind.

Now let us see how spirituality and satsang help here…Not in theory… but in real life. When you regularly expose yourself to satsang:

You start hearing and absorbing thoughts like:

* “Life cannot be controlled fully…”

* “Children have their own journey…”

* “Worrying does not solve… it only drains…”

* “Accept… and act calmly…”

Slowly… these ideas replace your fear-based thinking.

Real shift example:

Earlier:

Child not picking call → “They are ignoring me… something is wrong…”

After satsang influence:

“Maybe they are busy… let me not jump to conclusions…”

See the difference?

Same situation…But your mind is not creating unnecessary suffering.

Another powerful practice for middle-aged minds:

Daily “Mental Clean-up” (5 minutes at night)….Before sleeping… just sit quietly…

Ask yourself:

* “What disturbed me today?”

* “Was it reality… or my interpretation?”

Then tell yourself:

“I release this for today… I will handle tomorrow calmly.”

This simple closure prevents accumulation. Otherwise… thoughts pile up day after day…and become heaviness.

And one more deeply relatable truth…At this stage of life…

You are not struggling because life is difficult…You are struggling because

your mind is constantly predicting, comparing, and fearing. Spirituality helps you step out of this cycle.

Not by changing your external responsibilities….But by changing your inner handling.

You become:

* Less reactive

* More accepting

* Emotionally stronger

Not because life became easy…But because you became steady.

Finally… understand this gently…You have handled so much in life to reach here…

But now…it is time to learn how to handle your own mind.

Because…

If the mind becomes your friend…this phase of life can be the most peaceful.

If not…even small things will feel heavy. So don’t aim for a perfect mind…

Just aim for a slightly calmer mind… every day. That itself is progress. And that itself is spirituality in action.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

Like a relationship…

If you say, “I care”… but never spend time… never connect…

Maybe…

* 2 minutes of quiet gratitude before sleep

* One conscious visit to temple with awareness (not compulsion)

* Work becomes calmer

* Decisions become clearer

* Stress reduces

* Purpose becomes stronger

And even if it does… You may reach there externally…But feel empty internally. So don’t postpone completely. Integrate slowly.

No extremes. No force. Just small… steady connection. Finally… You are not far from the path…You are already standing on it. Just take one small step… today.

Because…Spirituality is not about leaving life….It is about not missing life while living it.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 168 | Epic Series – 51 | Why Guru? What does a Guru really do? | 
A reader asks with honesty:

“I pray deeply… I believe in God… I try to live a good life… I don’t follow any guru… Why should I? What exactly does a guru do? And I am also afraid… what if I choose the wrong one?”

A very sincere question indeed to get clarity..

Many of us feel…”If I am sincere… if I pray well… that is enough.”

Sincerity is powerful… no question. But sincerity alone does not guarantee right direction.

You can walk very fast… with full faith….But if the direction is slightly wrong… you will still go far away from the destination.

Prayer gives strength. Guidance gives direction.

So what does a Guru actually do?

A Guru does not give you something new. He helps you see clearly what you are unable to see by yourself.

Like a mirror…Your face is already there…But without a mirror… you miss details.

In the same way…

A Guru shows:

* Your blind spots

* Your repeated patterns

* Your hidden ego

* Your wrong assumptions

Not to judge you…But to refine you.

A man was trying to straighten a photo frame on the wall. He kept adjusting again and again… checking… correcting…

Still… it looked slightly off. After a long struggle… his friend came and said: “The wall itself is tilted.” In one moment… everything became clear.

The man’s effort was not wrong…His reference was wrong.

This is our life. We keep correcting actions…But sometimes… our understanding itself is slightly tilted. Guru corrects that base angle.

Even in the great Mahabharata,

the mighty Arjuna…

* Was sincere

* Was skilled

* Was dharmic

Yet… at a crucial moment… he was completely confused. At that time… Krishna did not give him power…He gave him clarity. That clarity transformed everything.

That is Guru’s role.

Not to control your life…But to clear your confusion.

Now the important question…

”What if I don’t have a Guru?”

Nothing is “wrong”. You can still live a good, meaningful life. But observe carefully…

Without guidance:

* You learn mostly through trial and error

* Same mistakes repeat for longer

* Confusion takes more time to clear

* Ego quietly hides as “I know enough”

Life moves…But with more struggle… more delay… more inner noise.

With right guidance:

* You see faster

* You correct earlier

* You understand deeper

* You grow with less friction

Same life… but more clarity.

Now the genuine fear… ”What if I choose the wrong Guru?”

This fear is valid. Because wrong guidance can create:

* Dependency

* Fear

* Blind following

* Loss of thinking

So don’t rush. But don’t close the door either. Be aware… not afraid.

How to recognise the right guidance?

Very simply…

* It reduces confusion… not increases it

* It makes you think… not blindly follow

* It brings inner quietness… not pressure

* It points you towards truth… not towards personality

After connecting… you will feel lighter… not controlled.

Also remember…Guru is not always a person in a traditional form.

Sometimes…

* A book awakens you

* A life situation shakes you

* A single sentence changes your direction

And sometimes… a living guide enters your life… at the right time.

So what should you do now?

Continue your sincere prayer. At the same time… stay open… observant… grounded. Don’t rush to accept. Don’t rush to reject. Let understanding grow naturally.

Finally….

Prayer connects you to the Divine…Guru helps you understand that connection clearly. Walking alone is possible… But right guidance removes unnecessary confusion.

Because in life…

Effort is important…But right understanding is everything.

When the seeker is ready…

Guidance appears… in the right form… at the right time.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 167 | Epic Series - 50 | Good Person… Still Suffering? Why? | 
A candid question from a reader:

“I am a God-fearing person. I always try to do good. I have not harmed anyone knowingly. Still… life is full of problems. One shock after another… in job… in family…

Sometimes I feel… why God punishes people who are truthful and loyal?…..I feel frustrated… disappointed… even disillusioned… Why is this happening?”

Many of us can relate to this situation. Let us explore further:

A sincere heart…Trying to walk the right path…And still… facing pain again and again…

So naturally… a question comes: “Am I doing something wrong? Or is God unfair?”

Now… let us go step by step… very simply.

Step 1: Life is not a “Reward System

Many of us believe:

1. Do good → Good will happen immediately

2. Do bad → Bad will happen immediately

But life is not a school exam where marks come instantly. Life is more like a seed and tree. You plant a mango seed today…Will you get mango tomorrow?

No…It takes time… seasons… patience.

Same way…

* Some results come from past actions (we may not even remember)

* Some results come from present actions

* Some results are training for future strength

So… not every pain is punishment.

Step 2: Good people don’t escape problems… They handle them differently

Look at this carefully…Two people face the same problem:

* One breaks down

* One becomes stronger

Problem is same… but response is different. Being good does not mean:

“No problems”

It means: “Better strength to face problems”

Step 3: A Story

There was a goldsmith. He puts gold into fire… heats it again and again…Someone asked: “Why are you burning this gold? It is already good!”

Goldsmith smiled and said: “I am not burning… I am purifying. Only when it goes through fire… it shines fully.”

Now think…

When life puts you in “fire”…

Maybe it is not to destroy you…Maybe it is to refine you.

Step 4: Insight from Epic

In the Mahabharata…The Pandavas were truthful… dharmic… good-hearted. But did they have an easy life?

No…

* Lost kingdom

* Went to forest

* Faced insult and pain

While Duryodhana… who did wrong… enjoyed for some time. So is life unfair?

No…At the end:

* Pandavas gained inner strength + lasting victory

* Duryodhana lost everything

So lesson is: Short term may look unfair… Long term is always balanced

Step 5: Hidden Mistake we do

Sometimes we think: “I am good… so nothing bad should happen to me”. This expectation itself creates pain.

Doing good is not a business deal with God. It is your nature… your choice… your dharma. When you expect return… frustration starts.

Step 6: What is really happening to you?

Let us decode your situation:

* Repeated problems → Life is trying to teach something deeply

* Frustration → Mind is expecting control

* Disillusionment → Faith is shaken by expectations

So the question is not:

“Why is God punishing me?”

Better question is:

“What is life trying to teach me now?”

Step 7: Useful tip

Simple… doable…

* Stop asking “Why me?”

Start asking: “What now?”

* Write your problems clearly

Many times… mind mixes everything and creates fear

* Take one issue at a time

Life feels heavy when everything is combined

* Strengthen inner routine

Prayer… silence… simple discipline…..Not for result… but for stability

* Talk to one trusted person

Don’t carry everything alone

Step 8: One Powerful Truth

Rain falls on all houses.

But:

* Strong roof → safe

* Weak roof → leakage

You cannot stop the rain…But you can strengthen your roof

Final Reflection

Right now you are seeing:

Pain → “God is unfair”

Try to slowly shift to:

Pain → “Life is shaping me”

Thought for the Day

Being good is not a guarantee for an easy life…It is a preparation for a strong life.

Don’t measure God by your problems…Measure your growth by how you rise through them.

Because sometimes…The same situation that breaks one person…Builds another person into something extraordinary.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 166 | Epic Series - 49 | Close… Yet Clashing? | 
A question from a reader:

“I have teenage children. We come from a traditional family.

Now they ask for late night parties… sleepovers… more freedom. Most of the times at home, they are glued to their mobile phones.

Whenever we say ‘No’… a big fight starts at home. Every weekend… we feel scared… what problem will come now?

They lock their room. They don’t listen. We feel we are losing them.

At the same time… if we don’t control… we feel unsafe… especially for our daughter.

We are confused… Where to draw the line? What should we do?”

This problem is very familiar to most of the parents. Let me not answer to this reader immediately…

First… feel this home.

Parents who gave love… care… values…Now standing confused…

“Where did we go wrong?”

And children… growing fast… wanting space… wanting to feel “I am big now”.

So what is happening here?

Not a “bad child”…Not a “bad parent”…It is a clash of two needs:

• Parent says: “I want to protect you”

• Child says: “I want to live my life”

Both are right…But both are speaking in the wrong way.

So what happens?

• Care becomes control

• Freedom becomes rebellion

• Talk becomes argument

• Home becomes stress

Now… let us see deeper . Teenage is like standing at the door. One foot inside the house….One foot outside the world…

They are not children fully…

Not adults fully…So confusion is natural.

Now think…

If you hold too tight…They will try to break free. If you leave fully…They may go in wrong direction.So parenting here is like flying a kite…

Too tight → thread breaks

Too loose → kite falls

Right balance… that is the art.

Let us take a small insight from the Epic…In the Mahabharata… Abhimanyu was brave…young… powerful…But he was not guided fully for the complete battle.Half knowledge… high confidence… can be dangerous.

Same with teenagers…

They have confidence… exposure… friends…But still need guidance… silently.

Now… what can you do?

Very simple… very practical…

• Stop “No” as first word

If every answer is “No”…They will stop asking.

Instead say:

“Let us talk… tell me more…”

When they feel heard… they will listen.

• Set rules… but together

Don’t say: “You cannot go.”

Say: “If you go… what time will you come back?”

“How will you stay safe?”

Make rules with them… not for them.

• Give freedom… with limits

Example:

• Yes for party

• But fixed return time

• Known friends only

• Phone always reachable

This gives both: Freedom + Safety

• Don’t enter their room… enter their world

Locked room is not the real problem…Closed heart is the real problem. Instead of forcing door open…

Open conversation: Sit casually… talk normally… no lecture.

• Choose your battles

Every small thing need not become Kurukshetra. Ask yourself: “Will this matter after 5 years?”

If not… let go. Save your energy for important things.

• Build trust… slowly

Say clearly: “I trust you… but I worry because I love you.”

This one line can change everything.

• For daughter… don’t show fear… show strength. Fear creates resistance.

Instead teach:

• Awareness

• Safety

• Confidence

Make her strong… not scared.

One important truth. You cannot control a teenager fully.

If you try… you will lose connection. And once connection is lost…Control also goes.

So first protect relationship…Then guide behaviour.

One small reflection

Right now…

Control → Fight → Distance

Change to:

Connect → Understand → Guide

Thought for the Day

Teenage is not a problem phase…It is a sensitive phase.

If you handle with control… you lose them. If you handle with connection… you shape them.

Don’t try to win over your children…Try to stay close to them.

Because in the end…Children don’t listen to strict parents…They listen to parents they feel safe with.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 165 | Epic Series -48 | Together… Yet Trapped? | 
A question from a reader:

“I am 71. I want to live a meaningful, peaceful life… without interfering in my children’s lives. But inside my own home… I feel restricted.

Even small things like meeting friends or attending satsang become a reason for conflict. My spouse insists I live only as per her way.

This leads to arguments… frustration… and sometimes a thought: Is separation the only way to find peace?

I feel stuck… What should I do?”

Let me not answer to this reader immediately…

First… feel this situation.

A man who has completed his responsibilities…Now standing at the evening of life…Where he seeks only three things:

• Some peace

• Some meaning

• Some freedom to be himself

But instead… He feels questioned…corrected… controlled…Not outside…Inside his own home. That hurts the most.

But now… look deeper…This is not just about one spouse controlling another. This is a silent power struggle between two fears:

• One says: “I want freedom before life ends…”

• The other says: “Don’t leave me alone in this phase…”

One is seeking space. The other is seeking security. And both are expressing it in the wrong language.

Where it becomes dangerous…When this continues…Love slowly changes form:

• Care becomes control

• Sharing becomes shouting

• Togetherness becomes tension

And then a new thought enters quietly: “Maybe I should walk away…”

But pause… very carefully…At this age…Separation is not just a “solution”. It is a life-altering decision with emotional, social, and spiritual consequences.

Sometimes it gives relief…But many times…It replaces one pain with another… loneliness.

Let us go to the Epic…

Think of King Dhritarashtra from the Mahabharata. He held on tightly to what he feared losing…Control… family… authority…

But that holding did not create peace……It created suffocation around him… and suffering within him.

The real problem here is not your spouse…And not even the situation…

The real problem is this: Two people… both hurting…but both trying to correct the other… instead of understanding the other.

Now… what is the way out?

Not theory… very practical… very honest.

• Accept one truth clearly….You cannot have complete freedom AND complete control over the relationship. Some adjustment is the price of companionship.

If you want peaceful togetherness…You must give up the need to “win”.

• Address the root… not the behaviour…..Don’t fight this: “Why are you stopping me?”

Speak to the deeper layer: “At this stage… I want to live meaningfully… not just pass time.”

And also acknowledge her fear: “I know you feel insecure when I go out often…” This melts resistance.

• Create structure… not rebellion…..Freedom taken suddenly feels like rejection.

Instead, build rhythm:

• Fixed days for satsang

• Fixed time for friends

• Shared activities together

When life becomes predictable… fear reduces.

• Restore dignity… without hurting…This is Very important. Calmly… firmly… respectfully say: “These few things are important for my peace. I will continue them… but I will not neglect you.” No anger. No guilt. No over-explanation.

This is mature boundary.

• Watch your reaction pattern

Right now…

Control → Reaction → Argument → Distance

Break it here:

Control → Pause → Calm response → No escalation

One person changing…changes the entire energy.

About separation… be very honest….Ask yourself quietly:

• Am I seeking peace… or escape from discomfort?

• Will I truly be happier alone… or just temporarily relieved?

If the answer is not 100% clear… Don’t take that step now.

One powerful reflection

At 71…Life is not asking: “How do I change my spouse?”

It is asking: “How do I respond with wisdom… in this final phase?”

Thought for the Day

In the last chapter of life…We are not tested by situations…We are tested by maturity in relationships.

Sometimes…Peace does not come by breaking the bond…It comes by softening the ego… setting gentle boundaries… and understanding the hidden fears on both sides.

When understanding increases…Control reduces…..When reaction reduces…Respect returns. And slowly…The same home…Becomes peaceful again.

This is not an easy path…But it is a higher path. And at this stage of life…Only the higher path gives lasting peace.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 164 | Epic Series-47 | Belief… Truth… or Trap? | 
A question from a reader:

“From my past experience, I have formed strong beliefs about people and life. Because of this, I react in the same way again and again. It is affecting my peace and relationships… but I am unable to change. What should I do?

Let me start with a few examples which one can relate to ;

Example 1 : At Home

A mother-in-law believes: “Daughter-in-law does not respect me.”

So what happens?

• Even if the girl is quiet → she feels insulted

• Even if she is busy → she feels ignored

• Even small things hurt

Slowly…

She talks less.

She becomes cold.

Distance increases.

Now the daughter-in-law actually starts avoiding.

Then the belief becomes “true”. But see carefully…It all started from one belief.

Example 2 : At Work

A man once got cheated in business. From that day, he believes: “No one can be trusted.”

Now in office:

• He checks everything again and again

• He doubts even honest people

• He does not delegate

Team feels suffocated.They stop giving their best. Some even leave.

Now he says: “See… people are not reliable.”

But the truth is… His belief created that reality.

Example 3 : With Children

A father believes: “My son never listens.” So every time the child speaks…

He already has irritation.

• He cuts him midway

• He scolds quickly

• He does not listen fully

The child feels hurt… and stops sharing. Now the father says: “See… he does not talk to me.” Again… belief became reality.

What is happening here?

1. One past experience → one strong belief

2. Belief → attitude

3. Attitude → behaviour

4. Behaviour → habit

5. Habit → personality

And then we say: “This is who I am.”

Now let us see the epic.

Think of Hanuman.Standing near the ocean… he believed: “I cannot cross this.”

Was it true? No.

It was just a forgotten strength… covered by belief. Then Jambavan reminded him: “You are powerful.”

That moment…Belief broke. Truth came out. And he flew across the ocean.

The Insight:

Life is not always responding to reality…It is responding to your belief. If belief is wrong…

life also starts looking wrong.

Useful Tip

Try this honestly for a few days:

• Catch one repeating problem (With spouse / child / colleague)

• Ask: “Where am I getting hurt again and again?”

• Write your belief . Complete this sentence: “I believe that ______”

Example:

“They don’t respect me”

“I must always be right”

“People will hurt me”

• See the cost of this belief

• Ask: “Because of this belief… how do I behave?………”What is it doing to my relationship?”

See clearly… no justification.

Next time…

Do one different action:

• Listen fully without reacting

• Trust a little

• Stay calm instead of proving

Just one small change.

• Watch the result carefull. You will be surprised. When belief changes a little….life also starts changing.

Thought for the Day:

Many problems in life…are not because of others…but because of one belief we never questioned.

Break the belief…and you will see a new life. Like Hanuman…your strength is already inside.

Just remove the wrong belief.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 163 | Epic Series - 46 | 1000 Moons… What Does It Do to a Human Being? | 
Today, I was invited to perform a bhajan concert…I went there with my entire team…It was for the Sathabhishekam of my neighbour while we were at Trichy.. I have known him for more than 40 years.

While singing… watching him sit quietly…with a soft smile… no tension… no hurry…

One deep thought came to me. I shared it during the concert ….and I felt… this is something all of us must hear.

We all think… “80 years means body has become weak.” But what if I tell you…

According to “Deivathin Kural” by Kanchi Paramacharya Sri Chandrashekarendra Saraswati…

80 years means the MIND has gone through a powerful purification. Now listen carefully… this is very deep.

Vedas say: “Chandrama Manaso Jataha” . Moon is connected to the mind. Now see this wonder…

In 80 years…a person sees around 1000 full moons. Each full moon gives a cooling effect to the mind.

Not just physically…Mentally. Emotionally. Spiritually.

So what happens slowly?

Every full moon is like a silent teacher. Without talking… it teaches:

Be cool…

Be steady…

Do not react too much…

After 1000 times….If a person has lived properly…Something amazing should happen:

Anger becomes less…Speed of reaction becomes slow…Desires lose their force…Ego starts melting…

And finally…

Mind becomes like the moon itself , cool, soft, and peaceful.

Now comes a shocking insight from Paramacharya…

He says…If after seeing 1000 moons…the mind is still hot… angry… restless…Then we have missed the whole purpose of life.

Sathabhishekam is not a birthday function.It is like saying: “This person has completed 1000 lessons given by nature.”

And now…He is ready to live like a Rishi ; less talking… more peace… more God thinking.

There is one more powerful point…At this age…Such a person has seen three generations. He becomes a link between past, present, and future.That is why elders say…Their blessings are equal to God’s blessings.

This reminded me of a very clear moment from the Mahabharata.

Look at Bhishma. He was lying on a bed of arrows… in great pain. If it was us….we would complain… shout… feel angry.

But what did he do? He gave calm teachings to everyone. No anger… no blame… no regret.

Why?

Because his mind was already cool and steady.Pain was in the body…but peace was in the mind.

So now ask yourself one honest question: “Every year… am I becoming calmer… or just older?” Because time will pass anyway…1000 moons will come and go…But the real question is: Did those 1000 moons change my mind?

If yes…

Life becomes peaceful.

If not…

We may live long…but we may not live well.

Today… just think quietly… “Am I learning from life… or just passing through it?”

Because in the end…The greatest achievement is not long life…It is a cool, peaceful mind.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 162 | EpicSeries - 45 | Are We Living Today… or Only Talking About Yesterday? | 
At every family dinner… it quietly starts…

• ”In our days, children listened…”

But deep inside…Is he actually saying… “I feel left out”?

Tell me… Why do we keep going back to “our days”?

Because…

That world was ours. We knew how to live there. We were respected there.

But today?

Everything feels new… fast… unfamiliar. When we don’t understand something… what do we do? We go back… to what we understand. That is the past.

Slowly… Past feels right. Present feels wrong.

In the Ramayana,

There is a very touching moment between King Dasharatha and his son Rama.

Dasharatha was a great king.

He loved Rama deeply.

But one day…Because of a promise, he had to send Rama to the forest.

Now think… Could he accept this new reality?

No……His heart was stuck in the past…”My son… my palace… my way of life…”

He could not adjust to the change. He kept holding on….to how life was.

And slowly…that pain became unbearable.

What broke him?

Not Rama going away alone. But his inability…to accept that life had changed

We may not be kings.We may not face such big situations.

But inside our homes…something similar happens.

Children grow.

Their world changes.

Their thinking changes.

But we…hold on to:

“My time…”

“My way…”

“My values…”

And then we feel:

“They don’t listen.”

“They don’t respect.”

But pause…Are they rejecting us… or rejecting the comparison?

The Truth

Children don’t move away from love. They move away from feeling judged.

• No generation is wrong.

• No generation is perfect.

But when we keep saying: “Earlier was better…”

We are silently saying: “What you are doing now is not good enough.” And that hurts them.

A Small Change… A Big Magic

One day, Ravi uncle tried something different. His granddaughter asked:

“Thatha… what did you do for fun when you were small?”

This time…He didn’t compare.

He simply shared: “We played in the streets… we fell… we got up… we laughed…”

She smiled: “Thatha… teach me!”

That moment…

No past vs present

No right vs wrong

Only connection. And suddenly….He didn’t feel ignored anymore.

Why?

Because he stopped proving…

and started sharing.

A Gentle Question for All of Us

Next time, before saying: “In our days…”

Pause and ask: Am I trying to connect… or trying to correct?

Then try this: “In our days, we did it like this… how do you do it now?” See what happens.

• Conversation begins.

• Hearts open.

Essence

• Past is for sharing… not for comparing

• Change is not disrespect… it is life moving forward

• Love grows when ego becomes soft

Thought for the Day

Today… I will not just remember my past…I will also make space for the present.

Because…If I don’t understand today… how will I stay connected tomorrow?

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

Because this is not about control…It is about place……..”Where is my place now… in my child’s life?”

• “Why didn’t they ask me?”

• “Why am I not included?”

• “Have I lost my importance?”

This creates restlessness… not peace…

• Notice the urge

• Shift your role consciously; From decision-maker → to silent strength.

• Create your own space. Peace does not come from involvement…It comes from your inner acceptance

From: “They cannot manage without me”

This is not rejection…This is life moving forward

In the epic…Kaushalya did not become less of a mother…

She became a larger one…Because she allowed Rama to walk his path… with her blessings… not her control

Thought for the Day

Sometimes… peace does not come by holding on tighter…It comes by gently loosening the grip…And trusting… “What I have given them… is enough for them to live their life”

Because…When love stops trying to control…It becomes a space… where relationships can breathe

So I asked a simple question:

The reply came: “No… they should understand on their own, right?”

This is the real problem (very clearly):

• You are doing a lot…

• You are not expressing your needs…

• But you expect others to automatically understand and appreciate

And when they don’t……You feel hurt, ignored, and taken for granted

The truth

People are not mind readers. If you don’t say it….Many times, they genuinely don’t know it matters to you.

Even in the Mahabharata,

Karna lived with a similar inner pattern.

He gave endlessly.He stayed loyal. He never openly expressed his deeper need to be recognised and respected.

That silent longing didn’t disappear. It stayed… and slowly influenced his decisions.

What is happening inside?

• You feel: “I am doing so much… why can’t they see?”

• Others feel: “Everything looks normal… what’s the issue?”

• The gap is not effort.

• The gap is communication.

Important Insight

• Doing silently is not wrong

• Expecting silently is the problem

Because unspoken expectations always become disappointment

What can be done?

Instead of waiting, say it early:

• “I’m okay doing this… but it would mean a lot if you notice or support me.”

or

“I’m feeling a bit overwhelmed… can we share this?”

This is not complaining. This is clarity.

Essence

• Effort deserves expression

• Expectations need words

• Silence creates misunderstanding

Thought for the Day

I will not expect others to guess what I feel. I will express it calmly… before silence turns into hurt.

@@ 157 | Epic Series - 40 | Comfort… or Chain? | 
One rich man had to stay one night in a small village.

No AC.

No soft bed.

Food was very simple.

Power kept going off.

He could not sleep the whole night. He kept turning and feeling irritated.

In the same house, there was an old farmer. The farmer worked hard the whole day in the hot sun. ……At night, he ate simple food, lay on a thin mat…

and slept peacefully in minutes.

The rich man was surprised.

He thought: “I have everything… but no peace…….He has so little… but he is peaceful.”

That night, he understood a big truth: Comfort did not make him strong. It made him dependent.

We all say, “I want a simple life.” But in reality:

• If AC stops, we get angry

• If food is not tasty, we complain

• If travel is not comfortable, we feel restless

Slowly… we get so used to comfort…..that we cannot live without it…….Then comfort becomes a chain.

Think about Rama. He was a prince. He had all luxuries. But when he had to go to the forest for 14 years:

He removed his royal dress.

He wore simple clothes.

He left everything… and walked away.

No anger.

No complaint.

No fear.

Even in the forest… his mind was peaceful.

Why?

Because his happiness was inside him. Not in comfort.

The Insight

Comfort is not wrong. But this is important:

• We should be able to live without it also.

• If we depend too much on comfort, we become weak.

If comfort goes… peace also goes. But if we learn to adjust…our mind becomes strong.

Useful tip

Try this sometimes:

• Eat simple food without complaining

• Travel without expecting luxury

• Sit quietly without phone or TV

Slowly… your mind becomes stronger…..And you will feel a deep peace…that does not depend on anything.

Thought for the Day

Comfort is good. But real strength is this: Being peaceful… even without it.

That is true simplicity.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 156 | Epic Series - 39 | What if something goes wrong? | 
A message from a reader:

“Hi, I need clarity…I get sudden episodes where

• Heart starts racing

• Breathing becomes tight

• Fear comes in

There is no trigger outside And in that moment, one question comes…Is something wrong with my body… or is this my mind?

And my biggest fear now is What if it happens again… and I lose control? what should I actually DO in that moment?”

This question from this reader is intense and powerful…Because it is not asking for explanation…It is asking for action in the moment of fear.

Let’s go to a moment from the Mahabharata

On the battlefield…

A warrior stood ready…But suddenly…His body reacted…

Hands trembled…Breath changed…Mind became unstable…That warrior was Arjuna

And what was his real fear?

Not the battle… But this: “I may lose control…”

The Insight

The panic is not the main problem…The fear of “losing control” is what amplifies it. The sensation begins….But the moment the mind says: “This is dangerous… I can’t handle this…”……That is when the system escalates….So the goal is not to stop panic…It is to not add fear to it

What to DO?

1. Pause- Don’t react immediately

2. Ground- Feel:

• Feet touching the floor

• Hands

• Breath

3. Say inside : “This is uncomfortable… but not dangerous”

4. Stay - Give it 60–90 seconds

Don’t run….Don’t fight…Just stay

Another Example

You are sitting in office…Suddenly…Heart starts racing…First thought comes: “Something is wrong…”

Now instead of reacting…You do this: You sit back slightly…Feel your feet pressing the ground…You notice your breath…

Fast… but okay…

You tell yourself quietly: “This feels intense… but I can stay with this”…..You don’t get up immediately…You don’t rush for water…You just stay…

For the next 60 seconds…The heartbeat is still fast….Mind tries again: “What if it gets worse…”

You gently respond: “Let me just observe for a moment more”…Something subtle happens…

The intensity peaks…And then…Very slightly…It starts reducing…Not suddenly gone….But no longer out of control…

And in that moment…You realise something powerful: “I did not lose control… even when it felt like I would”

The Shift

You are NOT controlling panic . You are removing fear from panic …..And that changes everything

In the epic…

Krishna did not remove Arjuna’s situation…He removed his inner confusion….And clarity restored his strength

Thought for the Day

Sometimes…We are not afraid of what is happening…

We are afraid of: “What if I cannot handle this…”

And that fear…Creates the real intensity

Maybe…The first step is not to control…But reminding yourself: “ I can stay with this… even if it is uncomfortable”

Because…The moment you stop fearing the wave…It starts losing its power

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️

@@ 154 | Epic Series - 37 | Know Yourself… Re-Discover Yourself | 
A few days back, I was listening to a guest speaker in my class. One line he said really touched me:

“People don’t become less with age… they just start thinking they are less.”

That thought stayed with me. He gave a simple example. Something we see in many homes.

A mother spends 25–30 years raising her children. Every small need… school… sickness… she is always there. Her whole life is around her children.

Then one day… things change.

Children grow up. They become busy. They don’t need her the same way. From being needed every minute…To being asked sometimes… To being told… “Now you take rest.”

Then grandchildren come. Again, she gives love and care.

But inside… a small thought starts growing:

• “Now I am just helping…”

• “My main role is over…”

• “I should not interfere…”

Slowly… quietly…She starts feeling small inside.

The same happens with a father.

He works for many years. He builds a home. He takes care of the family. Then one day… he retires.

People say: “Now relax… your work is done.”

But inside… a question comes: “If I am not working… who am I?”. They are not tired of life. They are tired of feeling not needed.

This also happened to Arjuna in the Mahabharata.

Arjuna was strong. He had won many battles. But on the battlefield… he became confused and weak. Not because he lost strength…

But because he forgot who he was.

At that time, Krishna did not give him power. He reminded him: “You have not become less… you have just forgotten yourself.”

And everything changed.

The speaker then said something very simple and powerful:

• In the first half of life, we live for others.

• In the second half, we must find ourselves again.

Look at our life:

1. We start with dreams

2. Then we take roles like parent, worker, caretaker

3. Slowly, we get so busy… we forget ourselves

4. Then we think… “I am not important now”

This is the biggest mistake.

Because…

• Your role can change

• Your work can reduce

• But your value never reduces

See small examples:

• A grandmother may think: “I am just helping”…..But she teaches values to children

• A retired father may think: “I am not earning”……But his experience can guide the family

Nothing about them is less. Only their thinking has become less.

Just like Arjuna:

Same person.

Same strength.

Same life.

Only one thing changed: He remembered who he was.

So today’s message is simple:

• Don’t step back from life

• Don’t think your role is over

• Don’t make yourself small

This stage of life is not the end. It is a new beginning.

Now you are here to guide… help… and lift others.

One truth to remember:

• If you think you are “extra”… people will treat you like that

• If you stand strong… people will feel your value

Thought for the Day

Age does not make you less…It helps you see your real strength.

The day you stop feeling small and stand strong…You become a light for others.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

Next…”I should have stopped…”

Guilt…

(And also hoping she doesn’t ask in the morning… “So…how was your midnight inspection?” 😄)

Next day…Decision…”From today… I will control…”

Day goes well…Busy… occupied…

Evening…A small stress…A small irritation…

Night…Same pattern…

If I pause here…

This is not about food…

Because hunger was not there…

Then what was it?

If I look honestly…Before opening the fridge…

There was something else…

A feeling….Hard to name…

• Slight emptiness

• Boredom

• Tired mind

• Maybe a small stress from the day

Nothing big…But something uncomfortable…

And in that moment…Food gave me something immediate…

Not just taste…

A small escape… distraction… comfort…

That’s why…It repeats…

Not because I don’t have control…

But because…It is solving something… temporarily…

A small reflection came later…

After eating…There is relief…But very short…

Then comes guilt….

And that guilt again becomes discomfort…

Which again needs escape…

Cycle continues…

(Somewhere this reminded me of what I had read in the Mahabharata…even powerful warriors knew what was right…yet felt an inner pull they could not explain…It was never about strength…It was about awareness in that moment…)

Today… one small shift I tried…

At night…Same urge came…To open the fridge…

This time…I paused for a few seconds…

And asked myself…”What am I actually feeling right now?”

Not “what to eat”…

But “what is happening inside”…

There was no clear answer immediately…

I just sat there…Did nothing for a minute…

Urge was still there…

But slightly less…

Then instead of eating immediately…

I had a glass of water…

Walked a bit…

Some days it worked…

Some days it didn’t…

But one thing became clear…

It was not hunger…

It was something else…

Looking for relief…

Thought for the Day

Sometimes…We don’t eat because we are hungry…

We eat because…

something inside wants to feel better… quickly

And maybe…Change does not start with control…

It starts with gently asking…

”What am I really needing right now?”

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 152 | Epic Series -35 | Why Are You Always On The Phone? | 
A scene from a reader’s home:

Night time…Dinner is over…..Father is sitting with the news…Mother is clearing the kitchen…

Teenage son is in his room…Door half closed…Phone in hand…Headphones on…

Mother walks in…”Come out for some time… always inside only”……..No response…

“Are you listening?!”

“I am coming…” (without looking up)

Mother comes out…Slight irritation…”He doesn’t even respond properly these days…”

Father adds…”Whole day phone… what will happen to him…”

After some time… son comes out…Sits for a minute…Phone still in hand…Scrolling…

Father suddenly says…”Can you keep that phone aside for 10 minutes?!”….Tone firm…

Son reacts…”Why are you both always behind me?! I am just using my phone!”

“Just using? Whole day this only!”

Conversation becomes louder…Then sharper…Then ends with…”Leave it… you won’t understand…”

Door closes again…

Silence in the hall…But inside…Both parents are disturbed…

Later that night…Mother says softly…”I am really worried… he is changing…” Father replies…”I also feel the same… but he doesn’t listen at all…”

If we pause here…And look closely…This is not anger…This is something deeper…

A quiet fear inside:

• “What is happening to my child…”

• “Are we losing him…”

• “Is he going in the wrong direction…”

This is anxiety…But it doesn’t come out as anxiety…It comes out as:

• Questions

• Instructions

• Irritation

• Control

From the child’s side…It feels like:

• “They don’t trust me…”

• “They are always finding fault…”

• “They don’t understand my world…”

Same house…Same love…Different experiences…

This is becoming very common now…Especially in homes with teenage children…Parents are not angry…They are anxious….Children are not careless…They are in a different world

The problem is not phone…Not behaviour…Not even communication…

The real issue is this silent belief: “If I don’t control this now… something will go wrong…”

And because of that…Every small thing becomes a trigger…Screen time… friends… studies… tone…

Everything feels like a signal of danger…

A reflection from the Mahabharata…

Dhritarashtra…A father who deeply cared for his son…But his fear… attachment… and anxiety…Did not allow him to step back and see clearly…He kept reacting…Trying to manage… control… protect…

In our homes…It is much smaller…But the feeling is similar…

Today… a small shift helped…

Instead of asking…”Why are you always on the phone?”

Trying something else…

Sitting next to him…And saying…”What are you watching… show me…”

No interrogation…No judgement…Just entering his world… for a few minutes…

At first… he was surprised….Then he showed…

Spoke a little…Not much…But something opened…

Because…

Connection reduces anxiety…Control increases distance…

• Sometimes…What comes out as anger…Is actually fear…

• What comes out as control….Is actually worry…

Thought for the day

With growing children…We don’t need to hold them tighter…

We need to stay closer…without making them feel controlled

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 151 | Epic Series - 34 | That Small Pause… I Missed That Day… | 
Let me share something I observed in myself a few months ago…

After a long day at office…Deadlines… discussions… constant movement… and the usual traffic…

By the time I reached home… I could clearly feel… the body was tired… and the mind was not at its best…

I had just sat down… trying to unwind a bit…

At that moment… a family member asked me…”Can you just check this document once? It’s a bit urgent…”

It was a simple request…

But in that moment…I noticed something…Instead of hearing it as a request…The mind interpreted it differently…Almost like… one more demand…

And before I could even see it clearly…The response came…

“Right now also? I just came home… can it not wait for some time?!”

Voice slightly raised… tone not pleasant…Not something I would normally like to express…

In that very moment…I didn’t feel anything unusual…It felt justified…

But a little later…When things became quiet…I could see it more clearly…The reaction… was not really about that one request…

It was coming from:

• Tiredness

• A sense of being stretched

• And an inner need for some space

That night…There was a simple reflection inside…

“If I had just paused for a few seconds… would the response have been different?”…Most likely… yes…

Maybe I would have said…“Give me 10 minutes… I’ll look at it…”

Same situation…Different energy…

Next day…

I casually mentioned…“I think I reacted a bit… I could have handled it better…”

And it was received lightly…But the learning stayed…

This small incident reminded me of a moment from the Mahabharata

There was a great warrior…Ashwatthama…Son of

Dronacharya…A person of great capability… and deep attachment to his father…

During the Kurukshetra war…Through a painful strategy… his father was made to believe that his son was no more…That shock broke him… and he lost his life soon after…

When Ashwatthama came to know this…One can only imagine the state of his mind…Pain… anger… hurt… all at once…

That night…

He did not pause…He did not get the space to step back…In that state… he entered the enemy camp…People were asleep…And he acted… without clarity…

Later…When the mind settled…Awareness returned…But by then…Nothing could be undone…

What did I understand from this?

The incident at home…And the incident in the epic…Are very different in scale…But somewhere… they point to the same truth…

It is not anger alone that creates damage…It is the absence of that small pause…

In our daily life…We may not face extreme situations…But we do face many small triggers:

• A simple request

• A tone we misinterpret

• A moment when we are already tired

And in those moments…The mind reacts faster than awareness…

A small reminder I am keeping for myself

When I sense:

• Slight irritation

• Tightness in response

• Urge to reply immediately

Just pause…Even for a few seconds…

A pointer from the Bhagavad Gita

From anger… comes confusion…From confusion… clarity is lost…And then… action goes off track…

Thought for the Day

I may not always control the first reaction…But I can learn to notice it… a little earlier…

Because…

A small pause in the right moment…can prevent a long regret later…

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 150 | Epic Series -33 | How much is enough? | 
One reader asked me: “I am working… earning… saving decently . Still… I am not peaceful…

If I slow down… I feel scared…

If I run more… I feel tired…

Whatever I earn… feels less…Others are doing better….So I feel… I should also do more…

Tell me… what is right?”

This is not one person’s problem…This is many people’s life today, based on what I see …

To be clear ….

Money is needed. Security is needed. You are not wrong. But slowly… something changes inside…

Work becomes pressure… Life becomes comparison…And peace quietly goes away…

See this carefully…

• You earn for safety… but feel fear…

• You save for peace… but feel tension…

Why?

Because of one habit: Looking at others… and measuring our life.

You buy something… feel happy…Then you see someone with something better…Suddenly… your happiness goes…Nothing changed outside…Only your mind changed…

In the Mahabharata…

Duryodhana had everything…Still… he was not happy…Because he kept comparing with others…That one habit destroyed his peace… and his life.

The truth…

Problem is not less money…Problem is… “nothing feels enough”.

So what to do?

1. Ask one simple question : Before doing anything big…

“Do I really need this?”

or

“Am I doing this because others are doing?”

2. Don’t lose what is important

Money is needed…But not at the cost of: Health…Peace…Family…

If these are going… something is wrong.

3. Decide your ‘enough’

At some point… tell yourself: “This is enough for my life.” Even if others do more… you don’t have to run.

4. When mind says ‘I should have done more’

Don’t worry…Just ask: “If I did more… what would I lose?”

Sometimes… peace is more valuable than money.

Useful tip

When you feel “not enough”…

Pause…

Look at your life: Food….House…Family…

Tell yourself: “I have enough for today.”

Thought for the Day

Peace does not come when you have more…Peace comes when you feel…what you have is enough.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 149 | Epic Series - 32 | Am I living… or just waiting? | 
One of the readers wrote to me… not casually… but from somewhere very deep:

“Children are settled abroad… I feel proud saying that to everyone. But when I come back home… it is very quiet.

Earlier… this house used to be full of noise, laughter, small fights… Now… even the clock sound feels loud.

I finish dinner early… without knowing why. Then I just sit… keeping the phone near me.

Whole week… somehow goes like this…waiting for that one weekend call.

When they call… I feel alive again. After the call… silence becomes heavier than before.

Tell me honestly… is this how life slowly becomes?”

When I read this…

I didn’t feel like advising. I just paused. Because this is not a “problem”… This is a life transition… nobody prepares us for.

What you need to reflect…

Nothing is wrong with you. In fact… something is very right.

• You have loved deeply

• You still have love in abundance.

Only one thing has happened…

That love has no daily place to go…so it has become waiting.

A moment from the Ramayana

When Rama left for the forest…Mother Kausalya didn’t just miss him. Every corner would remind her… Every routine would break…

It was not just absence…It was sudden emptiness after fullness.

But observe carefully…She didn’t run behind that emptiness. She didn’t sit waiting for life to return.

She stood where she was…

and continued to live with dignity.

What we slowly forget…Without realizing… we shift from:

Living our life

to

waiting inside our life

We start feeling: “My children are my life.” But truth quietly stands like this: They are your love… but not your entire life.

A small shift… that changes everything

1. Don’t reduce your world to one ringtone. Life becomes heavy… when it becomes small. Just step out… even without mood…talk to one person… sit where people are there… Slowly… life starts moving again.

2. Don’t keep life on hold till weekend….Why should only Saturday feel alive? Even a simple day can have life: Morning sunlight…a small routine…doing something with your own hands… Movement brings back meaning.

3. Let your love move… not sit

Right now… your love is sitting quietly inside. Just let it go out a little…A smile…a small conversation…a kind word…

You will feel the shift immediately.

4. Hold them in your heart… not in your expectation. They are not away from love… only away by distance.

Let your love become lighter: “I am happy you are doing well.”

That one line… frees both sides.

Just tell yourself…

Sit quietly… no effort… no force…And just say: My life is not over…it has just changed…and I will learn to live this phase too.”

Finally…

Life doesn’t become heavy because people go far…

It becomes heavy when we stop moving within.

Just a small shift…

From waiting… to living.

Thought for the day

Don’t slowly turn your life into a waiting room…

Let it remain a living space… where your presence itself brings warmth.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 148 | Epic Series - 31 | Acceptance… is not weakness… It is freedom… | 
Acceptance… is not weakness… It is freedom…

Today is Sri Rama Navami…

A sacred day… Let us not just celebrate…Let us see one truth…..that can change our life…

A message came from a reader…

“For years… my husband has been a good man in many ways… responsible… caring in his own way… but emotionally… we never really connected…

There was no sharing… no deep conversations… only transactional …many things I handled on my own…

I tried to express… I tried to open up… sometimes I felt heard… many times I felt alone…

Now… I have stopped expecting… I am quiet… I am just living… thinking… maybe this is how life is meant to be…”

When I read this message from the reader …I did not see acceptance…I saw a heart that has become tired of trying…

Let me show you a moment…Not a story… A real-life moment…

It is night… Dinner is over…

She has finished cooking… cleaning… everything…

He is sitting with his phone…

Scrolling… relaxing after a long day…

She slowly comes and sits near him…Not to complain… Not to argue…Just hoping… maybe today…He may say…

“Did you eat?”

“Are you tired?”

“Come… sit… let’s talk…”

She waits…

1 minute…

5 minutes…

10 minutes…

Nothing happens…Not because he is a bad person…But because… this is how he is…

She gets up quietly…Goes inside… lies down… turns to the other side…No tears outside…But inside… something breaks… again… silently…

Next day…

She behaves normal….Because… life has to go on…

Tell me honestly…

Is this acceptance?

Or…

Is this silent suffering?

Many people are living like this…Not fighting… not expressing…Just slowly becoming quiet inside…

Now listen carefully…

Acceptance is not…”I will adjust and keep quiet…”

Acceptance is: “I see clearly… this person may express love differently… may not meet all my emotional needs…”

And then…”I stop waiting for it in the same way…”

The moment you stop waiting…That silent pain… starts reducing…That heaviness… starts lifting…

Now learn from Rama…

When he was sent to the forest…He did not live like a victim…He did not wake up every day thinking…”Why me… why my life…”

He accepted…

But he also lived fully…With strength…With clarity…With dignity…Rama lost the kingdom…But he did not lose himself…

That is true Acceptance…

Today… ask yourself…

• Where am I becoming silent inside?

• Where am I still waiting in pain?

• What am I expecting that may not come the way I want?

And gently tell yourself…

“I release the waiting…I will take care of my life… my peace…”

This is not selfish…This is not blaming anyone…This is maturity…This is self-respect…

Simple Truth

• Fighting exhausts you

• Silence hides the pain

• Waiting keeps the wound open

• Acceptance… heals you…

Thought for the Day

Don’t just adjust and become silent…Understand… accept… and live with strength…

Like Rama…Even without a kingdom…He lived like a King…

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 147 | Epic Series - 30 | After us… what will happen? | 
One reader wrote…

“We have lived with values. My husband may not be very ritualistic…but he is spiritual… involved in satsangs. Together… we have done pitru karma sincerely…maybe not perfectly… but with devotion…

Our children are well settled…But they do not believe in these things…We tried… but it did not work…I don’t think they will continue these rituals…

What will happen to us after our time? Will it affect our journey?”

This is a very touchy topic…

I will share this with my humble personal understanding…I may not be answering this strictly from a ritualistic angle…but more from the spiritual essence of our scriptures…

Different people may have different views on this…

Across teachings of the Bhagavad Gita and reflections from texts like the Garuda Purana… one principle stands strong:

A person’s journey after life is primarily shaped by:

• One’s own karma (actions)

• One’s inner state (bhava)

• The dharma one has lived

This is the core foundation

Then what about pitru karma?

Yes… it has an important place… It is:

• A way of expressing gratitude

• A way of remembering lineage

• A subtle support offered with faith

But it is supportive… not the only deciding factor, in my humble opinion.

A very grounded way to see this

Think like this:

• Your life → the main foundation

• Rituals done by others → additional support

If foundation is strong…

support helps… but absence does not collapse everything

Your situation… seen clearly

• You have lived with sincerity

• You have performed what you could… with devotion

• Your husband is spiritually inclined… engaged in satsang

This combination itself has deep value. ……Perfection in ritual is not as important as

sincerity in living

A reflective moment from the Mahabharata…

Bhishma… at the end of his life…His concern was not: “Will everything be done after me perfectly?”

His strength came from:

• The life of dharma he lived

• The clarity of his mind

• The awareness he held

This shows…Inner state carries great weigh

Now the real fear in your heart

“What if my children don’t continue… will it affect me?”

Let me give my personal opinion;

1. Tradition values continuity… yes…

2. Rituals are beneficial… yes…

But…

Your journey is not helplessly dependent on another person’s belief…..Otherwise, it would become unfair…

A person who lived sincerely…

cannot be left unsupported…

just because someone else chose differently

Now… what is in your hands?

1. Continue what you are doing… without anxiety

Whatever pitru karma you are doing…Do it with:

• Calmness

• Faith

• Without worrying about “perfect or not”

Sincerity matters more than perfection

2. Strengthen your inner path

Your husband’s satsang… your devotion…

This is not small…

• Nama japa

• Simple prayer

• Good thoughts

These build your inner strength

3. Share… but don’t impose

If you wish… gently explain to children:

• Meaning… not fear

• Value… not compulsion

After that… leave it…Belief cannot be forced…

4. Reduce fear-based thinking

Repeatedly thinking:

“What will happen to me later?”

Will disturb your present…Instead say:

“I have done sincerely… I will continue sincerely…”

One honest truth.. Even today… in many families:

• Some follow rituals fully

• Some partially

• Some not at all

Still… life does not become unjust…Because the system rests on: karma… dharma… and a larger divine order

Finally..

You are not leaving your journey in someone else’s hands…Your sincerity….your devotion…your way of living…are your strongest support

Thought for the Day

Do what is right… with sincerity…

After that…do not live in fear of what others may or may not do

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 146 |  | A clarification to all the readers (TFD-146) | 
Many of you asked me a simple question after yesterday’s story (TFD-145):

• “If my heart is breaking, what can I do?”

• “How do I change my tears into kindness?”

These are not small questions. They come from deep inside the heart. This kind of pain feels very heavy… like a big stone sitting inside us.

• When we lose someone we love.

• When we see our loved ones crying.

• When something bad happens and we feel afraid.

Even a small incident can shake us. This pain feels so strong because it comes from love.

But remember this simple truth:

• Pain is love when it feels sad.

• Kindness is love when it becomes bigger.

When you are hurt, you can choose between two roads:

1. The First Road

We keep thinking again and again:

• “Why did this happen to me?”

• “This is not fair!”

• “I don’t want this!”

Slowly, the heart becomes tight… like a knot. We feel tired, irritated, and heavy.

2. The Second Road

We pause for a moment. Take a deep breath. And gently ask:

• “Am I the only one feeling like this?”

• “Or are others also feeling this pain?”

Then slowly you begin to see:

• Many people are hurting like you.

• Many are crying silently.

• Many are carrying the same heavy feeling.

Now something changes…

The pain is not only “mine” anymore. It is shared by many. This one small thought brings a big shift.

Now try one small step. Very simple:

In your heart, say:

“I wish everyone who feels this pain becomes peaceful and happy.”

That’s all. Nothing big to do. Just feel it truly.

Slowly…

That heavy stone starts becoming soft… like a pillow.

You may still cry… but inside, there will be a little calmness.

That is how kindness begins.

In many homes:

• A mother is sad, and the daughter-in-law is also sad.

• A wife is hurt, and the husband is also hurt.

• A son is angry, and the father is also angry.

Everyone says, “I am right!”

But no one sees the other person’s pain.

If just one person pauses and thinks,

“Maybe they are hurting just like me…”

That one thought can change the whole home.

Fights reduce. Understanding grows. Love comes back.

One Simple Truth

• If we hide our pain, it becomes heavy.

• If we accept our pain, it becomes lighter.

• If we feel for others, it becomes kindness.

A Little Secret for You

Next time your heart feels heavy:

• Sit quietly.

• Close your eyes.

• Take a slow, deep breath.

• Say inside: “Let everyone who feels this pain be okay.”

Do it slowly… feel it.

You will feel lighter… like a bird.

Thought for the Day

• A good heart feels pain.

• A better heart understands pain.

• A wise heart turns pain into healing.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

P.S.: If you have any questions, feel free to message me directly!

Contact: Janakiraman 9845204620

@@ 145 | Epic Series - 29 | When the heart cannot bear the pain… | 
Yesterday… something small happened…But it touched me deeply…

I was coming back to Bangalore from Tamilnadu … heavy traffic… vehicles rushing… nobody slowing down…

In the middle of the road… one small dog…It was scared… confused…It wanted to cross….But didn’t know when to go…It stood there… looking here… looking there…

Like a small child stuck in the middle of a crowd…Suddenly… out of fear… it ran…A fast vehicle hit it badly…

That cry…It was not just a sound…It was pain… fear… helplessness… all together….The dog slowly dragged itself to the side…

But at that moment…

Something inside me also got hurt…

I felt angry…

I felt helpless…

I felt very heavy inside…

Tears came… I could not stop…Even now… that scene is not leaving my mind…

Same thing… different hearts

Many people saw that…But not everyone felt like this…

Why? Because…Pain is outside…But feeling is inside…If heart is soft…Even a stranger’s pain feels like our own…

Have you seen this?

Some people can watch anything… and just move on…

Some people… even a small thing… they cannot take it…

That is not weakness…That is a loving heart…

But then… how to handle this?

In the Mahabharata…

After the war… everything was finished…They got the kingdom…But Yudhishthira was not happy…Everywhere he saw only sadness…People lost their loved ones…Families were broken…He could not bear it…

At that time… Bhishma told him: “You are feeling this because your heart is alive…But if you carry every pain inside… you cannot live…Turn this pain into compassion…”

That is the key

Pain can do two things:

• Close our heart

• Or open our heart

If we keep thinking about the same scene again and again…We suffer more…But if we turn that feeling into kindness…Heart becomes lighter…We become more human…

One example….

In many homes…Mother is crying…Daughter-in-law is upset…Both are hurt…

But nobody is understanding each other…If one person just pauses… and thinks: “Let me understand their pain also…”

That moment… things start changing…

Same way…

When we see pain outside…Instead of only feeling hurt…If we add a little kindness…It heals us also…

Useful tip

Next time something disturbs you…Don’t ignore it…

Sit quietly for 2 minutes…

Close your eyes…

• Take slow breath

• In your mind say: “Let that being be free from pain…”

• Just feel it from your heart

Slowly… mind will calm down…

Finally…

We cannot stop all suffering…But we can choose…Will it make us heavy…Or will it make us better…

Thought for the Day

A kind heart feels pain…

A wise heart turns that pain into compassion…

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 144 | Epic Series – 28 | The unseen power within us… | 
One reader asked a very simple question…

“Why do I feel happy some days and suddenly very low on other days, even when nothing has changed outside?”

Let us see something very real… very close to our life

Evening time…

A little girl runs to her mother and says, “Amma… see my drawing!”. Mother is busy… but still looks at it .

If she smiles and says, “Super… very nice!”The child’s face lights up. She jumps… runs… shows it to everyone…

Same house… same drawing…

But if the mother says, “What is this… not neat”, Immediately…The child becomes quiet…She folds the paper… walks away slowly…Nothing outside changed…

Only a few words…But something inside changed

One more moment… very common

Morning time…

You wake up…One small thought comes, “Today meeting… what if it goes wrong…”. That one thought sits in the mind…

While brushing… it is there

While eating… it is there

While travelling… it is there

Whole day…Good things also happen…But mind is not enjoying

Why?

What is happening inside us?

Inside every one of us… there is a silent space. In psychology, it is called “Subconscious Mind”. We cannot see it. But it is always working. Like a small recorder…

It records:

• What we think

• What we speak

• What we feel

Slowly… quietly…It starts shaping our life

A truth we miss !!

When we keep thinking one thing again and again…Mind starts moving in that direction

• We notice related things

• We meet people connected to it

• We take small actions… without knowing

Slowly…

Life starts becoming like our thoughts. This is called Visualization. This is NOT superstition

Even modern psychology says: Mind focuses on what we repeat ; Brain filters everything else ; We act based on that focus

So…What we think daily… becomes our path

In olden days, elders used to warn…”Don’t speak negative… Devas will say asthu (so be it)…” It was not fear…It was deep understanding

Because…

Whatever we repeat with feeling…Our mind accepts as truth

And slowly…Life moves in that direction

If we keep saying:

• “I will fail…”

• “Something bad will happen…”

Mind starts believing it; Fear grows; Confidence goes down…We take wrong steps. And unknowingly…We move towards what we fear

A moment from Ramayana

In the Ramayana…When Hanuman stood near the ocean…He looked at the vast water…And for a moment, He thought, “I cannot do this…”

At that time…Jambavan came near him…And gently reminded: “You are strong… you are capable… you have forgotten your power…”

That one reminder Changed everything. Hanuman did not get new strength. He just remembered what was already inside. And then…He crossed the ocean

The insight

Mind does not know What is real… what is imagined. It believes, What we repeat with feeling.

So…

• Positive thought → builds strength

• Negative thought → builds fear

Watch gently…Ask yourself, “What am I thinking again and again every day?”

Because…That is slowly becoming your life

Useful tip

Every day… just 2 minutes, Close your eyes…

See yourself:

• Calm

• Healthy

• Peaceful

Don’t think it is future; Feel like it is already there. Slowly…Mind will accept it

Finally…

Life does not suddenly change one day…It changes slowly…In the silent space inside us. What we plant there…Will surely grow

Thought for the Day

Be careful with your thoughts…

Because…

Your mind quietly says “asthu”

to whatever you keep repeating inside

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏
