@@ 143 | Epic Series - 27 | Why is it so hard to let go? | 
One day, during an upanyasam… a very touching story was shared…

There was an old lady… very simple… very devoted…

Every evening… without fail… she would go to the temple… just before closing time. She would stand in front of Perumal… eyes filled with tears…

And say: “Varadha… when will you give me moksham…I am waiting… please take me…” This went on… day after day…

The priest (Bhattar)… poor man… could not close the temple until she left…Every day he would get delayed…

One day… he thought… “Let me try something…” He quietly went behind the deity…changed his voice… and said:

“My dear devotee… I am very happy with your bhakti…

Tomorrow… I will give you moksham…”

What happened next…

For a few seconds… the lady just stood still…Her heart started beating fast…”Did God really speak to me…?” Her eyes became big…

Then suddenly… she folded her hands and said: “Varadha… just one small request…Let me see my great granddaughter’s child… After that… I am ready…”

A silent truth

Every day she was saying… “I want moksham…” But when it really came close…She was not ready

What is really happening?

This is not just her story… this is our daily life

We keep saying:

“I want peace…”

“I want a tension-free life…”

“I want to be happy…”

But inside…

We are holding tightly…

• To people

• To our role

• To expectations

• To “just one more moment”

And slowly… without even knowing… We start holding on to money also…Not because we need more…

But because…”Just a little more… then I will feel safe…”

Even when life is going well, Mind will not relax. It keeps counting, comparing, holding…

A moment from the Mahabharata

After losing everything… Dhritarashtra left the palace and went to the forest…He had lost his sons… his power… his kingdom…

Still… letting go was not easy. Slowly… with time… he had to learn detachment

The Insight

• Attachment is not love

• Attachment is dependence

Love gives freedom. Attachment brings fear

• Fear of losing

• Fear of change

• Fear of letting go

Even with money, The tighter we hold, The more fear quietly grows

The impact

Because of attachment:

• We think too much

• We worry for small things

• We get hurt easily

• We hold on… even when it hurts

Mind never feels light

What is the right way?

Not to run away from life…But to learn one simple thing: “To live fully… but not hold tightly”

• Love people… but don’t lose yourself

• Earn money… but don’t become its slave

• Enjoy life… but don’t cling to it

To reflect……

Ask yourself softly: “If this is taken away… will I break?”

If the answer is yes…That is attachment

Useful tip

Every day… tell your mind: “This is given to me… not owned by me…”

Slowly… something changes inside…

A small space… a small lightness…

Finally….

That lady asked for moksham…

But when it came near…

She asked for “one more…”

That “one more”… never ends

Thought for the Day

Let us not wait for life to force us to let go…Let us slowly, gently, loosen our grip…

Because…

Peace does not come by holding more. It comes, by needing less

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 142 | Epic Series – 26 | Did we forget to say Thank You? | 
One reader shared something very honest… “I realized that I keep expecting from everyone ; But I rarely feel or express gratitude… Even for small things… I simply take it for granted.”

A Simple Real-Life Moment

One morning…

Coffee comes to your hand.

Breakfast is ready.

Clothes are washed.

House is clean.

Everything is working smoothly…But did we even pause and think: “Someone did this for me…”

Or did we just say:

“Less sugar…”

“Too much salt…”

“This is not hot…”

So many small moments… we miss

Every day… life gives us chances to feel gratitude…

But we don’t even notice…

• Someone holds the door for us

• A driver drops us safely

• A child smiles at us

• Wife/husband silently takes care of things

• Parents wait for our call

• A friend listens patiently

• Electricity comes on time

• Water flows when we open the tap

• Our body wakes up healthy

These look “normal”…But they are not small

An old father was sitting quietly. Son was busy… always rushing.

One day, father said softly: “I don’t need anything…Just sometimes… speak to me with love…”

Son was shocked.He had given money… comfort…everything…

But he had not given gratitude… not given attention

A reflection from the Ramayana

When Rama returned to Ayodhya after 14 years…He did not say: “I suffered so much…”

Instead…

He thanked everyone who helped him:

• Hanuman for his devotion

• His brothers for their love

• Even the smallest help was remembered

That is greatness…Not power… not victory… But gratitude in the heart

The Insight

Gratitude is not something we show only when:

• Someone dies

• Someone does a big help

Real gratitude is in daily small moments

What happens when we don’t have gratitude

• We feel “nothing is enough”

• We keep complaining

• We lose joy in simple things

• Relationships become dry

Life becomes heavy… even if everything is there

What happens when gratitude grows

• Small things start giving happiness

• People feel valued around us

• Mind becomes peaceful

• Relationships become warm

Life becomes light… even with less

Useful tip

Every day… just pause for a moment… Ask: “What are 3 things I received today?”

And say silently: “Thank you…”

A small mirror for all of us

Next time when food comes, Before finding fault… pause…

Next time when someone helps…Before ignoring… pause…

Can we just say…”Thank you…”

Finally….

We don’t remember people for what they gave…We remember them for how they made us feel.

Gratitude makes people feel seen… respected… loved

Thought for the Day

Let us not wait for a big moment… Let us learn to say “Thank you”… in small moments.

Because…

Gratitude is not just a word… It is the highest habit of a beautiful heart.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 141 | Epic Series – 25 | Why do we keep finding fault? | 
One reader shared a small pain…

“There are some people… wherever they go… they keep complaining”.

• In traffic ,they shout.

• In temple ,they criticize.

• In hotel ,they find fault in food.

• At home ,they keep pointing mistakes.

They always say: “People are careless… no discipline… no honesty… no manners…”

Being with them… slowly becomes very heavy.”

Example A. Some men that we see..

Ramesh went to a temple with his uncle. The temple bell was ringing… people were praying… peaceful atmosphere.

But uncle started…

• “See how people are standing… no order!”

• “Look at that man… no respect!”

• “Why is this so crowded… no management!”

Ramesh quietly stood there. After coming out… he felt tired. Not because of the crowd…

Not because of the heat…But because of the constant negativity.

Example B. Some women we see…

Lakshmi was sitting with her relatives at a small family function. Everything was going on nicely…

But slowly…

• “This sambar has no taste…”

• “See how she has dressed… no sense…”

• “That girl is always on phone…”

“No proper upbringing nowadays…”

Even when something was good…Some small fault was picked. Others smiled… but inside…they felt uncomfortable.

One young girl sitting there became very silent.Not because anyone scolded her…

But because the atmosphere became judging and heavy.

Example C. What we see on road….

You are driving peacefully; Suddenly someone cuts acros; One bike jumps signal; Another person honks continuously…

Immediately, the mind starts: - @

• “These people don’t know driving!”

• “No discipline at all!”

• “India will never change!”

Anger builds… words come out…Even after reaching home…that irritation continues.

The incident lasted 10 seconds…But the disturbance stayed for hours

What is really happening?

Such people think…

• “I am just speaking the truth.”

• “I am correcting others.”

But slowly… something else happens.

• Mind gets trained to find fault in everything

• Joy quietly disappears

• Even good things are not seen

And without knowing…

• This habit spreads… to children… to family…

• The whole environment becomes tense

A moment to reflect from Mahabharata

During forest life, many sages visited the Pandavas. One day, a sage asked Yudhishthira: “What is the biggest weakness of human beings?”

Yudhishthira replied: “People easily see the mistakes of others… But they do not see their own.”

The Insight

Constant fault-finding is not awareness; It is a sign of inner disturbance

A peaceful mind:

• Sees mistakes… but stays calm

• Speaks when needed… but with kindness

• Knows when to leave things

The silent impact….

When we keep complaining:

• Children learn to judge

• Family loses warmth

• Even happy moments become heavy

People may not argue…But slowly… they start moving away emotionally

How to deal with such people

1. Do not absorb everything; Listen… but don’t carry it inside

2. Gently shift the energy ; Bring a lighter or positive topic

3. Protect your peace; Reduce exposure if needed

4. Look within. Ask yourself: “Am I also doing this unknowingly?”

Useful tip

Next time in traffic… or at home… or anywhere…Before reacting, pause for one second…

Ask: “Is my peace more important… or correcting everyone?”

Finally…

1. A mind that keeps searching for faults…Will never enjoy what is good.

2. A mind that is peaceful…Will find beauty everywhere.

Thought for the Day

Let us not make our presence heavy… Let people feel peaceful when we are around.

Because…

Real maturity is not in pointing mistakes…It is in spreading calmness.

@@ 140 | Epic series - 24 | What is Real Happiness? | 
One reader asked… a simple but powerful question: “What is real happiness?”

Reflecting from Mahabharata..

After losing everything in the dice game, Yudhishthira and his brothers were sent to the forest. No palace… no comfort… no security. They were living under trees.

One day, Draupadi asked in pain: “Earlier, we had everything. Now we have nothing. How are you still peaceful?”

Yudhishthira calmly said, “Did we lose everything?. We are still together…We can still eat… still talk…My mind is still steady…I have not lost my values…

If my mind is peaceful…

how can I say I lost happiness?”

Look at our life…

A student thinks, “If I get good marks… I will be happy.” After marks come… new tension begins.

An employee thinks, “If I get promotion… I will be happy.” After promotion… more pressure… less peace.

A parent thinks, “ If my children settle… I will relax” After that… worry about their future never ends.

A person buys a new house…

for a few days, happiness…

then EMI tension… fear… comparison.

The Truth we miss

In every stage we say: “Next step… then I will be happy…”

But happiness never comes.

Why?

Because we are searching outside…while the problem is inside.

The Painful Truth

We are not unhappy because life is hard…We are unhappy because our mind is never at rest.

Even with everything… we worry. Even with comfort… we feel empty.

The Real Insight

Happiness is not getting what we want…It is being peaceful with what we have.

Finally….

1. When the mind is peaceful, even a small life feels big.

2. When the mind is restless, even a big life feels empty.

Thought for the day

Let us stop running behind happiness…Let us learn to be still inside.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 139 | Epic Series-23 | பணமா? பாசமா?” - Money or Relationship? | 
In almost every family… this moment comes. Not as a big drama. But as a silent question… inside the heart.

A Story We All Know…

Ravi was working in another country. Good job. Good salary. After many struggles, life was finally becoming stable.

Back home, his father started falling sick. At first, it was small. Then slowly… it became serious.

Phone calls increased.

“Appa is not eating properly…”

“He is getting weaker…”

“Doctors are not very hopeful…”

Every call made Ravi restless. He opened the flight booking page many times. But he would close it again.

His mind started speaking:

“If I go now… what about my job?”

“What if I lose this opportunity?”

“What if this situation continues for months?”

Days passed. Weeks passed. One day, a call came. “Come if you can…”

That sentence had no urgency. No pressure. But it had something else.

A quiet helplessness.

Ravi went. When he entered the room, his father slowly opened his eyes.

He did not ask about money.

He did not ask about success. He just held Ravi’s hand… tightly.

And said only one sentence: “I was waiting for you…”

That was all. No complaints. No anger. Just waiting.

The Truth That Hits Hard

In that moment, Ravi understood something deeply: His father was not waiting for money. Not waiting for success. He was waiting… for him.

A Moment from the Epics

In the Mahabharata, when Yudhishthira was asked: “What is the greatest wonder in life?”

He said: “We see people leaving this world every day. Yet we live as if our loved ones will stay forever.”

The Real Insight

Life does not always ask us to choose:

Money OR Relationship.

That is too simple. The real question life asks is: “In your busy life… did you forget to show your love?”

The Silent Mistake that Many of us do is by thinking :

“I will go later…”

“I will spend time after things settle…”

“I will take care once life becomes stable…”

But life does not wait for our “perfect timing.” Love delayed too much, slowly becomes regret.

A Balanced Truth

No one is saying:Leave everything. Destroy your career. That is not wisdom.

But disappearing emotionally…

that is also not right. Sometimes, what matters most is small:

A visit.

A few days.

A heartfelt conversation.

A hand held at the right time.

For us, it may look small. For them… it is everything.

Thought for the Day

At the end of life…

Parents will not remember

how much money we earned.

They will remember: “Were you there… when I needed you?”

Because in the final moments…

People don’t ask, “How successful is my child?”

They feel, “Did my child make me feel important?”

Money builds life. But presence… gives it meaning.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 138 | Epic Series - 22 | What Is Karma Really? | 
Many people use the word karma. But very few people really understand it.

Some think karma means only pitru karma , the rituals we do for our ancestors. Some think karma means something bad that comes back to punish us.

But in simple truth, karma just means action. Every thought, every word, and every action we do is karma. And every karma quietly creates a result. Just like a seed becomes a tree.

A Story from the Mahabharata

One day, the great teacher Dronacharya wanted to test his students. He placed a wooden bird on a tree and asked all the princes to shoot its eye with an arrow. Many students came forward.

Dronacharya asked the first student, What do you see?”The student said, “I see the tree, the branches, the bird, and the sky.” “Step aside,” said the teacher.

Another student came. “What do you see?” The student replied, “I see the bird and the tree.” The teacher said ,”Step aside.”

Finally Arjuna came forward. Dronacharya asked him the same question. “What do you see?” Arjuna said quietly, “I see only the eye of the bird.”

The teacher asked , “Do you see the tree?” . Arjuna said , “No.” The teacher asked “Do you see the sky?” . Arjuna said ,” No, I see only the eye.” Dronacharya said, “shoot”. The arrow flew and hit the bird’s eye perfectly.

The Insight

Dronacharya then told the students something very important. “Success does not come by accident. It comes from focused action.”

Arjuna did not become great because of luck. He became great because of the karma he practiced every day. His effort, his discipline, and his focus created his future.

Understanding Karma

Karma is not a punishment system. Karma is simply the law of cause and effect.

If you plant a mango seed, you cannot expect a coconut tree.

In the same way: Kind actions create peace. Angry actions create conflict. Hard work creates growth. Life quietly reflects the actions we repeat every day.

A Simple Way to Remember

Yesterday’s karma created today. Today’s karma is quietly creating tomorrow.

Try This Today

Before you speak… pause for a moment. Before you react… think for a moment. Ask yourself one small question: “Is this action creating peace… or creating trouble?”. That one moment of awareness can change many things in life.

Thought for the Day

Karma is not something mysterious.It is simply the result of the small actions we repeat every day. Choose your actions carefully. Because one day, they quietly become your destiny.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 137 | Epic Series - 21 | The Invisible Wall Called Ego | 
There is something in human life that is very difficult to notice. It quietly shapes many of our reactions and relationships. It is what we often call ego.

The interesting thing about ego is this: it is easier for us to notice it in others than to recognize it within ourselves. That is simply how the human mind works.

Ego does not shout loudly. It does not announce its presence. It quietly appears in small thoughts inside the mind.

Thoughts like:

“Perhaps I am right.”

“Why should I say sorry first?”

“Why didn’t they understand me?”

“Why should I be the one to listen?”

These thoughts seem very normal. All of us experience them at some point. But when they stay for too long, they slowly begin to influence how we behave.

Sometimes this feeling appears as pride. Sometimes it appears as hurt. Sometimes it quietly shows itself as silence, stubbornness, or distance.

And slowly, without anyone intending it, conversations stop. Friends drift apart. Families become distant.

All because the mind finds it difficult to bend just a little.

A story from Mahabharata

After the great Kurukshetra War, the mighty warrior Bhima still carried anger towards the old king Dhritarashtra.

Dhritarashtra had supported his son Duryodhana, and that support had led to the destruction of the kingdom.

After the war, the Pandavas came to meet the old king with respect. But inside Dhritarashtra’s heart, pain and anger were still burning. He asked Bhima to come forward so he could embrace him.

But deep inside, the king’s anger had not fully left him. The wise Krishna sensed the danger. Quietly, he placed an iron statue of Bhima before the blind king.

Dhritarashtra embraced it with tremendous force. The statue shattered into pieces. In that moment, a painful realization dawned on him. His anger had taken control of him

With tears in his eyes he said softly: “My pride and attachment have already destroyed my sons and my kingdom. Even now, they refuse to leave me.”

At that moment, the old king saw clearly what he had not seen before.

The Insight

Ego rarely damages life in one dramatic moment. Instead, it works slowly.

It stops us from apologizing.

It stops us from listening.

It stops us from learning from one another.

Gradually, it builds an invisible wall between people. And often, we carry that wall without even realizing it.

Useful tip

Whenever a disagreement arises, pause for a moment and quietly ask yourself:

“What matters more right now ; proving a point, or preserving the relationship?”

That small pause can soften many situations. When the mind relaxes, relationships begin to heal.

Thought for the Day

Ego may make us feel important for a moment.

But humility quietly builds respect, love, and lasting relationships.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 136 | Epic Series - 20 | When “Enough” Still Feels Like “Not Enough | 
In many homes around us, life actually looks quite comfortable.

The house is good. There is money in the bank. Children are studying well. From the outside, everything seems fine.

And yet… sometimes inside the home, the air feels a little tight.

• A father hesitates before switching on one more light.

• A mother quietly puts back something she wanted to buy for herself.

• A child slowly drops a small wish because the moment does not feel right.

It is not because the family is poor. Often, there is already enough.

But somewhere inside the mind, a small voice whispers:

“What if tomorrow becomes difficult?”

“What if money is not enough later?”

So the heart begins to hold money very tightly. And when we hold money tightly… sometimes we also hold worry tightly.

This is not greed. Most of the time it comes from love ; the wish to protect the family and keep everyone safe. But slowly the mind begins to feel scarcity, even when life is already abundant.

A story from the Mahabharata

After the great war, the noble king Yudhishthira organised a grand charity. Thousands of people were fed. Clothes and wealth were given to many.

Everyone praised the king. “Such generosity!”. At that moment, a small mongoose walked into the gathering. Half of its body was shining like gold.

It rolled on the ground and then said something surprising. “This charity is wonderful… but it is still not equal to something I once saw.” Everyone was curious.

The mongoose began to tell a simple story . Once there lived a very poor family. One day they had only a little flour just enough to cook one small meal.

Just as they were about to eat, a hungry guest arrived at their door.

The father gave his portion. The guest was still hungry. The mother gave her portion. Still hungry. The son gave his portion. Still hungry. Finally, the daughter-in-law quietly gave the last piece of food. That day, the whole family slept hungry.

But their hearts were peaceful… because they had fed someone who needed food more than them.

The mongoose then said, “A few grains of that food fell on the ground. When I rolled on them, half of my body turned into gold.”

“Since then I have travelled everywhere hoping to see a greater act of giving… so that the rest of my body may also turn to gold. But I have never seen anything equal to that.”

The entire gathering became silent.

The Insight

That poor family had almost nothing. Yet their hearts felt full.

Today many people have much more. Yet somewhere inside… it still feels like something is not enough.

So the real question is not: “How much money do we have?” The real question is: “How free is our heart?”

Thought for the Day

True wealth is not in how much we hold. True wealth is in the freedom to share… without fear.

When the heart feels abundant, even a small act of giving becomes golden.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 135 | Epic Series -19 | When Prayer Does Not Bring Peace | 
Many people around us love God very much.

• They wake up early in the morning.

• They light the lamp.

• They do pooja.

• They visit temples.

• They listen to beautiful spiritual talks.

• They write Rama Nama or Krishna Nama.

• They chant shlokas every day.

All these are wonderful things.

But sometimes something puzzling happens. Even after doing all this…The mind is still not quiet.

• There is worry.

• There is fear.

• Small things disturb us.

• Thoughts keep running here and there.

Then a small question comes inside the heart. “If I am praying so much… why is my mind still not peaceful?”

The reason is simple. Sometimes our hands are praying… but our mind is somewhere else.

While doing pooja, the mind may be thinking about office work. While chanting God’s name, the mind may be worrying about tomorrow.

So the prayer goes on… but the heart is not really there. It becomes like a machine repeating words.

A reflection inspired by the Ramayana

Once, a group of wise sages were sitting together and discussing devotion. They knew many scriptures and many rituals. Just then, Hanuman came there. The sages asked him kindly, “Hanuman, you always look peaceful and happy. What is your secret?”

Hanuman smiled. Then he said something very simple. “When I take the name of Rama, I think only of Rama. My mind, my heart, and even my breath are with Him in that moment.”

Then he said softly, “If the mind is running everywhere, even a thousand prayers can feel empty. But if the mind is truly present, even one prayer can fill the heart with peace.”

The Insight

Prayer is not about how much we do. It is about how deeply we do it. One minute of prayer with a full heart can give more peace than one hour of distracted chanting.

God does not count how many rituals we perform. God simply feels the love and sincerity in our heart.

Useful tip

Next time you sit for prayer, try something very small. Before starting, pause for a moment. Take one slow, gentle breath. Tell your mind softly, “For the next few minutes, I am only here.” Then chant the name of God slowly. Listen to the sound yourself. Let your mind stay with that one prayer.

Even if it is just five minutes, let those five minutes be complete. Slowly, very slowly… the mind will become calm.

Thought for the Day

Prayer is not about doing many rituals. It is about bringing the whole mind and the whole heart into one small moment with God.

Where attention goes…peace quietly follows.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 134 | Epic Series - 18 | The Ghost in Your Head | 
Have you ever sat down to eat a nice meal… but you could not enjoy the food? Because you were still angry about something someone said yesterday.

Have you ever looked at your child… but you did not really see their smile? Because your mind was worrying about money for next month.

This is how many of us live. Our body is here, but our mind is somewhere else.

• We cry about things that are already over.

• We worry about things that have not even happened.

It is like carrying a bag of rotten garbage everywhere we go. Then we complain that life smells bad. But we forget that we are the ones carrying the bag.

Because we keep living in the past or the future, we forget to live in the present moment.

A Story from the Mahabharata

After the great war of Kurukshetra, King Yudhishthira finally got the kingdom. He had the crown. He had the palace. He had all the wealth. But inside his heart, he was deeply sad.

Thousands of people had died in the war. Even many of his own relatives were gone. Day and night he kept thinking,

• “This war happened because of me.”

• ”So many people died because of me.”

He stayed in his room and cried. Finally he went to meet his grandfather Bhishma, who was lying on a bed of arrows. Yudhishthira fell at his feet and cried like a small child.

Bhishma looked at him with great kindness and said something very simple. “My son, the fire has already burnt the forest. The trees are gone. The smoke is gone. Why are you still blowing on the cold ashes?”

Then Bhishma told him a hard truth. “If you keep crying for those who are gone, you will forget the people who are still alive. The people outside need a king. But you are too busy talking to the ghosts of the past.”

Bhishma then said: “Time is like a river. It only flows forward. Once the water passes your feet, you cannot bring it back.”

The Insight

Yudhishthira slowly understood something very deep. The past was already finished. But he was punishing himself again and again by remembering it.

Because of that, he was forgetting the people who needed him right now. He realized something important.

The past is like a graveyard. If you keep living there, nothing new can grow.

So he wiped his tears, walked out of his room, and began serving his people.

He could not change the past, but he could take care of the present.

The Lesson for Us

Many of us live in the same way. We keep holding old anger. We keep thinking “What if?”

But remember one thing. You cannot receive a new gift if your hands are already full of old trash.

If your mind is stuck in something that happened last year, you may miss the person who is speaking to you right now. Slowly, minute by minute, we throw away our own life.

Try This Today

When your mind starts travelling to yesterday or tomorrow, pause for a moment. Take a slow breath. Touch your arm. Look at the person near you and really see them. Come back to this moment.

Thought for the Day

The past is gone. The future is not here yet. This moment is the only place where life truly exists. Do not live like a ghost. Be here.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 133 | Epic Series - 17 | The Powerful Difference Between an Emotion and a Feeling | 
Let me begin with something very practical that can change how we handle many daily struggles.

Most people use the words emotion and feeling as if they are the same. But they are actually different.

An emotion is the first sudden wave that rises inside us.

Anger.

Fear.

Jealousy.

Hurt.

It comes automatically. We do not choose it.

A feeling, however, is what grows when the mind starts thinking about that emotion and building a story around it.

When the mind keeps replaying the event…

When it keeps analysing what happened…

When it keeps telling a story around the emotion…

That is when the emotion slowly turns into a feeling that stays much longer.

Understanding this small difference can save us from many daily problems.

The Bhagavad Gita beautifully hints at this psychological truth. Let us go to the battlefield of Kurukshetra.

Standing in the chariot was the great warrior Arjuna. When he first saw his relatives, teachers, and friends standing on the opposite side, a powerful emotion rose inside him.

Grief.

His hands trembled. His heart softened. This was natural. Even the wisest person can feel such emotions.

But then something else happened.

Arjuna’s mind began to build thoughts around that emotion.

“If I fight, families will be destroyed.”

“If families collapse, society will decay.”

“If society decays, sin will spread everywhere.”

The mind started constructing a long chain of thoughts.

What began as a momentary emotion of grief slowly turned into a heavy feeling of helplessness. The warrior who had faced countless battles now felt unable to act.

He finally dropped his bow and said, “I cannot fight.”

At that moment Krishna did not tell Arjuna, “Do not feel sad.”

Instead, Krishna helped him examine the thoughts that were feeding that heavy feeling.

Through the wisdom of the Gita, Krishna gently separated the original emotion from the many conclusions Arjuna’s mind had built around it.

Once Arjuna saw the situation clearly, the heavy feeling lifted. The emotion had passed. Clarity returned.

Now let us pause here.

This small insight can transform many situations in our daily life.

Someone criticises us. First, an emotion arises - hurt. That is natural. But then the mind starts adding stories.

“They don’t respect me.”

“They always treat me like this.”

“No one values me.”

Soon the original emotion becomes a heavy feeling that stays for hours or even days. But if we pause and recognise: “This is just an emotion passing through me.”

Then the mind does not build a long emotional story around it. The wave rises… and the wave falls. Peace returns much faster.

Useful tip

When something unpleasant happens, pause and ask yourself:

“Is this just an emotion passing through… or is my mind building a long story around it?”

Stopping the story often dissolves the suffering.

Essence

Emotions arise automatically.

Feelings become heavier when the mind keeps feeding them with stories. Wisdom lies in noticing the difference.

Thought for the Day

Today, when an emotion arises, I will watch carefully whether my mind is quietly turning it into a long-lasting feeling.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 132 | Epic series - 16 | The Moment When the Kingdom Was Taken Away | 
Many times in life, everything seems perfect.

Your plans are ready.

Your future looks bright.

Everyone around you is celebrating.

And suddenly… in one moment… everything changes.

Let me share a powerful moment from the life of Rama in the great Ramayana.

The whole city of Ayodhya was glowing with happiness. Streets were decorated with flowers. Music filled the air. People were celebrating with joy. Because the next morning, Rama was going to be crowned king.

Everyone loved him. He was kind, wise, and gentle with every person. People believed that when Rama became king, the kingdom would become like heaven. That night the palace was full of excitement.

But in another corner of the palace, something very different was unfolding. Queen Kaikeyi reminded King Dasharatha about two promises he had once made to her.

Then she asked for something shocking. “Send Rama to the forest for fourteen years… and make my son Bharata the king.”

Dasharatha collapsed in grief. His heart was breaking. But a promise made by a king could not be broken.

The next morning, Rama was called. The king could hardly speak. Tears filled his eyes. Finally the painful truth was told.

“You cannot become king today. You must go to the forest for fourteen years.”

Just a few hours earlier the whole kingdom was preparing to crown him. Now he had to walk away from everything.

No palace.

No throne.

No power.

Only the forest.

What do you think Rama did?

Did he protest? Did he say, “This is unfair?”

No.

Rama folded his hands, smiled gently, and said something very powerful.

“If this is my father’s word, then this is my path.”

No anger.

No bitterness.

Only quiet strength.

Within a few hours he removed his royal clothes and began walking towards the forest. The entire city wept.

But Rama’s mind remained calm.

Insight

In life, plans change suddenly.

A promotion disappears.

A relationship breaks.

A dream collapses.

We feel life has cheated us.

But the true strength of a person is not seen when life goes as planned. It is seen when life suddenly changes direction.

Most people break when life changes their path. Great people walk the new path with dignity.

Rama did not lose his greatness when he lost the kingdom. In fact, the world remembers him because of how he handled that loss.

Sometimes life removes something from our hands… but quietly gives us something deeper.

Character.

Inner strength.

Grace.

Useful Tip

Today, if something small does not go your way…

A plan fails.

Someone disappoints you.

Something changes suddenly.

Pause for a moment.

Take a slow breath and ask yourself:

“Can I handle this with the calm strength of Rama?”

You may discover a quiet power inside you.

Essence

Life will not always follow our plans. But dignity in difficult moments makes a human being truly great.

Thought for the Day

Today, if life changes my plans, I will not lose my balance. I will walk forward with calm strength.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 131 | Inside Us Series-27 | The Message That Changed a Father | 
Many parents say this about their teenage children.

“They have too much attitude.”

“They get angry for small things.”

“They don’t talk properly anymore.”

So we scold them. We correct them. We lecture them.

But very often…

We see their behaviour. We don’t see their pain.

One evening a father came home after a long day. As soon as he entered the house, he saw his 16-year-old son sitting on the sofa, staring at his phone. School bag on the floor. Shoes still on.

The father immediately became angry. “Why are you sitting like this? Did you finish your homework?” . No reply. The boy kept looking at his phone.

The father’s voice became louder. “All day phone! No responsibility! This is why your marks are dropping!”.

The boy suddenly stood up. “Appa, please stop!”. And he walked quickly into his room. Big Bang !! The door closed loudly.

The father became even more upset. “See this behaviour! Teenagers these days have no respect!”. An hour passed. Dinner was ready, but the boy did not come out.

The father went near the room and knocked. No answer. He slowly opened the door. His son was sitting on the bed… quietly crying. The father was shocked. He had never seen his son cry like that. Just then the phone on the bed lit up.

A message popped up from one of his classmates. The father’s eyes fell on the message. It said: “Don’t come to school tomorrow. Everyone is sharing that video. They are all making fun of you.”

The father’s heart started beating fast. He slowly asked, “What video?”. The boy hesitated… and then showed his phone.

During a class presentation that morning, the boy had become nervous. His voice shook. Some students secretly recorded it.

Now the video was spreading in school groups… with laughing emojis. The boy spoke with tears in his eyes. “Everyone laughed today, Appa. I didn’t know where to look… I felt so small.”

He looked down. “And when I came home… I thought you will understand… but you were also angry with me.” The father felt something break inside him.

A few minutes earlier he had only seen a careless teenage boy with attitude. Now he saw a frightened young heart trying to survive humiliation. He slowly sat next to his son.

For a moment he said nothing. Then he gently put his arm around him. “I am sorry,” he whispered. “You didn’t need another person shouting at you today. You needed someone standing next to you.”

The boy leaned on his father’s shoulder and cried. That night the father learnt something very important. Teenagers may look strong outside.

But inside… their hearts are still very young. Sometimes their anger is not disrespect. Sometimes it is pain they don’t know how to explain.

What is Empathy?

Empathy simply means trying to feel what the other person may be feeling.

Not judging quickly.

Not reacting immediately.

Just pausing for a moment and asking ourselves, “If I were in their place… how would I feel right now?”

When a teenager slams the door…

When a spouse becomes silent…

When an elderly parent repeats the same question…

Instead of seeing only the behaviour, empathy helps us see the heart behind it.

Essence

Most conflicts in families happen because we react to what people do, without understanding what they are going through. Empathy turns reaction into understanding. And sometimes… that single moment of understanding can heal a heart.

Thought for the Day

Before correcting someone’s behaviour, try to understand their feeling. Because when people feel understood, their hearts open… and relationships become stronger.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 130 | Inside Us Series -26 | The Place You Dreamed Of… and the Quiet Restlessness After | 
Let me begin with something very simple.

Sometimes the place we dream about for years does not give the peace we thought it would. This is not because the place is bad.

It is because the mind that goes there is the same restless mind that lived here.

Let me tell you a small story.

For thirty years, Rajesh wanted to see Kashmir. Whenever he saw pictures of snow mountains on television, his eyes would light up like a little boy. He would say to his wife, “One day I will stand there…Then I will feel complete.”

Life kept moving.

Children’s education…..Loans.

…..Responsibilities.

The dream quietly waited.

Finally, after retirement, at the age of 63, he booked the trip.

He told everyone proudly, “At last… Kashmir!”. The first day felt magical. Snow everywhere. Cold air touching his face. Beautiful mountains. He took many photos. Sent them to the family WhatsApp group.

Everyone replied:

“Wow!”

“So beautiful!”

“Enjoy, Mama!”

His heart felt full.

But something strange happened on the third day. That morning he stood quietly near the lake. The mountains were still beautiful. Nothing had changed outside. But inside, something had changed.

A small thought came. “Okay… we have seen this.” Another thought followed. “What next?”

He looked at the mountains again.The excitement had quietly gone. His wife asked gently, “Are you happy?”

He smiled and said, “Yes, yes… very nice.” But inside there was a small emptiness.

The place he had dreamed about for thirty years had become normal.

On the flight back home, he was already telling his son, “Next year maybe we should go to Switzerland.”

This happens to many of us.

We think:

• “When I reach there… I will feel happy.”

• ”When I buy that… I will feel complete.”

• ”When I achieve this… life will settle.”

But the happiness of reaching

is very short.

Why?

Because the mind enjoys anticipation more than arrival.

Before the trip: Excitement. Planning. Dreams.

During the trip:

Photos. Sharing. Proof.

After the trip:

“Is this all?”

And then, The mind quietly starts searching for the next thing.

The insight

Many times we are not chasing a place. We are chasing a feeling.

The feeling of excitement.

The feeling of aliveness.

The feeling of escaping our routine life.

But when we reach the place,

the mountains change ; The mind does not. And if the mind is restless, even paradise will feel temporary.

Let me tell you another small moment.

One evening, after returning home, Rajesh sat on his balcony. His wife brought him a simple cup of tea. The sun was setting slowly. Children were playing in the street.

For the first time in many days,

he sat quietly without taking a photo. After a few minutes he said softly, “You know… this feels peaceful.” His wife smiled. She did not say anything. Because she understood something very deep.

Peace was not in Kashmir. Peace was in the quiet mind sitting on the balcony.

If we cannot sit peacefully in our own living room, no mountain in the world can permanently satisfy us. But if the mind learns to be still, even a small cup of evening tea can feel like heaven.

Useful tip

Today, sit quietly for ten minutes.

No phone.

No TV.

Just sit. Watch your breath. Listen to the small sounds around you. Let the moment be enough.

Slowly you will discover something beautiful. Contentment does not come from going somewhere. It comes from being fully where you already are.

Essence

Anticipation excites.

Arrival settles.

Restlessness reveals the wandering mind.

True peace begins when the mind says: “For this moment, this is enough.”

Thought for the Day

Today I will not rush to the next thing. I will sit gently inside this moment and discover the peace that was always here.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 129 | Inside Us Series - 25 | The Tiny Expectations That Quietly Change Our Day | 
Many of us believe our mood changes only because of big problems.

But if we observe carefully, most of the time our mood changes because of something very small… so small that we almost miss it.

Let me share three simple moments from everyday life.

Nothing dramatic. Just scenes that quietly happen in many homes. Yet inside these ordinary moments lies a powerful truth about our mind.

Situation – 1

One morning I woke up in a very pleasant mood.

The house was calm. Coffee was ready. The day looked beautiful.

The previous night I had sent an important message to someone. I expected a reply in the morning because it would help me take a decision.

So naturally, the first thing I did after waking up was check my phone.

No reply.

“Maybe it will come in a few minutes,” I told myself.

I refreshed again.

Still nothing.

The house was exactly the same. The coffee tasted the same. The morning air was still pleasant.

Yet slowly my mind began to tighten.

I became quieter. Slightly restless. Even small things began to irritate me.

After some time I realised something very interesting.

My mood had not changed because of the other person.

My mood had changed because my mind had already written a small story: “By morning, the reply will come.”

When life did not follow that story, disappointment quietly walked in.

Situation – 2

An elderly father was sitting in his living room one evening.

His son works in another city. Every Sunday evening the son usually calls him.

That Sunday the father finished dinner early and sat near the phone.

7:30…

8:00…

8:30…

No call.

The father told himself, “He must be busy.”

But slowly something inside him became heavy.

He did not complain. He did not show anger. But the evening felt unusually long.

The next day the son called and said, “Appa, yesterday was extremely hectic. I could not even touch my phone.”

The father smiled gently and said, “That’s okay.”

But the truth is… that small expectation had already coloured his entire evening.

Sometimes the heart waits for one small sign of love. When it does not arrive, the heart becomes quietly sad.

Situation – 3

A young mother had been very busy the whole week.

On Sunday she thought, “Today we should all go out for dinner together.”

She happily got the children ready. She dressed nicely. The whole plan was already alive in her mind.

When she told her husband, he looked at her gently and said, “Today I am extremely tired… can we go next week?”

He did not refuse with anger. He simply spoke honestly.

But suddenly the mother’s face fell. Her excitement disappeared. The children sensed it. The house became silent for a while.

Later she herself realised what had happened. Nothing terrible had happened.

Only one small thing…

The day had not gone the way she had imagined. And that was enough to disturb the heart.

The Deep Truth Behind These Moments

If we observe carefully, many of our daily emotional disturbances come from three very small places inside us.

1. Unfulfilled expectations

The message that did not arrive.

2. Missing or delayed communication

The phone call that never came.

3. Plans that did not unfold as imagined

The outing that did not happen.

None of these are life tragedies. Yet our mind quietly creates a picture of how things should happen.

And when life moves in a slightly different direction, the heart becomes unsettled.

A Quiet Insight

The world did not wake up with the intention of spoiling our day. People did not plan to hurt us. Life simply moved in its own rhythm.

But our mind had already written a small script… and reality followed another script.

The moment we see this gently, something soft happens inside us. The irritation melts. The mind becomes lighter.

And we realise something very beautiful: Peace was never outside. It was always inside… waiting for our understanding.

Thought for the Day

The next time your mood suddenly changes, pause for a moment and ask yourself softly:

“What small expectation inside me was quietly waiting… that life chose not to deliver today?”

Very often, that one honest question brings the lost smile back.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 128 | Inside Us Series - 24 | The Day Coffee Came Late | 
Every morning, Meenakshi’s coffee comes at 6:15.

Not 6:20.

Not 6:10.

Exactly 6:15.

The tumbler must be stainless steel. The newspaper must be folded neatly. Her chair must face the balcony. Only then… the morning feels complete.

Her family knows this very well. Her son often jokes, “Even the sun may be checking the time… waiting for Amma’s coffee before rising.”

Everyone laughs. Meenakshi also smiles. But inside, the routine means a lot to her. It gives a peaceful beginning to the day.

One Sunday morning, something unusual happened.

Her daughter-in-law overslept. Coffee came at 6:40. Just twenty-five minutes late. Nothing big.

But somehow… the whole morning felt different. Meenakshi did not shout. She did not complain loudly. She simply spoke less.

Her face became a little tight. The newspaper stayed on the table longer than usual. Slowly, the cheerful house became a little quiet.

After some time she said softly, “These days… nothing runs properly.”

But the real issue was not the coffee. Something deeper had been touched. It was the feeling that something had slipped out of her control.

What Is Really Happening?

Routine gives comfort. Especially as we grow older. Children become busy with their lives. Friends move away. Health becomes a little uncertain.

Life slowly changes around us. So we hold on to small daily routines.

Morning coffee.

Evening walk.

Same chair.

Same time.

These small habits quietly give us a feeling of stability. They whisper gently to the mind, “At least this small part of life is still in my control.”

So when the routine changes even a little, the mind feels disturbed. Not because of the coffee. But because something inside feels unsettled.

Small Signs We All Have

Sometimes we may notice this in ourselves.

Feeling irritated when guests come suddenly. Feeling uneasy when travel disturbs our routine. Wanting the house to be arranged in a very specific way. Feeling uncomfortable when a daily habit is missed.

It may look like discipline. But sometimes… it is simply our fear of change.

A Simple Truth

Controlling small things makes us feel we are controlling life. But life doesn’t work that way.

Life changes.

Plans change.

People change.

When we hold our routines too tightly, we feel uncomfortable when they break. But when we stay a little flexible, the mind stays peaceful.

One evening, suddenly the power went off.

No TV.

No lights.

No routine.

The grandchildren quickly lit candles. They all sat on the floor and started telling stories.

One child cracked a silly joke. Another child acted like a movie hero. Soon the whole house was laughing loudly. Even Meenakshi laughed… and laughed.

Probably more than she had laughed in many days. Later that night she quietly realised something. That evening had no routine at all.

Yet it brought so much joy. Peace had not come from routine. It had come from being relaxed and enjoying the moment.

A Gentle Reminder

Routines are good. They give rhythm to our day. But sometimes we must allow life to be a little different.

Once in a while,

Drink coffee at a different time. Let someone else arrange the house. Travel without planning everything.

And simply say, “It’s okay.”

Because real strength is not in controlling everything. Real strength is in accepting small changes peacefully.

Essence

Routine should help us live better. We should not become prisoners of our routine. Life does not need to be perfectly arranged to be beautiful.

Thought for the Day

Today, allow one small change in your routine. You may discover that when control relaxes, peace quietly enters the heart.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 127 | Inside Us series – 23 | When the Plate Is Full… but the Heart Is Not | 
Many people today live with numbers.

Sugar level.

Blood pressure.

Cholesterol.

The doctor says, “Be careful.”

Family says, “Control food.”

So they do the right thing. They avoid sweets. They skip second servings. They say “No, no… I should not eat this.”

Outside, it looks like discipline. But inside, something small sometimes happens.

A quiet voice whispers, “Others are enjoying… I cannot.”

They smile. They act normal.

But somewhere deep inside, a tiny feeling remains. “I am missing something.”

Let me tell you a small story.

Prithvi uncle loved laddus. Not ten laddus. Just one. But after his diabetes diagnosis, he stopped completely. For three years he did not touch a sweet.

At every wedding, he would say proudly, “No sweets for me. I am very strict.” Everyone appreciated his discipline.

But one day his niece asked him softly, “Uncle… do you miss it?”

He laughed loudly and said,

“No no! I have full control!”

But that night, when everyone slept, he opened the fridge. There was a small laddu kept for the child. He just looked at it. He did not eat it. He closed the fridge slowly.

It was not hunger. It was something else.

The next week he went for his regular check-up.

The doctor looked at his reports and said kindly, “Your sugar is stable. Very good control. Once in a while you can eat a small sweet. Slowly. With awareness.”

Prithvi uncle was surprised. “Doctor… really?”. The doctor smiled.

“If the mind feels deprived, one day it will rebel. Better to eat with awareness than to live with silent craving.” That evening something beautiful happened.

His niece brought a small laddu and placed it in his hand. He sat quietly. He broke it into two tiny pieces. He closed his eyes. He ate slowly.

For the first time in three years… he tasted the sweetness without guilt… without fear.

And then something surprising happened. He did not want another one. The craving disappeared.

The Gentle Insight

When we fight the mind, the mind fights back.

When we say, “I must never have this,” the mind keeps remembering it again and again. But when we say calmly,

“I can have it… but I choose wisely,” the mind relaxes.

Self-awareness is a beautiful gift. It means watching the mind gently. Not suppressing it. Not blindly obeying it. Just understanding it.

Acceptance changes everything.

“Yes… my body needs care.”

“Yes… I must be mindful.”

But life does not have to feel like punishment.

A peaceful mind digests even simple food better.

A tense mind suffers even with perfect diet.

The Beautiful Balance

Health is not only about control. It is also about calmness. Sometimes a small sweet eaten slowly with joy harms less than a life lived with silent frustration.

The real training is not of the tongue. It is of the mind.

When awareness grows, Craving becomes softer. Food becomes lighter. Discipline becomes peaceful.

And slowly we discover something wonderful. Joy does not come from eating more.

Joy comes from wanting less… peacefully.

Thought for the Day

When the mind feels free,

discipline stops feeling like sacrifice.

It simply becomes a gentle way of caring for ourselves.

And that is true health.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 126 | Inside us series - 22 | When Carefulness Wears a Mask | 
Saving is wisdom. Minimal living is beautiful. But sometimes, fear quietly hides behind the mask of “I am simple.”

Let me tell you a small story.

Nothing wrong. It showed discipline. But slowly, something else also grew.

If guests came unexpectedly, he felt tense. If children asked for a small outing, he calculated first. If his wife wanted to replace a 15-year-old mattress, he said, “We are not luxury people.”

Mama replied automatically, “We cannot help everyone.”

That night he could not sleep. He did not come from poverty. In fact, he had grown up in a comfortable home. Fees were paid on time. Food was always enough. There was no great struggle.

But there was one sentence he heard often: “Be careful. Money does not stay.”

He remembered how elders would switch off lights quickly. How they would say, “Why waste?” even when there was enough. How appreciation came more for saving than for sharing.

Somewhere inside, without pain… without drama… a silent belief had formed:

“Security comes only by holding tight.”

He had earned well. He had saved well. But he suddenly saw the truth.

There was no real danger around him. No crisis. No shortage.

Yet his heart behaved as if something might collapse any moment.

He was not living minimally. He was living guarded… even when there was nothing attacking him.

He was wearing the mask of “simple life,” but inside he was still picking pennies to feel safe.

Next morning something small changed.

He bought fruits ; not costly ones, just enough to share. He agreed to replace the old mattress. He told his grandson, “Let us give quietly. We have enough.”

Nothing dramatic happened. But the house felt lighter.

His wife’s back pain reduced. The watchman smiled widely. The grandson hugged him tightly.

And Ganesan mama felt something he had not felt in years. And that is “Ease”

The Gentle Truth

Minimalism from peace feels light. Minimalism from fear feels tight.

When we hold too tight:

• We postpone comfort again and again.

• We bargain even when we can afford not to.

• We hesitate to give, even small things.

We may not be poor. We may not have suffered.

But sometimes we are afraid of losing a safety that is already secure.

We think we are protecting the future. But sometimes we are imprisoning the present.

The Art of Giving

Giving does not mean spending wildly. It means saying:

“We are safe.”

“There is enough.”

“Let joy also live in this house.”

Start small -

A better pillow.

A shared sweet.

A tip without counting coins.

A quiet help to someone who cannot return it.

Giving is not about money. It is about relaxing the heart.

Thought for the Day

Ask gently: “Am I choosing simplicity from strength… or from fear?”

When the heart feels safe,

saving and giving walk together. And that is true wealth.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 125 | Inside Us Series - 21 | We Worked for Tomorrow… But Missed Each Other Today | 
Before we begin…

This is not about a wrong husband. This is not about a neglected wife. This is about two good people… who were so busy building life that they forgot to slowly live it.

The Long Journey

Raman and Mala began like most of us. Limited salary. Unlimited responsibilities. Children’s education. Parents’ health. Monthly EMIs. Social commitments. Their conversations were practical.

“Fees deadline is Monday.”

“Electricity bill is due.”

“Keep the medical reports ready.”

They were not distant. They were united in purpose.

Whenever Mala felt like sitting beside him and talking without agenda, Raman would say with affection,

Retirement came with garland, shawl, applause and photographs. At last, no alarm clock. No office pressure.

That evening, Mala prepared his favourite vegetable curry. She remembered how he used to praise it in their early years. She set the table with care.

But that evening there was a big match, Royal Challengers Bengaluru playing Chennai Super Kings. Raman was watching with childlike excitement.

“Wonderful shot!” he exclaimed.

Mala smiled. Served him. Sat opposite.

“Is it good?” she asked gently.

“Yes, yes… very nice,” he replied, eyes still following the ball.

And yet… something small inside her felt unseen.

Not because he did not care. But because he did not notice.

Not grand gestures. Not dramatic expressions.

Just this:

One evening where he would look at her fully and say,

“After all these years… how are you?”

For thirty years she had adjusted beautifully.

“After this phase…”

“After this pressure…”

“After retirement…”

But emotional needs, when postponed too long, do not disappear. They simply become quiet.

Many husbands are like Raman , sincere providers, dependable, steady.

Many wives are like Mala who are silent pillars, steady supporters, graceful adjusters.

No one is wrong.

But sometimes, when life has been about duty for decades, shifting to companionship needs conscious effort.

Security was built. Now connection must be rebuilt.

If you are like Mala:

Speak softly about your wish. Not as complaint, but as invitation.

“Now that we have time… shall we give some time to ourselves also?”

If you are like Raman:

Retirement is not just leaving office. It is returning home completely.

Five undistracted minutes daily. No phone. No television.

Just listening.

That is not romance in cinema language. That is companionship in its most dignified form.

The Real Insight

Love does not disappear in long marriages. It simply gets covered under responsibility.

And in senior years, love does not need fireworks. It needs attention.

A shared cup of coffee.

A short evening walk.

A hand resting gently over another.

Small gestures. Deep meaning.

Thought for the Day

Today, let me not assume love is understood. Let me express it. Let me ask for it gently. Let me offer it freely.

Because growing old together

is not about how many years we lived in the same house…

It is about how many moments

we truly saw each other.

@@ 124 | Inside Us Series - 20 | When the Wi-Fi Became the Family Member | 
Before We Begin…

This is not a story about phones. It is a story about a house where everyone is connected… but nobody is together.

By the end of this, you may look at your dining table differently.

One night, the power went off.

No Wi-Fi. No charging. No network. At first, irritation.

“Data is not working!”

“When will current come?”

Slowly, something else happened.

Silence.

Real silence.

They all came out of their rooms because it was too hot inside. They sat in the hall. No screens. No escape. For the first time in months, they looked at each other.

Sriram noticed his father’s tired eyes. Meera saw Paati’s shaking hands. Ramesh heard his children’s natural laughter not emoji laughter.

Paati began telling a story from her childhood. No background music. No editing. No filter. They listened. And something melted.

The Stunning Insight

Technology is not the villain. It gives speed. Information. Entertainment. Connection.

But connection is not the same as closeness. Screens give light to the face. But only people give warmth to the heart.

When every generation escapes into its own screen:

• Gen Z escapes into validation.

• Middle age escapes into work and distraction.

• Elders escape into spiritual videos and forwards.

Everyone is busy filling a small emptiness inside.But the more we fill it with noise…The more silent the home becomes.

A house becomes a home only when voices mix. When plates touch at one table. When someone says, “Wait… tell me more.”

No one will remember Reel number 3,482. No one will remember Conference Call 7,912. No one will remember Forward Message 2,006.

But one day a child will remember: “We always ate in separate rooms.”

Or

“We sat together… even if only for 20 minutes.”

A Small Change

One meal a day. No phones. No TV. No headphones. Just people. At first it will feel awkward. Like strangers sitting together.

But slowly… the house will breathe again.

Thought for the Day

Today, before I connect to Wi-Fi…I will connect to one person in my home.Because a strong signal is good. But a strong relationship is life.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 123 | Inside Us Series - 19 | When Being Informed Is Not the Same as Being Included | 
Before We Begin…

This is not a story about a “difficult” mother-in-law. This is a story about a woman who once led everything… And one day quietly felt like a guest in her own son’s life.

If you have ever smiled and said, “Anything is fine for me,”

while your heart whispered something else…this is for you.

Let us begin.

Meenakshi Amma is 68. Hariharan is 72.

Once upon a time, people waited outside their office rooms. They were respected professionals. They built wealth. They built homes. They built their children’s future.

Today their son and daughter are settled, successful, and happy.

Birthdays, they are invited. Festivals, they are called. Family decisions, they are informed. Everything is proper.

Yet… something feels empty.

One day the son calls.

“Amma, we’ve shifted Arjun to a new international school. Admissions done.”

“Oh… okay,” she says softly.

She remembers how she once compared ten schools before deciding for her own children.

Now she hears about her grandson’s future after it is finalized.

Another day. “Amma, we’re going to Europe next month.”

“Very nice…” she smiles.

When did they plan it? Why didn’t they even discuss? She doesn’t want control. She just wants connection. But she cannot say it.

Because she fears being called “old-fashioned.”

At home, new curtains are put up. “Nice choice,” she says. She remembers a time when nothing entered the house without her decision. No one insulted her. No one rejected her. But slowly she feels replaced. She is Respected…

but not consulted.She is Informed…but not involved. And that pain is very silent.

Meanwhile, the son feels stuck. If Amma is silent, he feels guilty. If his wife feels judged, he feels tense. He loves both. But he feels like a rope in a gentle tug-of-war.

No shouting. No open fight. Just a subtle territorial tension.

Mother thinks, “I built all this.”

Wife thinks, “This is my home now.”

Both are right. Both are insecure.

What Is Really Happening?

For 40 years, she was the decision-maker. Then retirement came. Children moved out. Without announcement, her role changed. But no one prepared her heart for that change.

When identity is tied to responsibility, losing responsibility feels like losing relevance. The real pain is not about school or curtains.

It is one question: “Do I still matter?”

The stunning Insight

There is only one throne in every family. Not money. Not kitchen. Not authority.

It is the throne of relevance. An aging mother fears becoming irrelevant. A young wife fears not being allowed to belong. Both are fighting for the same emotional need: “To feel important.”

When we see this clearly,

the war becomes unnecessary.

Gentle Counselling Guidance

For Mothers-in-law: Shift from authority to mentorship. Instead of asking, “Why didn’t you ask me?” Try saying, “If you ever need my experience, I’m always here.”

Create a new identity beyond decision-making. Your value does not reduce when your role changes.

For Daughters-in-law: Involving is not surrendering. One simple sentence can heal deeply: “Amma, what do you think?”. Even if you choose differently later, the act of asking gives dignity.

For Sons:

Do not avoid. Facilitate. Reassure your mother with love. Reassure your wife with clarity. Bridges are built by communication, not silence.

Essence

Elders do not need control. They need to feel that their presence still has weight. Young couples do not need interference. They need to feel trusted. When ego becomes empathy, home becomes peaceful again.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will not just inform.

I will involve. Because respect is formal. But inclusion is love.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 122 | Inside Us Series -18 | When You Stand in the Middle | 
Before We Begin…

This is not a story about generation gap. This is a story about a heart that is getting pulled from two sides. If you are in your late 30s or 40s… you may see yourself here.

Let us begin.

Arun is 43. At home, his father from the Duty Generation often says,

• “In our days, children never talked like this.”

• “Why is he answering back?”

• “Why is she coming home so late?”

There is worry in his voice. But it comes out as complaint.

On the other side, his daughter Ananya , pure Gen Z says,

• “Appa, please… this is normal.”

• “You don’t understand my world.”

• “Why is everyone controlling me?”

There is confidence in her voice. But it sounds like disrespect.

Arun stands in the middle.

If he supports his parents, his children feel hurt. If he supports his children, his parents feel ignored. Some nights he sits alone after everyone sleeps.

He feels tired. Not body tired. Heart tired.

“I handle office pressure. I pay bills. I manage everything,” he thinks…….”But why does this feel harder than all that?”

One evening something small happened.

His father complained again. Ananya answered sharply. Silence fell like a stone. For a moment, Arun looked carefully.

He did not see a stubborn old man. He saw a 72-year-old father scared of a world he does not understand anymore.

He did not see an arrogant teenager. He saw a young girl trying to find her own voice.

And suddenly… tears came to his eyes. He understood something powerful. His parents are not trying to control. They are trying to protect.

His children are not trying to insult. They are trying to grow.

And he… He is not stuck. He is the Bridge Generation.

A bridge carries weight from both sides. People walk over it every day. Nobody stops to thank it. But without the bridge, two lands can never meet.

That night Arun did not give a lecture. He sat next to his father and said softly, “Appa… tell me how you felt when I was 17.” His father’s eyes became moist.

Later he went to Ananya and said, “I may not fully understand your world. But I want to learn.”

Her voice became gentle. No big miracle happened. But something melted.

The Stunning Insight

Middle age is not about being squeezed. It is about being chosen.

Chosen to translate fear into understanding. Chosen to turn complaint into conversation.

Chosen to hold both hands , the wrinkled one and the restless one.

Yes, it is heavy. Yes, sometimes you will cry alone.

But one day you will see : Your parents felt respected ; Your children felt understood. And your family did not break.

Thought for the Day

If today you feel sandwiched between elders and Gen Z…Do not feel unlucky. You are not trapped. You are the bridge that keeps love flowing between yesterday and tomorrow. And that is a sacred role.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 121 | Inside Us Series - 17 | When the Serial Became More Important Than Dinner | 
Before We Begin…

This is not a story about television. This is a story about a small moment. A folded drawing. A child who waited. And a grandmother who said, “Later.”

By the end of this, you may not look at 7:30 pm the same way again.

Let us begin.

Every day at 7:25 pm something strange happens in the house. Plates move fast. The cooker is turned off quickly. Phone calls are stopped in the middle.

Because at 7:30 pm… the Sun TV serial begins.

Lakshmi Paati is 59. “It helps me relax,” she says. First it was only one episode. Then two. Then she started waiting from 6:30 itself.

If someone talks during the serial, she says, “Shhh…important scene!”. If someone changes the channel, her face becomes serious and angry.

She laughs when the heroine wins. She cries when the heroine cries. She gets angry at the villain. The people in the TV feel like real people to her.

One evening, her grandson Arjun came running. “Paati! See my drawing! I drew our house!”. He was very happy. His eyes were bright.

“Later… later… very important scene,” she said, without even turning to look at him.Arjun stood there. He waited.

The hero shouted. The villain cried. Loud music played. At last, the episode finished.

Lakshmi turned and said, “Now show me.” But Arjun had already gone to his room. The drawing was lying folded on the table.

Tomorrow the same scene will come again on TV. But that small moment with Arjun will never come again.

What Is Really Happening?

The serial is not bad. It gives: Story, Feelings, Drama, Excitement.

But real life is quiet.

In real life:There is no loud music. No big speeches. No shocking twists every 20 minutes.

Life is simple. Sometimes it feels too simple.

So the mind likes drama. It feels active and alive. But slowly… something changes.

The serial is not just for relaxation anymore. It becomes something we feel we must not miss. And when we feel we must not miss it,

we start missing something else.

The Real Problem

It is not just love for TV. It is emptiness inside the heart. When children are busy, Husband does not talk much, Days look the same again and again, The serial fills that empty space. It gives strong feelings without any real problem.

But those feelings are borrowed.They are not our real life. And borrowed feelings cannot hug you. They cannot sit next to you. They cannot love you back.

The Insight

When we watch too much drama, Peace starts feeling boring. Simple talking feels dull. Silence feels uncomfortable.

But real love grows in small simple moments.In listening to a child. Sitting quietly near your husband or wife. Laughing at a small joke. Watching the sky in the evening.

These moments look small now.But later in life, they become the biggest memories.

No one remembers Sun TV Episode 542. But a grandson will always remember: “Paati did not look at my drawing.” Or “Paati switched off the TV and sat with me.”

A Small Change

Two days in a week, switch off the TV 30 minutes early. Just 30 minutes. Sit together. Ask one simple question: “How was your day?”

At first it may feel little strange. But slowly, warmth will come back. Because screens give light. But people give warmth.And warmth is what the heart truly needs.

Essence

Relaxing is good. Enjoying is good. But when the TV becomes more important than the person sitting near us, we slowly move away

from our own life.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will watch. But I will not forget to look into the eyes of the people who love me.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 120 | Inside Us Series - 16 | When Health Becomes a Full-Time Job | 
Sridhar is 55. His day does not begin with a smile or a small prayer. It begins with numbers.

Fasting sugar.

Resting heart rate.

Steps target.

Calories allowed.

He stands on the weighing scale before he greets his wife. He checks his watch before he looks at the sky. Breakfast is not served. It is measured. Oil is counted in teaspoons. Almonds are counted one by one, not simply eaten.

If his smartwatch shows 7,842 steps at night, he walks inside the bedroom to make it 8,000. Even if everyone else is ready to sleep.

One evening, his wife made hot kesari for a festival. The house was full of sweet smell and happiness. He looked at the bowl. “How many calories?” he asked. “Just taste a little,” she said softly. He shook his head. That night, while his family laughed in the hall, he sat alone and searched on his phone: “What is the glycemic index of semolina?”

His small granddaughter sat on the floor and ate the sweet quietly. She looked at her grandfather once… then looked down. Everything in his life looked perfect. But nothing felt happy.

What Is Really Happening?

This is not just health care. This is fear wearing the dress of discipline. The body becomes a school project. Food becomes a math problem. Walking becomes punishment. Numbers become “good” and “bad.”Somewhere inside, there is a small hidden thought: “If I control everything, maybe I can stop aging.”…….,”If I am perfect, maybe I can escape death.” But life does not work like that.

The Inner Experience

• He feels proud when he is strict.

• He feels guilty if he eats one extra bite.

• He feels scared when one number goes up.

• He feels angry when others eat freely.

• Family members feel watched and judged.

• Meals lose their joy.

• Talks become only about sugar, cholesterol, and carbs.

• The body becomes strong. But the mind becomes tired.

Insight

Real health is not only about muscles and numbers. It is also about peace.When discipline becomes too tight, it stops being strength.It becomes fear.

Our body is not a robot.

It changes. It moves. It grows older.One sweet on a festival day will not destroy your health. One relaxed day will not erase years of care.

But daily stress… daily fear… daily tension…can slowly hurt the heart and mind.You cannot count cortisol like calories.But stress touches everything inside you.

A Gentle Shift

Ask yourself quietly: “Am I taking care of my body…or am I trying to fight death?”

There is a big difference. Healthy living keeps joy. Fearful living removes it.

Balance means:

• 80% discipline.

• 20% being human.

Because food is not only fuel.

It is love.

It is family.

It is memory.

Useful Tip

This week:

Eat one meal without counting.

Walk one day without tracking.

Have one sweet without guilt.

Then watch carefully. The sky will not fall. Your body will not break. In fact, your smile may return. And when the mind relaxes, the body also feels lighter.

Essence

• Awareness builds strength.

• Obsession builds tension.

• A long life without joy is not real health.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will take care of my body without hurting my peace.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 119 | Inside Us Series – 15 | The Unspoken Competition Between Siblings | 
At a wedding reception, two brothers stood next to each other for a photograph. Same parents. Same small bedroom growing up. Same school uniforms. But life had taken them in different directions.

One had built a big house with a grand entrance. One lived in a simple, peaceful home. One’s children were abroad. One’s children worked nearby and visited every weekend. They smiled. They hugged. They meant it. Then the relatives began.

“His son is in America.”

“His daughter just bought another apartment.”

“He is doing extremely well now.”

No one meant harm. It was casual talk. But something invisible moved inside both of them.

Later that night, one brother told his wife quietly, “They always highlight his side.” The other brother, in another house, told his wife, “No matter what I do, it’s never enough compared to him.” Both were accomplished. Both were respected. Yet both felt… slightly measured.

What Is Really Happening?

Sibling rivalry does not disappear. It grows up. In childhood, it is loud, fighting for toys. In adulthood, it becomes silent ,fighting for validation. Because a sibling is our first mirror. The first person we were compared to. The first person who made us ask, “Am I enough?”. That question sometimes never fully leaves. Even at 50. Even at 60.

The Hidden Signs

• Feeling uneasy when relatives praise the other.

• Casually listing your achievements without being asked.

• Changing topics when their success is discussed.

• Slowly creating emotional distance.

It looks normal.It sounds normal.But inside, an old scoreboard is still active.

Insight

The real competition is not between brothers. It is between self-worth and insecurity. When you truly feel enough,someone else’s success feels inspiring. When you secretly doubt yourself,

even love feels like comparison. The relative’s words are not the wound.The wound is older.

One day, their mother was in the ICU. Both brothers sat outside, quietly. No business titles. No property discussions. No children’s achievements. Just two boys waiting for their mother to open her eyes. In that corridor, something melted. They were not “successful brother” and “less successful brother.” They were two children of the same woman. Two hearts carrying the same childhood memories. Two shoulders sharing the same responsibility. And suddenly, the scoreboard looked meaningless. That silence taught more than any family function ever could.

A Gentle Shift

Instead of thinking, “They always highlight him,”

Ask, “Why does his success disturb my peace?”

Instead of proving yourself,

practice celebrating.When your sibling rises,it is not your fall. It is your family’s growth. There is enough space for both stars in the same sky.

Essence

• Childhood comparisons can quietly live for decades.

• Self-acceptance dissolves rivalry.

• Siblings are not competitors ,they are shared history.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will replace comparison with connection

especially within my own family.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 118 | Inside Us Series – 14 | When Retirement Changes Everything | 
Mr. and Mrs. Rao retired six months apart.Suddenly, they had too much time together. During working years, small disagreements were absorbed by busy schedules. Now, every small habit , the way he reads the newspaper, the way she arranges the kitchen becomes a source of irritation.

Their adult children notice the tension: “Why are you arguing over little things now?”. But neither feels they can explain ; they don’t want to seem petty, yet the discomfort is real.

What Is Really Happening?

Retirement often triggers relationship stress:

• The shift from structured work-life to full-time togetherness is intense.

• Expectations that the other should “fill the empty hours” create frustration.

• Adult children may feel pulled in as mediators.

Inside, there is also loss of identity and purpose. What used to define them like work, social circles, daily tasks is gone.

The Inner Experience

• Retired couple: “I love them, but small things irritate me endlessly.”

• Children: “We are trying to stay neutral, but it feels tense.”

• Grandparents: “We feel restless, but don’t know how to change it.”

No one is at fault. Everyone is struggling with change.

Insight

Conflict here is less about each other, more about life transitions. Awareness and gentle communication can prevent years of distance or resentment.

Useful tip

Set small “personal spaces” each day:

• 1 hour alone reading or walking

• A quiet hobby

• Independent social interaction

Then come together for shared activities intentionally, not by default.

Essence

• Retirement changes roles, not love

• Space restores patience and perspective

• Awareness prevents silent resentment

Thought for the Day

I will respect my needs and my partner’s needs in this new chapter of life.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏
