@@ 117 | Inside Us Series – 13 | When the Phone Becomes the Lifeline | 
Raju is 58. Children are settled abroad. Retirement is three years away. The house that once echoed with noise now closes by 9:30 p.m. Every evening at 8:00 p.m., he sits in the same chair. Waiting……Not for dinner…..Not for news…. But For a call !!

When the phone rings and his son’s name flashes, his face lights up like Deepavali lamps. “How are you, Appa?”. For 12 minutes, Raman feels alive. Relevant. Needed. After the call ends, the silence feels louder than before.

One day the call didn’t come. Time zones, busy schedule, meetings which are the normal reasons. But Raju’s mind whispered: “Maybe he doesn’t need me anymore.” He tried calling. No answer. He checked WhatsApp. “Last seen 7:42 pm.” His heart tightened. That night he could not sleep.

The next morning he told his wife angrily, “Children forget parents once they settle.” But what he was truly feeling was not anger. It was emptiness.

What Is Really Happening?

After 50, life quietly changes: Roles reduce. Responsibilities decrease. Identity shifts. Social circles shrink.

When purpose reduces, people unconsciously attach their entire emotional meaning to one relationship , often children, sometimes a friend, sometimes a spiritual mentor.

The relationship becomes the only source of validation. And slowly, dependence replaces connection.

The Inner Experience

• The parent feels: “If they don’t call, I don’t matter.”

• The spouse feels sidelined.

• The children feel pressured.

• The person feels invisible in their own home.

It is not love that hurts. It is when love becomes the only pillar holding your emotional roof.

The Stunning Truth

Loneliness in the second half of life is not about being alone. It is about losing relevance. So the mind clings. Because being needed feels like proof of existence. But here is the deeper awakening :

• Your worth does not retire when your job retires

• Your identity does not shrink when children grow. Your life does not end when your role changes.

• Attachment at 25 is passion. Attachment at 55 can become fear of fading.

A Gentle Shift

Instead of asking: “Why didn’t they call?”

Ask: “What am I building for myself now?”

This phase of life is not an ending. It is a redesign with New friendships, Community involvement, Mentoring, Spiritual growth, Creative expression.

When your world expands, you stop suffocating someone else to feel alive.

Useful tip

Create one new commitment that does not involve family approval:

1. A weekly class.

2. A volunteer role.

3. A walking group.

4. A long-delayed interest.

Let your life have multiple emotional sources. When roots spread wide, one branch bending does not shake the tree.

Essence

• Love your children.

• Love your partner.

• But also love your evolving self.

• Attachment born from emptiness creates pressure.

• Connection born from fullness creates joy.

Thought for the Day

I am not living only through others. I am still growing, still relevant, still becoming.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: The “Inside Us” series draws wisdom from psychology to reflect on our day-to-day life.

@@ 116 | Inside Us Series – 12 | When Every Sensation Feels Like a Warning | 
Last month, Ravi felt a small pain in his chest. Just a mild tightness. Within seconds, his mind had already travelled far ahead.

“Is this my heart?”

“What if it is serious?”

“My uncle had a heart attack at 52…”

“What if I collapse suddenly?”

He checked his pulse. Googled symptoms. Sat quietly, monitoring his breathing. By evening, the pain had reduced. But the fear had not. The next day it was a headache. Then a stomach sensation. Then a slight dizziness. Each small body signal became a headline in his mind. Medical tests were normal. Doctor reassured him: “You are fine.”

But his thoughts whispered, “What if they missed something?”. His wife once said gently, “You think about your health more than your health thinks about you.” He laughed. But inside, he was tired.

What Is Really Happening?

This is health anxiety sometimes called hypochondria. It is not drama. It is not attention-seeking. It is a nervous system stuck in alarm mode. The body produces thousands of sensations every day. Most people ignore them. An anxious mind magnifies them.

Normal becomes dangerous.

Temporary becomes permanent. Possibility becomes probability.

The Inner Experience

• The person feels: “If I don’t stay alert, something terrible will happen.”

• The family feels: “We don’t know how to reassure him anymore.”

• The doctor says: “You are medically fine.”

• But the mind says: “Not fully convinced.”

Reassurance gives relief. But only for a few hours. Then the cycle begins again.

Insight

Health anxiety is rarely about the body. It is about fear of loss. Fear of leaving loved ones.Fear of unfinished responsibilities. Fear of death itself. The mind believes, “If I monitor constantly, I can prevent mortality.” But monitoring is not control. And excessive scanning creates more sensations. The more you look for danger, the more you find it.

A Gentle Shift

Instead of asking: “What is wrong with my body?”

Ask: “What is my fear trying to protect?”

Then gently remind yourself: “My body is designed to fluctuate. Sensations are not emergencies.”

Limit the folllwing:

• Repeated Google searches

• Repeated checking

• Repeated doctor visits without medical need

Each check strengthens the anxiety muscle. Each pause weakens it.

Useful tip

When a sensation appears:

1. Rate it calmly from 1 - 10.

2. Continue your activity for 20 minutes without checking.

3. Re-rate it.

Most sensations reduce when attention reduces. Your body needs trust more than surveillance.

Essence

• Awareness is healthy.

• Obsession is exhausting.

• Life cannot be fully secured before it is lived.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will care for my body… but I will not let fear occupy it.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: The “Inside Us” series draws wisdom from psychology to reflect on our day-to-day life.

@@ 115 | Inside Us Series -11 | (Today’s reflection comes from a very personal loss of someone close to my… | 
(Today’s reflection comes from a very personal loss of someone close to my heart. I share it gently and with respect, knowing many have faced a similar sudden goodbye. If these words bring even a small measure of comfort, they have served their purpose.)

When Goodbye Comes Without Goodbye

Vimala and her mother shared something rare. Every morning, the phone would ring. Every evening, it would ring again.

“Did you eat?”

“Are you resting?”

“Don’t strain yourself.”

Simple words. But behind them was a heartbeat connection.

Her mother was strong. Fiercely independent. She insisted on living alone. “I don’t want to trouble anyone,” she would say. “And when my time comes, I want to go peacefully.”

Vimala would laugh it off. “Amma, don’t talk like that.”

One day there was a function at home. Guests. Noise. Responsibilities. She thought, “I’ll call Amma tomorrow.”

Tomorrow came. But the phone did not ring. Instead, a call came. Her mother had passed away peacefully in her sleep. No illness. No suffering. Exactly as she had wished. The world said, “What a blessing.” But Vimala’s heart said, “I didn’t say goodbye.”

And that sentence began to echo inside her.

The Unfinished Sentence

When someone we love dies suddenly, the pain is not only about loss. It is about incompletion.

• One more conversation.

• One more “I love you.”

• One more chance to ask forgiveness.

• One more ordinary morning call.

The mind keeps replaying:

“If only I had called.”

“If only I had known.”

This is not weakness. This is love searching for closure.

Insight

Closure is not given by the last conversation. Closure is given by the entire relationship.

Did Vimala love her mother? Yes. Did her mother know it? Absolutely. Did they speak almost every single day? Yes. One missed call cannot erase a lifetime of connection.

What Sudden Loss Teaches Us

When death comes quietly, without warning, it shakes our illusion of control. We believe: “I will say everything tomorrow.”

“There is time.”

“We will meet again.”

But life is not a scheduled appointment. It is a flowing river. And yet , something beautiful hides here.

Vimala’s mother left exactly how she wanted peacefully, without burden. Her final gift to her daughter was not a goodbye. It was dignity.

The pain Vimala feels today is not because something was missing between them. It is because something was so deep. Grief is love with nowhere to go.

For Anyone Carrying This Silent Hurt

Many people walk around with this same invisible ache:

A father who passed suddenly.

A friend lost in an accident.

A sibling gone without warning.

And inside there is one unfinished sentence. If this is you, listen gently: Your love was not dependent on one last conversation. The bond you built over years does not disappear because of one missed moment.

The relationship lives inside you now.

You can still speak to them.

You can still thank them.

You can still say, “I’m sorry.”

You can still say, “I love you.”

The heart does not need a phone line.

A Healing Exercise for Vimala

Tonight, sit quietly. Write a letter to Amma. Say everything you think you missed. Cry if you must. Then end with: “Amma, I release you in peace. Thank you for loving me the way you did.” You may be surprised. The heaviness may soften.Because love does not end. It only changes form.

The Deeper Truth

We do not get closure from death. We get perspective.

One day, Vimala will realise:

Her mother did not leave without goodbye. Every morning call was a goodbye. Every “Did you eat?” was a goodbye. Every ordinary day was sacred ,she just did not know it then. And neither do we.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will not postpone love. I will speak kindly. I will express gratitude. Because ordinary moments are not ordinary. They are life itself.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 114 | Inside Us Series- 5 | When the Mind Never Switches Off | 
At 2:17 a.m., Priya was awake. The house was silent. Everyone was asleep. But her mind was not.

• “Did I say something wrong in that meeting?”

• “What if Appa’s health report shows something serious?”

• “Why is my son so quiet these days?”

• “What if something happens to us financially?”

Her body was lying down.Her thoughts were running a marathon. Morning came. She packed lunch, smiled, reminded homework, attended calls. No one knew she had fought an invisible battle all night.

In the evening her husband said gently, “You worry too much.” She smiled. But inside she thought, “If I don’t worry, who will?”

What Is Really Happening?

This is not “overthinking.” This is anxiety. Anxiety is love mixed with fear and responsibility.

It tells us:

• Be alert.

• Be prepared.

• Prevent disaster.

It tries to protect. But when protection becomes constant anticipation of danger, peace slowly disappears.

The Inner Experience

• The anxious parent feels: “If I relax, something will go wrong.”

• The spouse feels: “I don’t know how to calm her.”

• The children feel: “Something must be wrong.”

• The anxious person feels tired… but cannot stop.

Anxiety does not shout like anger. It whispers endlessly.

The Truth

An anxious mind confuses possibility with probability. Yes, many things are possible. But not everything is likely. The brain is built to scan for danger. It is not built to rest unless we train it.

Deeper insight

Worry feels like control. But it is actually imagination without boundaries.

Instead of asking: “What if something goes wrong?”

Ask: “What is in my control today?”

And then do only that. If your mind runs ahead, bring it back like you would bring back a child running towards traffic not with anger, but with firmness.

Say internally: “Not now. I am safe in this moment.” Because most anxiety lives in tomorrow.

But life is happening today.

Useful tip

Every night, before sleep, write down:

• 3 things that went right today.

• 1 thing you handled well.

• 1 thing that is not in your control and consciously release it.

Anxiety shrinks when gratitude expands.

Essence

• Anxiety is a protective instinct that forgot how to rest.

• You are not weak because you worry.

• But you deserve a mind that knows how to pause.

Thought for the Day

Today, I will not try to control the future. I will care… and then I will trust.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: The “Inside Us” series draws wisdom from psychology to reflect on our day-to-day life.

@@ 113 | Epic Series – 15 | When Caring for Elders Feels Heavy | 
Smriti principle:

पितृदेवो भव

Pitru devo bhava

Meaning: See your parents and elders with respect and gratitude. They are sacred not because they are perfect, but because of their love and sacrifice.

Rohit lived in a busy city with his wife, two children, and his aging father.

Life was fast. Deadlines.Traffic. School meetings. EMI. Phone calls that never stopped.

His father had slowed down.He walked carefully. He asked the same questions again and again. He needed reminders for medicines. In the beginning, Rohit helped gently. But slowly, tiredness entered his voice.

One evening, his father called softly from the bedroom, “Rohit… can you come here? Rohit replied from the hall, a little loudly, “Appa… please… I just sat down.” There was Silence.

Later that night, Rohit went to drink water. He noticed the light in his father’s room was off, but his father was not asleep. He was sitting quietly, holding an old photo album. Rohit stepped closer.

In one photo, he saw himself as a small boy sitting proudly on his father’s shoulders. In another, his father was holding his tiny hand while crossing the road. In another, he was asleep on his father’s chest. A memory came back.

When Rohit was five, he was afraid of the dark. Every night he would wake up crying. His father would lift him, walk up and down the house, whispering, “I am here. Don’t worry.”

His father never said,

“I am tired.”

“I have office tomorrow.”

“I just sat down.”

Tears filled Rohit’s eyes.

The strong hands that once carried him were now slightly shaking. The fast steps that once ran behind him were now slow and careful. In that moment, something changed.

Care did not feel like a burden anymore. It felt like a circle of life completing itself.

Even in the great stories like the Ramayana and the Mahabharata, respect for elders was not shown out of fear. It came from understanding. From gratitude. From dharma lived with sincerity. But let us be honest.

Today, caring for elders is not always easy. Some people give money but have no time.

Some give time but feel exhausted. Some siblings argue silently about who is doing more. Some feel guilty for even feeling tired.

If you are feeling this way, you are not wrong. You are human.

Care becomes heavy when it is done with pressure, comparison, or silent resentment. Helping with irritation is like offering tea with a frown. The tea is there. But warmth is missing.

At the same time, we must remember caregivers also need care. Love without balance becomes exhaustion.

Balance without love becomes coldness. True dharma is both.

Insight

One day, if life blesses us with old age, we too may walk slowly. We may repeat stories. We may depend on someone to open a medicine strip.And our children are watching quietly. Not what we preach. But how we treat our parents.

Care is not about doing everything perfectly. It is about doing small things with presence.

• Sitting for five minutes.

• Listening fully.

• Speaking softly.

And also saying kindly, “I need some rest. Let us share this. That is not disrespect. That is healthy love.

Essence

Elders are not a burden. They are living reminders of time, sacrifice, and impermanence. Care with sincerity, not compulsion. Set boundaries with kindness. Share responsibilities without blame. Love matters more than perfection

Useful tip

If elder care feels heavy today:

1. Pause. Take three slow breaths.

2. Remember one sacrifice your parent or elder made for you.

3. Speak one gentle sentence to them.

4. Have one calm conversation at home about sharing responsibilities.

Small compassion reduces big tension.

Thought for the Day

I honor my elders with love and balance.Care is not compulsion. It is a circle of life.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 112 | Epic Series - 14 | When Desire for Comfort Becomes Restless | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

कामात्मा सुखसङ्गे दुःखमपि अनुभवति

Kamatma sukha-sange duhkham api anubhavati

Meaning - One who is attached to pleasures experiences both comfort and suffering.

Nikhil wanted a new phone. His old phone was working perfectly. But his friend had the latest model. Bigger screen. Better camera. Fancy features. From that day, his mind became restless.

He told his parents, “Everyone has it. I also need it.” After many requests, they bought it for his birthday. For two days, Nikhil was the happiest boy in school. He clicked photos. Played games. Showed everyone. On the third day, another friend came with an even newer model. Suddenly, his excitement dropped. The same phone that made him proud now felt “old.”

At night, his grandfather asked gently, “Why are you not smiling with your new phone?”. Nikhil said, “There is a better one now.” Grandfather smiled and said, “When I was young, we did not even have a phone. We had each other. And we were happy.” He continued softly, “If happiness depends on the next thing, it will keep running faster than you.” That sentence stayed in Nikhil’s mind. He realized something simple but powerful: The phone had not changed. His comparison and craving had.

In the Bhagavad Gita, we see this clearly. Duryodhana had a palace, power, and wealth. Yet he was restless. Rama lived in the forest with simple clothes and simple food. Yet he was peaceful. The difference was not comfort. It was attachment.

Today we say:

• “If I get this promotion, I’ll relax.”

• ”If I buy that car, I’ll feel complete.”

• ”If I move to a bigger house, life will be perfect.”

But once we get it, the mind quietly says, “What next?”. Desire itself is natural. But when desire controls the mind, peace disappears.

Desire is like eating chips from a packet. You say, “Just one.”Then you look down. the packet is empty. And you are still searching for more!

Insight

• Comfort gives pleasure.

• Contentment gives peace.

• Pleasure depends on things.

• Peace depends on the mind.

• When we keep chasing more, the mind keeps running.

• When we pause and appreciate, the mind settles.

• True luxury is not the biggest house.It is a calm heart inside any house.

Essence

Desire is natural. Attachment creates suffering. Enjoy what you have. Be grateful for it. Balance brings lasting happiness.

Useful tip

Today, when craving arises:

1. Pause and observe it.

2. Ask: “Do I need this, or do I just want excitement?”

3. Look around and name three things you already have and are grateful for

4. Gratitude slows the restless mind.

Thought for the Day

I enjoy comforts mindfully,

without letting desire disturb my peace. Contentment is my true luxury.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 111 |  | Living with Balance and Wholeness | 
The Bhagavad Gita says:

संतुलितचित्तः सदा सुखी

Santulitachittah sada sukhi

Meaning: A balanced mind is the foundation of lasting happiness.

Even great heroes like Rama, Arjuna, and Yudhishthira faced extremes like intense battles, personal loss, duty versus desire. Their peace did not come from an easy life. It came from an anchored mind.

They did not control the storm. They controlled their balance within it.

Let me tell you about Suresh. He is not a king.He is not a warrior. He is a software project manager in Chennai. Every morning, he leaves home before his children wake up. Every night, he returns after they sleep. Promotion was coming. Targets were heavy. His phone never stopped ringing.

One evening, he came home unusually early. His 8-year-old daughter was sitting with her school project, a drawing of “My Family.” She had drawn her mother. She had drawn her brother. She had drawn herself. There was an empty space beside them.

He smiled and asked gently, “Where is Appa?”

She looked up innocently and said, “You are always in office, so I left space. When you get time, you can stand here.” It was not accusation. It was not anger. It was simple truth.

That empty space hit him harder than any office deadline. That night, he did not sleep. He realized something earth-shattering: He was succeeding in everything except the life he was working so hard to build. He had tilted too far toward ambition. In trying to secure happiness, he had postponed living it.

The next day, he did not resign. He did not abandon responsibility. He made small adjustments:

• No office calls after 8 pm

• Sunday mornings became “family breakfast day.”

• Once a week, he walked his daughter to school.

Promotion still came. But more importantly, peace returned. Balance did not reduce his success. It deepened it.

Life constantly pulls us:

• Work pressure versus family presence

• Success versus sanity

• Ambition versus contentment

We swing like a pendulum.

Too much work burnout. Too much comfort stagnation. Too much emotion instability. Too much control rigidity. Wholeness comes when nothing inside us is neglected

Balance is like walking with a cup of hot filter coffee. Lean too much toward work ,you spill on family. Lean too much toward distraction ,you trip over responsibility. Walk steadily and you actually enjoy the coffee.

Insight

Happiness is not achieved by maximizing one part of life. It is achieved by harmonizing all parts.

A balanced mind:

• earns without greed

• loves without attachment

• works without losing self

• rests without guilt

Wholeness is not perfection.

It is alignment. When your actions match your values,

when your success includes your loved ones, when your mind is steady in praise and criticism, you become unshakeable.

Essence

• Balance is the true wealth

• Success without harmony is silent suffering

• Small daily adjustments create lifelong peace

• Wholeness is living fully in every role without losing yourself

Useful tip

Today ask yourself:

1. Where am I leaning too much?

2. What part of my life is silently waiting like that empty space in the drawing?

3. What one small correction can I make today?

Balance is not achieved in one dramatic decision. It is built in small daily corrections.

Thought for the Day

I live in balance. I nurture mind, heart, and action. Wholeness not extremes , is my path to peace.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 110 | Epic Series - 112 | When You Measure Life Against Others | 
Bhagavad Gita principle:

स्वधर्मे निधनं श्रेयः परधर्मो भयावहः

Swadharme nidhanam shreyah, paradharmo bhayavah

Meaning: Follow your own path. Measuring yourself against another’s path brings fear and suffering.

Arun and Vivek studied in the same class. Arun was sincere, hardworking, and calm. He scored good marks and his parents were proud. One day, exam results came.

Arun scored 92%. He was happy. He ran home and showed his father. His father smiled and asked, “Very good! What did Vivek score?” Arun said, “95%.” The smile disappeared. His father said, “See… he scored more. You must work harder.” That night, Arun sat quietly.

Yesterday, 92% made him happy. Today, 92% made him feel small. The marks had not changed. Only the comparison had.

Slowly, Arun stopped enjoying his success. Every achievement felt incomplete. Every step forward felt like he was still behind someone.

Years later, Arun got a good job. A stable life. A loving family. But whenever he heard about someone earning more, buying a bigger house, or traveling abroad, his mind whispered: “You are behind. One day, his young daughter showed him her drawing. It was imperfect. Colors outside the lines. Shapes uneven.

She asked, “Appa, is it nice?” He said, “It is beautiful.” She smiled proudly and ran away. He suddenly realized something powerful. She was happy not because her drawing was perfect. She was happy because it was hers. She was not comparing. She was simply expressing.

At that moment, Arun understood what he had lost all these years. He had stopped living his life. He had started measuring it.

In the Bhagavad Gita, each person is asked to follow their own dharma. Arjuna was not asked to become Bhishma. He was not asked to become Karna. He was asked to become the best version of himself. Because peace comes from alignment and not comparison.

Today, comparison follows us everywhere. We compare salaries. We compare houses. We compare children. We compare lifestyles. Social media makes it worse. Someone is always ahead in something. But we forget something important. You are seeing their highlight. You are living your full story.

Comparison is like checking your neighbor’s plate during dinner…Their food always looks tastier. But only you know what suits your stomach. Comparison steals joy silently. It turns blessings into burdens. It makes “enough” feel like “not enough.”

Here is the truth. Your life is not a race. It is a journey designed uniquely for you. A mango tree does not compare itself to a coconut tree. Both grow. Both give. Both fulfill their purpose. When you focus on your path, your mind becomes peaceful. When you compare, your mind becomes restless.

Essence

Comparison creates dissatisfaction.Gratitude creates contentment. Respect others’ success. But do not use it to measure your worth. Your journey has its own timing, meaning, and beauty.

Useful tip

Today, when comparison arises:

1. Pause and take a breath.

2. Remind yourself: “My journey is unique.”

3. Think of one blessing or progress in your life.

4. Feel gratitude for it.

Gratitude brings the mind back to peace.

Thought for the Day

I honor my journey. Comparison does not define my happiness. Contentment grows from gratitude, not envy.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 109 | Epic Series - 11 | When Pennies Control the Mind | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

सत्यं ब्रह्म तपः शौचं दानं यथाशक्ति

Satyam brahma tapaḥ shaucham daanam yathashakthi

Meaning - Charity, truth, purity, and giving should be done according to one’s means without attachment to wealth.

Every morning, Mr. Krishnan went to the same tea stall. Tea cost ₹12. Every day, he would search his pocket for exact change. If he had ₹20, he would hesitate. He would think, “If I give ₹20, I will lose ₹8.”

One day, a small boy was standing near the stall. He was watching others drink tea. He looked tired and hungry. The tea seller asked gently, “Do you want tea?”. The boy said softly, “I don’t have money.” Mr. Krishnan saw this. He looked at his pocket. He had only a ₹20 note.

His mind started calculating immediately.

• “If I give, I lose money.”

• ”If I start giving like this, people will expect more.”

• ”I must be careful.”

So he stayed silent.

Another man standing nearby, a daily wage worker, took out coins from his pocket. Slowly counting… ₹2, ₹5, ₹2, ₹1, ₹1. He gave ₹12 and said, “Give him tea.”

Mr. Krishnan was shocked. He knew that man earned much less than him. The boy smiled with pure joy, holding the hot tea like it was treasure. That smile stayed in Mr. Krishnan’s mind the whole day.

That night, he opened his cupboard and looked at his savings. Everything was safe. Nothing was missing. But something inside him felt empty. He realized a painful truth: His money was full. But his heart had become small. The poor man had fewer coins. But a richer mind.

That day, Mr. Krishnan understood that he was not protecting his money. He was imprisoning his peace.

Even in the Bhagavad Gita, wealth was never meant to control the mind.

Great kings and sages gave according to their capacity. Not to become poor, but to remain free. Because attachment creates fear. And fear destroys peace.

Today, many people can afford to give. But the mind says:

• “What if I need this later?”

• ”What if something goes wrong?”

• ”What if I lose security?”

So we hold tightly. Not realizing that tight fists create tight hearts. And tight hearts cannot feel peace.

Holding on to every rupee for peace is like locking your refrigerator to save food…. You save the food perfectly.

But you stay hungry!

Abundance is not measured by how much you keep. It is measured by how free you feel inside. When you trust life, your mind relaxes. When you give, even a little, fear loosens its grip. Because the real enemy is not lack of money. It is fear of lack. And that fear never goes away by holding more. It goes away by trusting more.

Essence

1. Money is a tool, not your master. When pennies control the mind, peace disappears

2. When generosity enters, peace returns

3. Even small giving creates inner richness.

Useful tip

Today, if fear of lack arises:

1. Pause and observe the fear.

2. Give something small ; money, time, or kindness.

3. Do not calculate. Just give with sincerity.

4. Notice the lightness inside you.

That lightness is true wealth.

Thought for the Day

I release fear of lack. I give with sincerity and peace, and abundance grows in my mind and heart.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 108 | Epic Series-10 | When Fear and Insecurity Take Over | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

अध्यात्मविज्ञानं शान्तिं लभते

Adhyatma-vijnanam shantim labhate

Meaning – Knowledge of the self and inner awareness brings peace, overcoming fear and insecurity.

Meena was always worried. Not because something was wrong. But because something might go wrong. She worried about money, her health, and her children’s future.

Her mind was like a TV that never switched off ; always playing “What if… What if… What if…”

One night, there was a power cut. The house went dark. Her young son held her hand and asked, “Amma, are you scared?” She said honestly, “Yes.” He smiled and said, “I am not scared. You are there.”

That simple sentence shook her.

He felt safe not because the future was guaranteed , but because she was present.

Then a deeper question arose in her mind: If I am always afraid, who is holding my hand inside?

She realized something profound. Nothing terrible had happened all these years. But fear had stolen her peace. The future had not hurt her. But fear of the future had.

In the Bhagavad Gita, Arjuna stood trembling on the battlefield. His hands shook. His mind imagined loss and disaster. Krishna did not promise him a perfect future. He gave him something greater , inner clarity.

Because when the mind becomes steady, fear loses its home.

Fear is like checking your exam results again and again. Even after seeing “PASS,” you refresh the page , just to confirm life has not changed its mind!

Insight

Fear does not live in the future. It lives in the imagination. Most of what we fear never happens. But the mind and body suffer as if it already did. Fear drains today’s strength to fight tomorrow’s imaginary problem.

Here is the truth. Security does not come from controlling life. It comes from trusting yourself.

A strong mind says, “Whatever comes, I will face it.” You have survived every yesterday. That strength is still within you.

We often ask:

• “Will I have enough?”

• ”What if I fall sick?”

• ”What if I lose everything?”

But while worrying about tomorrow, we forget to live today.

Fear weakens the present. Confidence strengthens it. And a strong present builds a safe future.

What True Strength Looks Like

Strength is not having guarantees. Strength is trusting your ability to handle uncertainty. Prepare wisely. Save responsibly. Care for your health. But do not let fear sit inside your heart like an unwanted tenant. Because fear never pays rent. It only occupies space.

Useful Tip

Today, when fear arises:

1. Pause. Take one slow breath.

2. Ask: “Is this real now, or imagined?”

3. Do one small action that strengthens you today.

4. Then release the rest.

Tell yourself gently: “I have handled life so far. I will handle what comes.”

Essence

Fear makes you feel weak. Inner awareness reminds you that you are stronger than your fears.

Peace does not come from knowing the future. It comes from trusting yourself in the present.

Thought for the Day

I focus on what I can strengthen today. Fear of the future cannot control my peace.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 107 | Epic Series - 9 | When We Postpone Health for “Later | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

शरीरं यथासंख्यम् ध्यानं साधनं योगे

Shariram yathasankhyam dhyanam sadhanam yoge

Meaning - The body is a temple; caring for it is a spiritual and practical duty.

Srinivasan was a hardworking father. Every morning he said,

“After this project, I will start walking.” Every evening he said, “From Monday, no more sweets.” Monday never came. But laddoos always came.

His daughter once said, “Appa, come play badminton.” He smiled, patted his stomach, and said, “Appa is busy building your future.” One day in the office, he felt tightness in his chest. Not pain… just discomfort. He ignored it. “Gas problem,” he said confidently like all self-certified doctors.

A week later, he was in a hospital bed. Machines were beeping. His daughter was sitting quietly, holding his hand. She whispered, “Appa, you said you are building my future. But I need you in it.”That sentence broke him more than the diagnosis.

In that silent hospital room, he realized: He had saved money.

He had bought insurance. He had secured investments. But he had not secured his own body. And without the body…

who will enjoy the savings?

In the Bhagavad Gita, discipline is not optional. Heroes prepared themselves physically and mentally. Rama trained. Arjuna practiced daily. They did not say, “After the war, I will become fit.” Strength came before responsibility.

Ignoring health is like ignoring your phone battery warning. 1% remaining. You say, “It will survive.” Two minutes later , blackout. Then you run around searching for charger like it is a treasure hunt!

Insight

• We postpone health because we think we have time.

• But health does not shout.

It whispers first.Then it nudges. Then it forces.

Small neglect becomes silent damage. You can delay a meeting.You can delay a purchase.You cannot delay consequences. The body keeps score quietly. And here is the deeper truth: When you care for your body, you are not being selfish. You are protecting everyone who depends on you.Your family does not need your overtime. They need your heartbeat.

We say:

• “After this deadline.”

• ”After the children grow up.”

• ”After I retire.”

But illness does not check your calendar. Cravings feel sweet for five minutes. Regret tastes bitter for years.

Useful tip

Small steps:

• 10 minutes walking.

• One less spoon of sugar.

• Sleeping 30 minutes earlier.

• Drinking water before coffee.

Tiny acts.Huge impact.

Today, do one thing your future self will thank you for:

1. Take a short walk.

2. Stretch your body gently.

3. Eat one mindful meal without distraction.

4. Sleep on time.

Tell yourself,” I am not postponing my life.”

Essence

Money can be earned again.

Lost time cannot. Deadlines will keep coming. But your body will not renew automatically. Care for it today.

Because your body is not a machine.It is the home where your soul lives.

Thought for the Day

I care for my body today,

not tomorrow. Discipline and awareness are my true strength.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 106 | Epic Series - 8 | When the Glass Feels Half Empty | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

न कर्मणि मन्यते फलम्

Na karmani manyate phalam

Meaning - Do your duty without attaching to results or judging others.

Once, a visitor came to Hastinapura and asked two different princes about the people in the kingdom.

First he asked Duryodhana, “Tell me, what kind of people live here?”. Duryodhana replied, “They are selfish, dishonest, and jealous. It is hard to find a good person.”

Then the visitor asked Yudhishthira the same question. Yudhishthira smiled and said, “They are kind, generous, and noble. It is hard to find a bad person.” Same kingdom. Same people. But two different eyes.

Krishna later explained gently, “We often see outside what we carry inside.”

Imagine a typical morning at home. You wake up and see the kitchen not fully cleaned.

Immediately the mind says, “Why is this always left for me?”.

You go to work. A colleague gives a suggestion. Mind says, “Why is he interfering?”

Your child scores 80 marks. Mind says, “Why not 90?”

Slowly the day becomes heavy.

Now imagine the same day with a small shift:-

Kitchen not cleaned? “Someone must have been tired. Let me help.”

Colleague suggests something? “Maybe I can learn something here.”

Child scores 80? “Wonderful effort. Let’s improve together.

Nothing outside changed. Only the lens changed. Just like Yudhishthira and Duryodhana.

• Yudhishthira was known for fairness and humility

• Krishna guided with compassion, not criticism

• Those who judged harshly created conflict and confusion

Judgment clouds clarity. Compassion brings wisdom.

Today we often

• See the half-empty glass

• Judge family, friends, colleagues

• Give advice easily but resist feedback

• Reject help because “I know better”

And slowly:

• Relationships become tense

• Growth becomes slow

• Happiness feels distant

The glass was never empty.

Our mind was.

Judging others while ignoring your own flaws is like cleaning your neighbour’s balcony while pigeons are sitting comfortably on your own head!

Insight

• Peace does not come from correcting others. It comes from correcting ourselves.

• When we shift from: “Why are they like this?” to “What can I learn here?”, Life becomes lighter.

Gratitude turns scarcity into abundance. Openness turns conflict into connection. Self-reflection turns ego into wisdom.

Useful tip

• Today, when negativity arises:

1. Pause. Take one slow breath.

2. Ask: “What is this situation teaching me?”

3. Replace one complaint with one appreciation.

Small shifts create big peace

Thought for the Day

I choose gratitude over criticism. I reflect before I react. I see the good that already exists.When my lens changes, my world changes.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 105 | Epic Series - 7 | When Love Turns into Constant bickering | 
सुख-दुःखे सम भाव: प्रेमस्य आधारः

Sukh-duhkhe sama bhavah: premasya adhaarah

Meaning - Love stays strong when we remain steady in both happiness and difficulty.

Raghavan is 64. Meenakshi is 60. Married for 35 years.They have seen everything together like rented houses, tight budgets, school fees, medical emergencies, weddings, responsibilities.

Now the children are settled.

One in Bengaluru. One abroad. The house is neat. Quiet. Almost too quiet.

One Sunday morning Meenakshi walks into the hall. The fan is running.The newspaper is open. Tea cup half empty. “Again you left the fan on all night?” she asks. Raghavan lowers the paper.

“It was hot.”…….”It’s December.”……….”You always exaggerate.” That’s it.

Small spark !!! She goes into the kitchen ; slightly louder than necessary. Vessels make more sound than required. He increases the TV volume.Two mature adults. Silent war.By afternoon, no one speaks.But here is what is not spoken:

She feels “I cook, I manage, I think about expenses. Why doesn’t he care about small things?”

He feels, “I worked my whole life for this family. Why does she only notice mistakes?”

In the evening, he quietly cuts fruits and keeps a plate near her. She pretends not to notice. Later that night, she leaves his medicines neatly arranged near his bedside. No apology. No big talk.But love is still there. Hidden beneath ego.

Even in the Epics:

• Rama and Sita faced exile and separation

• Arjuna and Draupadi faced humiliation and war

• Great love was tested again and again

Their bond survived because they stood steady during hardship. Not perfect. But patient.

In later years, couples often face:

• Irritation over habits built for decades

• Health concerns and stress

• Emotional emptiness after children leave

Small comments slowly replace warm conversations.

• “Did you take your tablet?”

• ”Why is this kept here?”

• ”How many times should I tell you?”

The romance of youth becomes routine. And routine, if not handled carefully, becomes resentment.

After 30+ years of marriage, couples can argue without raising their voice.Just one look is enough.It’s a silent language only long-married people understand!

Insight

Long marriages don’t collapse suddenly.They slowly dry out when appreciation stops. Most complaints are not about fans, lights, or medicine. They are about one deep question: “Do I still matter to you?”. When we feel valued, we soften. When we feel ignored, we harden.

The real danger is not disagreement. It is taking each other for granted.Love in youth is excitement. Love in later years must become kindness.

• Speak softly

• Overlook small mistakes

• Thank each other often

• Protect peace more than ego

After decades together, you are not just husband and wife. You are each other’s life story. That is sacred.

Essence

• Romance becomes companionship

• Small irritations can build emotional distance

• Gratitude and patience keep love alive

Useful tip

If irritation arises today:

1. Pause for 10 seconds

2. Ask: “Is this bigger than our 35 years together?”

3. Say one gentle sentence - “Peace is more valuable than proving a point”

Thought for the Day

After walking together for many years, I choose peace over ego.Small habits will not overshadow a lifetime of love.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 104 | Epic Series -6 | When Planning Becomes a Prison | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

कालं यथा नीच्यते नास्ति तस्यापि फलम्

Kalam yatha neechyate naasti tasyapi phalam

Meaning - Time is fleeting; obsessing over future outcomes yields little peace or control.

Even in the epics:

• King Dasharatha’s excessive attachment to the future led to worry, fear, and unintended sorrow

• Heroes like Arjuna and Rama focused on action in the present, trusting dharma for the outcomes

• Overplanning for every possibility rarely prevents suffering; action + mindfulness does

We often:

• Save excessively or plan endlessly for future “security”

• Obsess over investments, insurance, or contingencies

• Forget to live, enjoy, or act meaningfully today

The mind whispers:

• “Better safe than sorry”

• “I will act now only if I’m fully prepared”

• The result: present moments slip away, and anxiety accumulates

Overplanning for the future is like wearing a full-body suit of armor to fetch milk, you are “safe,” but clumsy, stressed, and missing the joy of walking freely!

Insight

• Security is an illusion; presence is power.

• Action and mindfulness in the present create resilience

• Obsession with future control breeds fear, frustration, and missed opportunities

• Balance: plan enough to act responsibly, but live fully today

Essence

• Excessive focus on future security robs present peace

• Mindful action now builds true preparedness

• Trust in effort and presence, not fear-driven planning, restores balance

Useful tip

Today, if you find yourself overplanning or worrying about the future:

1. Pause and breathe

2. Ask: “What action can I take today to strengthen myself?”

3. Release control over things beyond your reach

4. Engage fully in what’s possible right now

Even a small shift toward presence restores calm and clarity.

Thought for the Day

I act responsibly today, without obsessing over tomorrow. Presence is my true security.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

Ps: The “epic series” draws wisdom from our Itihasa and purana to reflect on and illuminate our everyday lives.

@@ 103 |  | When Pride Blinds You to Reality | 
Bhagwat Gita says:

अहंकारो बलं विनाशकं

Ahankaro balam vinashakam

Ego and pride, when unchecked, can lead to downfall.

Once there was a boy named Harish. He loved building sandcastles at the beach.One day, he built the biggest, most beautiful sandcastle he had ever made. Towers, walls, even tiny windows all perfect.

Harish stood back and said proudly: “No one can make a sandcastle like this! Everyone will admire mine!”. People came and looked. Some clapped. Some took pictures. Harish’s chest swelled with pride.He felt important. He felt safe. He thought: “I am clever. I am talented. I am the best!”

But just then, a sudden wave rushed in. Ayyo! The castle crumbled.Harish froze. His heart sank. All his pride, all his boasting, was gone in seconds.

An old man sitting nearby smiled gently and said: “Son, the castle was never yours. What matters is your hands, your heart, and how you build the next one.”

Harish understood. Pride in things that don’t last can blind you. True strength is how you act, how you care, and who you are, not what you make or achieve.

Insight

This happens every day, in every home:

• You feel proud because your phone is the newest model.

• You feel proud because you got the highest marks.

• You feel proud because your parents, your house, or your car is impressive.

But life can flip in a heartbeat : a phone breaks, grades drop, money comes and goes.Pride in temporary things is like a sandcastle at the edge of the sea which is beautiful, but fragile.

Pride is like saying: “My sandcastle is the best!”. Meanwhile, a tiny crab or a wave can destroy it instantly.

Essence

• Pride in what doesn’t last blinds us to reality.

• True power comes from kindness, honesty, patience, and humility.

• Attachment to things, status, or praise creates pain and worry.

• Humility keeps your heart calm, adaptable, and strong.

Useful tip

If you feel proud today:

• Pause. Ask: “Is this really eternal, or just temporary?”

• Smile at your achievement, but don’t cling to it.

• Act with humility and gratitude, no matter what others think.

Even this tiny awareness softens arrogance and grows peace inside.

Thought for the Day

I honor what I have done,

but I stay humble and grounded. Pride cannot define me; my character does.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: The “Epic Series” draws wisdom from our Itihasa and Puranas to reflect on and illuminate our everyday lives.

@@ 102 | Health Series -2 | When Eating Less Felt Like Being Good | 
After 60, Saraswathi proudly started eating less. “Why eat so much now?” she would say. “My body doesn’t need it.” Rice became just a small plate, dal turned watery, curd skipped more often. She told friends, “See, I am controlling my food!” She felt disciplined.

But slowly, small things became harder. Climbing stairs left her breathless. Standing for long made her knees ache. Getting up from the floor needed help. One morning, she whispered to herself, “I eat so little… still my strength is slipping away.”

What Is Really Happening?

Here’s the truth: As we grow older, our appetite may drop, but our body’s need for nourishment does not. Even if your stomach feels light, your body still needs protein from dals, paneer, milk, or sprouts, good fats from nuts and seeds, and minerals from green vegetables and fruits to keep muscles, bones, and energy strong.

When we eat less without thinking about quality, the muscles quietly waste away. The weight may stay the same, so it feels like “just aging.” But it’s not age, it’s under-nourishment slowly taking strength away.

Little- Known Tip: Just adding a small bowl of sprouts or a few peanuts, a glass of milk or curd, and a handful of nuts during the day can keep your muscles alive and energy up.

The Inner Experience

• The body whispers: “I am getting weaker.”

• The mind accepts: “This is just how it is at my age.”

• The family cheers: “She eats very little, that’s so disciplined.”

Everyone thinks eating less is discipline. But the body quietly asks for care, support, and nourishment.

Simple Insights

• Eating less is not always eating right.

• Strength comes from good nourishment, not skipping meals.

• At every age, the body is like a house, if you don’t give it healthy building blocks, it starts to weaken quietly.

Simple, Gentle Exercises for Strength

• Sit to Stand: From a chair, stand up slowly and sit down again, a few times each morning.

• Heel Lifts: Hold a chair for support and lift your heels up and down to strengthen calves and ankles.

• Shoulder Rolls: Roll your shoulders forward and backward while sitting to loosen stiffness.

• Walking Around: Even a few minutes in the house or garden keeps muscles active.

Simple, Powerful Tips

• Focus on what you eat, not just how little you eat.

• Include dal, paneer, curd, milk, sprouts, nuts, vegetables, and fruits regularly.

• Drink water often to stay hydrated.

• Gentle daily movements like walking, standing, or stretching keep muscles and joints strong.

Essence

• Appetite may reduce, but body needs stay the same.

• Weakness is not always age, it may be hidden under-nourishment.

• Nourishment and gentle movement are the keys to independence, energy, and joy.

Thought for the Day

I will feed my body with love and simple nourishment, not starve it in the name of discipline.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: The “Health Series” blends simple wisdom and modern knowledge to encourage awarenes, not replace medical advice.

@@ 101 | Health series -1 | When tiredness quietly became the normal | 
Meenakshi finishes her morning work. Nothing difficult. Only her regular household routine. By afternoon, she feels tired. She sits down and says softly, “Nowadays, I feel tired very easily.”

Her family replies with concern, “It is age. This happens.” Meenakshi agrees. She rests more. She moves less. She avoids going out because she does not feel energetic.

Days pass. Weeks pass. The tiredness stays. One day she says quietly, “I rest so much… still I feel tired.”

What is really happening?

Here is something simple to remember. As we grow older,

our body does not ask for big effort. It only asks not to be forgotten.

Many people think, “Let energy come first, then I will move.” But the body often says, “Move a little, and I will give you energy.”

Very small movement…

a short walk, stretching the arms, standing in the balcony,

or walking inside the house…

These gentle actions tell the body, “I am still here. I still care.” When the body is used kindly, it responds kindly. When it is not used for long, it slowly becomes quiet.

This is not a failure.This is not weakness. It is simply the body waiting for attention.

Remember this.

Tiredness does not always mean stop completely. Sometimes it means, “Start slowly… very slowly.”

Do not force. Do not compare. Just begin gently.

Rest is important. Movement is also important. Both together keep the body awake.

Thought for the Day

I will be kind to my body. I will move gently… and allow strength to return.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

Ps: The “Health series” reflects simple, widely accepted lifestyle wisdom meant to encourage awareness, not replace medical advice.

@@ 100 | Inside us Series - 9 | Caught in the Middle, When Loyalties Clash at Home | 
Vikram lives with his mother and his wife, Anjali. Both women love him deeply, but their expectations often clash:

His mother wants traditional routines followed. His wife wants her own space and decision-making authority. Small disagreements over meals, chores, or visiting relatives escalate quickly

Vikram feels torn, “I don’t want to hurt my mother.I also want to support my wife and our independence.”

The tension creates silent stress. Meals are quiet. Conversations feel like minefields. Even small disagreements cause guilt and anxiety

What Is Really Happening?

• Conflicts often stem from unspoken expectations, past patterns, and emotional attachment.

• Male members often feel responsible for “balancing” love and loyalty, leading to chronic stress.

• Both women may feel unheard, escalating resentment over time.

Insight

Conflict is natural when multiple generations share space, but awareness, boundaries, and empathy can prevent escalation. Vikram’s role is not to “pick sides” but to facilitate respectful communication and fairness.

Useful tip

• Listen to both perspectives without judgment

• Encourage dialogue: “Let’s discuss what works for all of us calmly.”

• Set boundaries collectively: chores, privacy, and visiting routines

• Avoid taking sides; focus on solutions that respect everyone’s needs

Essence

• Family love can create emotional friction if boundaries and communication are unclear

• Male members are mediators, not referees

• Respect, fairness, and empathy reduce tension

Thought for the Day

I will honor both my mother and my spouse, facilitate understanding,and maintain balance without sacrificing love or peace.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 99 | Inside Us Series - 8 | Guilt After Losing a Loved One | 
Last year, Anil lost his father. After the funeral, in the quiet moments, memories came back like little shadows. He remembered the phone calls he never made, the talks he postponed, the small acts of care he thought he could always do “tomorrow.”

• I should have called more often.”

• ”I should have stayed by his side in his last days.”

• ”Why didn’t I understand him better?”

Even though he loved his father very much, these regrets felt like a heavy fog around his heart. Every memory seemed to whisper, “If only…”

What Is Really Happening?

Feeling guilty after someone dies is normal. It reminds us of things we didn’t do, words we didn’t say, or moments we missed. When we are sad, this guilt can feel louder, often hiding the real love and care that was always there.

But guilt is not a sign that we failed; it is a sign that we loved. It shows how much we cared and how much we wanted to do more.

One evening, while looking through his father’s things, Anil found an old notebook filled with letters his father had written to him. They were full of encouragement, care, and pride.

One letter said: “Son, I always knew you loved me. Never doubt that. Life is messy, and no one is perfect. Remember, love is not measured by perfect words or actions, but by a heart that cares for another every single day.”

In that moment, Anil understood that his father had always felt loved. The “missed moments” were much smaller than the lifetime of love and care they shared.

Insight

• Guilt cannot change the past. But it can teach us to be kind to ourselves and to others.

• Accepting guilt, forgiving yourself, and remembering your loved one helps your heart heal and lets love stay instead of pain.

Useful Tip

• Say the words you didn’t get to say out loud, write letters, keep a journal, or pray; let your heart speak.

• Remember the love and care you shared, not just the things you regret.

• Forgive yourself on purpose, knowing you did your best as a human.

Thought for the Day

I honor the love we shared,

accept what I cannot change,

and allow my heart to heal, one gentle step at a time.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

Ps: The “inside us” series draws on human psychology to reflect on our everyday lives.

@@ 98 | Inside us Series -7 | When Peace Feels Uncomfortable | 
Anjali’s life is finally stable.No major conflicts.Supportive partner. Children doing well. Yet she feels restless. When everything is calm, she becomes irritable. She picks small faults. She creates unnecessary arguments.

Later, she feels guilty and confused. “Why can’t I just enjoy when things are good?” she wonders.

What Is Really Happening?

This is fear of calm. For those who grew up in emotional chaos or constant stress, the nervous system learns to stay alert. Calm feels unfamiliar. Silence feels unsafe. So the mind creates disturbance because disturbance feels known.

The Inner Experience

• The person feels: “Something bad is about to happen.”

• The partner feels: “Nothing I do is enough.”

• Children sense tension even without conflict.

No one understands the pattern.

Insight

• Peace is not always experienced as safety.

• Sometimes it feels like vulnerability.

• The body needs time to learn that calm can be trusted.

Useful tip

When things feel “too quiet,” pause and tell yourself: “Right now, nothing is wrong.” Let the body catch up with reality.

Essence

• The nervous system repeats what it knows

• Chaos can feel familiar; calm can feel threatening

• Safety is a learned experience

Thought for the Day

I allow calm to exist without disturbing it.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 97 | Inside us Series - 6 | When Parents Begin to Feel Like a Burden | 
Pushpa used to run the house. Everyone depended on her. Meals were ready. Decisions were hers. The house moved around her rhythm.

Now she moves slowly. Her son tells her, “Amma, sit. I will do it.” He means well. But slowly, no one asks her opinion anymore. Conversations happen over her head. Decisions are taken “for her good.”

She eats, watches TV, sleeps. One day she tells a neighbour softly, “I feel like my work is done now.”

What Is Really Happening?

This is loss of emotional dignity. Aging doesn’t only reduce physical strength. It reduces felt usefulness.

When elders are cared for but not included, they begin to feel like a burden even in loving homes.

The Inner Experience

• The elder feels: “I am only tolerated, not needed.”

• The children feel: “We are doing our best.”

• The grandchildren don’t know how to connect.

Love exists. Meaning slowly disappears.

Insight

• Care that removes dignity causes silent suffering.

• Elders don’t need constant help. They need respectful relevance.

Useful tip

• Instead of saying: “Don’t worry, we will handle it” , Try: “What do you think we should do?”

• Give them a role. Give them a voice.

Essence

• Being needed keeps people alive inside

• Dependency without dignity hurts deeply

• Respect is the strongest form of care

Thought for the Day

I will care without taking away dignity.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 96 | Inside us Series -5 | When Love Slowly Turns Into Control | 
Raghavan loves his son deeply. He has struggled all his life, made sacrifices, and wants his son to be “safe.” So he checks often:”Why are you coming home late?”……..”Is this job stable?”……”That friend doesn’t seem right.”………”We know better. We have seen life.”

He believes this is care. His son listens quietly. He doesn’t argue. But he shares less. Decides less. Visits less.

At home, Raghavan complains,

“Children don’t respect elders anymore. What he doesn’t see is that his son didn’t stop loving him. He stopped feeling trusted.

What Is Really Happening?

This is fear wearing the mask of love. When anxiety is not recognised, it turns into control. Control feels protective to the one exerting it. But suffocating to the one receiving it.

The Inner Experience

• The parent feels: “If I don’t intervene, something will go wrong.”

• The adult child feels: “I am never enough.”

• The spouse feels caught in between.

• Grandparents feel hurt by distance they don’t understand.

Everyone feels justified. Everyone feels wounded.

Insight

• Care guides.

• Control restricts.

The intention may be love, but the impact becomes distance.

Useful tip

• Before advising or intervening, pause and ask: “Am I speaking from concern, or from fear?”

• Concern supports growth. Fear tries to prevent life.

Essence

• Love without trust becomes pressure

• Pressure breaks connection

• Trust keeps relationships alive

Thought for the Day

I will express love in ways that strengthen, not suffocate.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 95 | Epic Series – 4 | When You Are Wronged and No One Speaks Up | 
Mahabharata says:

यत्र नार्यस्तु पूज्यन्ते रमन्ते तत्र देवताः

Yatra naaryastu poojyante ramante tatra devataah

Meaning - Where a person’s dignity is respected, there goodness and right conduct live.

In the royal court, Draupadi stood humiliated.Elders were present. Scholars were present. Warriors were present. Yet… no one spoke.

She asked only one question: “Where is dharma?”. Silence was the answer. Her pain was not just what was being done to her; it was that no one stood up to protect her.

In real life, this happens to us too. You are treated unfairly in a meeting, in a family discussion, or in a public place.

Others see it.They know it is wrong. But they remain silent.

The pain hurts more than the insult itself. A question arises inside: “Why did no one stand up for me?”

The Inner Conflict

The mind keeps replaying the moment: “Was it my fault?”…….”Did I say something wrong?”……,”Should I have handled it differently?”

What hurts most is this thought: Why did silence feel heavier than words?. At that moment, trust in people gets shaken.

Insight

The deepest wound is not humiliation; it is being left alone during humiliation.

Draupadi’s faith was not in people. Her faith was in dharma itself.

When human voices failed her,

her inner surrender became her strength.

Essence

• Silence in the face of injustice causes deep harm

• Dignity is a right, not a favor given by others

• True inner strength begins when outside support disappears

Useful tip

If you feel unheard today, gently remind yourself: “My worth does not reduce because others stayed silent. Stand tall, even if you are standing alone.

Thought for the Day

I will not measure my dignity

by others’ courage or silence.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 94 | Inside us Series -4 | When People Stop Sharing , Not Because They Don’t Care | 
Meena used to talk a lot.She talked about her day, small worries, things that made her happy. Slowly, people started replying: “You are thinking too much.”……..”This is very small”……….”Just adjust.”……..”Why do you take everything so seriously?”

After some time, Meena spoke less. Now the family says: “She doesn’t share anything.”……”She keeps everything inside.”

But no one noticed when she stopped trying to speak.

What Is Really Happening?

People don’t stop sharing because they want to hide things. They stop sharing when their feelings are ignored.

Each time someone says, “It’s nothing”….”Don’t feel like that”

The mind learns: “It is not safe to talk here.” So the person becomes quiet slowly.

What People Feel Inside

• The quiet person feels,”My feelings don’t matter.”

• Others feel:”She has changed.”

• Elders feel confused: “Earlier, children talked more.”

No one wants to hurt anyone. But distance grows silently.

Insight

Listening is not about giving advice. Listening is about allowing feelings.

Advice given before understanding can close hearts. Understanding feelings first opens hearts.

Useful tip

When someone shares, say this first: “That sounds difficult.”…..”I can understand why you feel like this.”

Even if you don’t agree. Even if you want to give advice later. These simple words can rebuild trust.

Essence

• Silence is often for self-protection

• Not because of lack of love

• Feeling understood heals more than advice

Thought for the Day

I will not hurry to correct feelings. I will first try to understand them.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 93 | Inside Us Series - 3 | When Small Irritations Become Big Reactions | 
Anand comes home tired. He notices the TV volume is high.

The newspaper is not in its place. Dinner is ten minutes late. Nothing major.

Yet his tone becomes sharp.

“Why is everything always so disorganised?” His wife feels hurt. She has been managing work, children, and her aging father’s hospital visits. She snaps back.”You only see what is wrong!” The children retreat to their rooms.Grandparents sit silently.

Later, Anand himself wonders,

“Why did I react so strongly? It was such a small thing.”

What Is Really Happening?

This is not about the TV or dinner. This is emotional overload. When stress has no outlet, the mind looks for the nearest safe place to release it. Home becomes that place.

Small irritations become emotional triggers because the container is already full.

The Inner Experience

• The tired person feels: “I have no space to breathe.”

• The other feels: “I am not appreciated, only criticised.”

• Children feel: “Home is tense. Better stay quiet.”

• Elders feel: “We shouldn’t interfere.”

No one feels safe enough to be open.

Insight

Strong reactions are often misdirected emotions.What looks like anger is usually:

• fatigue

• helplessness

• feeling unvalued

• long-held stress

Useful tip

Before reacting, silently ask: “What am I really carrying today?” Then speak the feeling, not the irritation.

Instead of:

• “Why is this not done?”

Try:

• “I am feeling very drained today. I need some quiet time.”

This single shift changes the emotional climate.

Essence

• Homes break not due to big fights

• But due to unprocessed daily stress

• Expression heals; explosion damages

Thought for the Day

I will not unload my tiredness on those I love. I will name my feelings, not act them out.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 92 | Samskara Series-2 | Pumsavana / Ayush Homam - Ensuring Child’s Health and Longevity | 
Dharmashastra says:

संततिं पुष्टिं चायुष्यम् साधयेत् पूर्वकृतं।

सर्वसुखायै च बालकं पोषयेत् सततं।

Santatim pushtim chaayushyam sadhayet poorvakritam.

Sarvasukhayaai cha balakam poshayet satatam.

Meaning: Parents should consciously nurture the child’s health, vitality, and longevity even before birth, to ensure overall well-being and happiness.

A struggle many families silently face is how to protect and nurture a child in today’s uncertain world

• “Is my child healthy and strong?”

• ”How do I ensure they grow up happy, resilient, and free from illness?”

• ”What habits and environment should I provide before and during pregnancy?”

Dharma Shastra teaches: early care, mindful practices, and supportive environments influence a child’s health and life trajectory.

(1) A couple planning a child focuses solely on genetics and doctor checkups.

• Despite good medical care, the mother experiences stress, irregular diet, and long working hours.

• The baby is born healthy but easily prone to anxiety and digestive issues.

Dharma perspective: Ayush Homam and related rituals symbolize attention to physical, mental, and spiritual well-being, not just ceremony ; mindful preparation matters more than formalities.

(2) Another couple Follows a balanced diet, light exercise, and daily meditation for calmness

• Creates a peaceful home environment with music, stories, and laughter

• Involves grandparents in supportive guidance

Outcome:

• Pregnancy is smoother

• Child is born healthy and calm

• Family relationships grow stronger

This demonstrates the practical wisdom behind Pumsavana / Ayush Homam ; it emphasizes creating a holistic environment for the child before birth.

Insight

• The child’s well-being begins even before conception and during pregnancy

• Mental, emotional, and physical preparation are equally important as medical care

• Dharma encourages conscious, holistic nurturing over mechanical ritual performance

Essence

• Health, resilience, and happiness of a child are shaped by parental awareness and environment

• Rituals like Ayush Homam are tools to cultivate mindfulness and intentional care

• Early care ensures a lifetime of stability, strength, and contentment

Useful Tip

Today, parents or soon-to-be parents can:

• Create a calm, supportive home environment

• Pay attention to nutrition, rest, and emotional balance

• Even small mindful actions, gentle music, gratitude practices, peaceful communication positively influence the child’s growth

Thought for the Day

A child’s strength, health, and happiness begin with mindful preparation and intentional care.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 91 | Inside Us Series-2 | When Expectations Are Silent, But Resentment Is Loud | 
Suma prides herself on being “adjusting.” She wakes up early, finishes her work, packs lunch, manages the home, and still reaches office on time. She rarely asks for help. When something bothers her, she tells herself, “It’s okay, I will manage.”

Her husband Mahesh assumes all is well. After all, she never complains. At home, his mother often says, “Earlier we managed everything without help. These days people expect too much.” Suma smiles and says nothing.

Months pass. One evening, a small comment triggers a sharp response. Mahesh is confused. “Why are you reacting like this? I didn’t even know this was an issue.” Suma snaps, “I have been adjusting for years!”

What Is Really Happening?

This is not anger. This is unspoken expectation turning into resentment. When expectations are not expressed, they don’t disappear. They accumulate quietly.

The mind keeps a silent score: “I did this for you. I adjusted there. I didn’t say anything then”

One day, the weight spills out often over something small.

The Inner Experience

• The one adjusting feels: “No one values my effort.”

• The other feels: “I was never told this mattered.

• Elders feel accused for rules they didn’t know were hurting.

Everyone feels misunderstood.

Insight

• Adjustment without communication is not sacrifice. It is self-neglect

• Healthy families are not those with fewer expectations; they are those where expectations are spoken early and gently.

Useful tip

• Before resentment builds, try this sentence: “I am okay doing this, but I need you to know it matters to me.”

• It is not a complaint.It is clarity.

Essence

• Silence does not protect relationships

• Clarity does

• Unsaid expectations create emotional debt

Thought for the Day

I will not expect others to read my mind.I will speak with respect, before resentment speaks for me.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 90 | Inside us Series – 1 | When Everyone Is Busy, But Everyone Feels Alone | 
Ramesh and Lakshmi have been married for 18 years. Ramesh leaves early for work. Lakshmi manages her job, the house, and the children’s schedules. Evenings are rushed. Dinner happens with phones nearby. Conversations are mostly about bills, school, or “Did you pay that?”. They are not fighting. They are not unhappy either. But they are not connected.

In the same house, Appa and Amma are retired. They don’t complain. They have food, TV, and health.Yet Appa often says, “Everyone is busy these days.” Amma nods quietly.

No one has done anything wrong. Still, everyone feels a little unseen.

What Is Really Happening?

This is not a family problem. This is an attention problem. When life becomes task-driven, relationships slowly become transactional. We talk to manage, not to connect.

Over time:

• Couples become efficient teammates, not emotional partners

• Children speak when needed, not when they feel

• Elders withdraw, assuming they should not disturb

No anger. No drama.Just quiet emotional distance.

The Inner Impact (Often Unnoticed)

• The husband feels: “No one really needs me, only my role.”

• The wife feels: “I am holding everything together, but no one sees me.”

• The grandparents feel: “We are present, but irrelevant.”

These feelings don’t shout. They sit silently and drain warmth from the home.

Insight

• Connection does not require more time. It requires intentional presence

• Five undistracted minutes of genuine listening can heal more than hours of being physically together.

Useful tip

Once a day, ask just one human question and pause.

• To your spouse: “How was today for you really?”

• To your child: “What made you happy or upset today?”

• To an elder: “What were you thinking about today?”

No fixing. No advising.Just listening. That is enough.

Essence

• Families don’t break due to conflict alone

• They weaken due to emotional neglect

• Presence is the most affordable emotional medicine

Thought for the Day

I may be busy, but I will not be emotionally absent.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 89 |  | When Doing the Right Thing Still Brings Pain | 
Mahabharata says:

धर्मो रक्षति रक्षितः

Dharmo rakshati rakshitah

Means Dharma protects those who protect it though not always immediately.

Yudhishthira was honest.

Truthful. Committed to dharma.Yet he lost his kingdom, he lost his freedom, he watched his wife humiliated

If anyone could ask, “Why me?”. It was him. Yudhishthira’s suffering was not due to wrongdoing. It was due to dharma being tested, not rewarded

From life perspective, You act fairly. You don’t manipulate.You try to stay ethical. Still you get sidelined, others take advantage, life feels unjust. The mind wonders, “Is being good even worth it?”

The Inner Conflict !!

The heart feels tired. The mind feels cheated. Faith begins to crack. This is where many abandon dharma.

Insight

Dharma does not promise comfort. It promises inner protection. Yudhishthira did not escape pain , but pain never destroyed his integrity. What he protected in suffering

later became his strength.

Essence

• Dharma is not a shield from difficulty

• It is a shield from inner collapse

• External loss is temporary; inner loss is permanent

Useful tip

When life feels unfair today, ask, “What inside me must remain untouched by this?”. Protect that.

Thought for the Day

Even if life tests me, I will not abandon my values.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 88 |  | When You Give Your Best and Still Feel Unseen | 
Ramayana says:

अकर्तव्यं न कर्तव्यं धर्मस्य पन्थानमास्थितः

Akartavyam na kartavyam dharmasya panthanam aasthitah

Meaning: One must walk the path of dharma, even when one’s role is unnoticed or unrewarded.

Lakshmana followed Rama into the forest. No throne.No recognition. No applause. He slept less. Stood guard every night. Protected silently.

History remembers Rama’s exile. Very few remember Lakshmana’s sacrifice. Yet Lakshmana never asked:” Why me?”………”Why not my comfort?”…….”Why is my effort invisible?”. He chose duty over acknowledgment.

At home, You manage everything.Nobody notices until something goes wrong.

At work, You carry responsibility.Credit goes elsewhere.

Inside, a quiet pain forms: “I give so much… why does no one see it?”. This pain is heavier than workload. It is the pain of invisibility.

The mind asks:

• “Should I stop caring?”

• ”Should I withdraw?”

• ”Is my effort pointless?”

Dharma gives a startling answer. Your worth is not decided by who notices you. It is decided by who you become through your actions.

Lakshmana’s strength was not recognition. It was inner alignment.What is done with clarity strengthens the self

even if the world stays silent.

Essence

• Recognition feeds ego; duty builds character

• Silent effort purifies the mind

• Inner dignity is greater than external praise

One-Minute Practice

Today, if your effort goes unnoticed:Pause and ask, “Did this action make me smaller or stronger inside?” If it made you stronger, it was never wasted.

Thought for the Day

I do not need applause to stand tall.My integrity is enough.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 87 |  | Garbhadhana, Preparing for Parenthood | 
Dharmashastra says:

यतः सर्गो भवति सन्ततिरेव सृष्टे:।

सुतं जनयेत्पूर्वकृतं धर्मं पालनं च।

Yatah sargo bhavati santatireva srishthe:

Sutam janayet poorvakritam dharmam palanam cha.

Meaning: The birth of a child is not merely biological; it arises from conscious intention, ethical conduct, and preparation. Parents have the duty to create the right environment for nurturing body, mind, and values.

A struggle many modern couples silently face is how to consciously prepare for parenthood:

• “Are we ready emotionally and mentally?”

• ”How can we raise a child with the right values in a fast-paced, material world?”

• ”I want to give them health, education, and good habits ,but where do I start?”

Dharma Shastra teaches: parenthood is not accidental; it is a responsibility that begins even before conception. Thoughtful preparation shapes the child’s life and family harmony.

(1) A young couple plans to start a family. They focus only on the medical and financial aspects like doctor visits, savings, and home arrangements.

Months later, they realise Emotional readiness was lacking. Arguments and stress affected the mother’s well-being. Their child was affected by the tense environment even in the womb

Dharma perspective: preparing emotionally, spiritually, and morally ensures not just a healthy child, but a harmonious family environment.

(2) Another couple focuses on nutrition, exercise, and meditation before conception. They consciously cultivate patience, gratitude, and mindfulness. Pregnancy is smoother. Stress levels are lower. They naturally pass on positive habits and resilience to the child

This illustrates the practical wisdom of Garbhadhana Samskara: the child inherits not just genetics, but the mental and emotional climate of the parents.

Insight

• Parenthood begins before conception; it is a duty of intention and preparation

• Health, mindset, and values of parents directly influence the child

• Dharma emphasizes holistic preparation : physical, emotional, and moral

Essence

• Children inherit the environment and discipline created by parents

• Thoughtful preparation is a gift to both the child and the family

• Conscious parenting starts with awareness and intention

Useful Tip

Today, if planning for parenthood:

• Reflect on physical, mental, and emotional readiness

• Discuss values, habits, and environment you wish to create

• Even small steps like mindfulness, healthy routines, and patience are powerful preparation

Thought for the Day

Parenthood is a conscious journey; preparing with intention shapes a child’s life and family harmony.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏
