• the Epic and Samskara series, drawing wisdom from our cultural and spiritual heritage, and

• the “Inside Us” series offering gentle insights into emotions and relationships from psychological wisdom and lived experience. Kindly stay tuned 🙏

@@ 86 |  | When the Mind Collapses Under Pressure | 
Bhagavad Gita says:

क्लैब्यं मा स्म गमः पार्थ

Klaibyam ma sma gamah Partha

Meaning: Do not surrender to inner weakness in moments of crisis.

Arjuna stood between two armies. Weapons ready. Everyone watching. Yet his hands trembled. His mouth dried. His mind collapsed. This was not fear of battle. It was emotional overload.

He thought:

• “If I move forward, I hurt others.”

• ”If I step back, I betray myself.”

• ”No choice feels right.”

PLEASE NOTE : This is not ancient. This is us.

You sit at your desk. Notifications pile up. Expectations pull you in opposite directions. At home, you are needed. At work, you are demanded. Inside, you are exhausted.

You are not lazy. You are not incapable. You are emotionally overloaded just like Arjuna.

However, Krishna did not say: “Be positive.”………”Be strong.”….. Try harder.” He said something radical, “Your weakness is not who you are.”

The moment Arjuna stopped identifying with his collapse, clarity began.

Insight

Breakdown is not failure. It is a signal that the mind has reached its limit.

Essence

Feeling weak does not mean you are weak. It means you have been carrying too much for too long. When the mind collapses, it is asking for clarity and not punishment.

Strength returns the moment you stop judging yourself and start listening inward.

Useful Tip

When you feel overwhelmed today:

• Pause for one full breath.

• Say silently: “This feeling is passing through me. It is not me.”

• Take one small right step, not the whole solution.

That is enough for now.

Thought for the Day

When my mind feels weak, I will not abandon myself. I will pause, regain clarity, and move forward gently.

Dhanyosmi 🙏

@@ 85 |  | Isha Upanishad says | 
Isha Upanishad says;

तेन त्यक्तेन भुञ्जीथा

Tena Tyaktena Bhunjithaa

Meaning: Enjoy life by letting go and not by holding tightly.

Constant stress from over-attachment to money, status, outcomes, people, or expectations, Enjoyment disappears because the mind is always afraid of loss.

A Gentle Message from the Upanishads

Many people believe enjoyment comes from having more. But experience shows that enjoyment disappears when fear of loss increases.

This Mahavaakya teaches a simple truth. Letting go mentally allows you to enjoy what you already have.

(1) A professional reached a good position but could never relax. Even vacations were filled with work calls and anxiety.

When he consciously stopped checking emails after work hours, his productivity improved and family time became meaningful. Enjoyment returned when gripping reduced.

(2) A person expected constant attention and validation from a loved one. Small delays or silence caused anxiety.

When expectations were loosened and trust increased,

the relationship felt lighter and warmer.

Insight

• Hold sand tightly, it slips away.

• Hold it gently, it stays.

Life responds the same way.

Essence

• Enjoyment and attachment do not grow together.

• Letting go does not mean neglect , it means trust

• Peaceful enjoyment comes from mental freedom.

Useful Tip

• Identify one area where you are gripping too tightly.

• Loosen expectations slightly and observe the relief.

Thought for the Day

I enjoy life more when I hold it gently.

Meaning: Inner strength and clarity come through sincerity and steady effort, not shortcuts.

Frustration and disappointment arising from

• “I am doing so much, but nothing is changing.”

• ”Others progress faster; why not me?”

We get Temptation to take shortcuts in life.

Growth is rarely dramatic. It is slow, steady, and often invisible.

This Mahavaakya reminds us: Real transformation comes from consistent, honest effort, not instant results.

(1) A professional felt stuck while peers moved ahead quickly. Promotions were slow; recognition was delayed. Instead of quitting or cutting corners, he focused on improving skills and maintaining integrity. Years later, when responsibility came, he was ready and respected.

(2) A person working on emotional healing felt discouraged. Despite therapy and self-work, old patterns kept resurfacing. Over time, small changes appeared with calmer responses, better boundaries, less self-blame. Healing had been happening quietly all along.

Insight

• A plant grows underground first before appearing above the soil.

• No amount of pulling makes it grow faster.

Essence

• Honest effort builds lasting strength.

• Slow progress is still progress.

• Shortcuts weaken foundations.

Useful Tip

• Focus on showing up daily, not on results.

• Ask: “Did I act sincerely today?” That is enough.

Thought for the Day

Steady effort will take me forward.

This Mahavaakya is not moral pressure it is a reminder that integrity simplifies life.

(1) A professional was asked to slightly misrepresent facts to make a report look better. Outwardly, nothing seemed wrong. But inside, there was constant unease.

Later, he chose to speak honestly, calmly and respectfully.The fear passed, and self-respect returned. Peace came not from approval, but from alignment.

(2) A person kept agreeing to family expectations to avoid conflict, while feeling resentful inside.

One day, they spoke honestly, not harshly about their limits. The conversation was uncomfortable, but clarity replaced resentment. Relationships became lighter.

Essence

• Integrity builds quiet confidence.

• Living honestly does not always make life easy ,but it makes it peaceful.

@@ 82 |  | Brhadaaranyaka Upanishad says |

Meaning: I am not just my body, thoughts, emotions, or roles ; I am more than all of these.

Feeling over-identified with roles and labels :

• “I am only my job.”

• ”I am only my past.”

Losing peace when any role is shaken.

A Gentle but Powerful Message from the Upanishads

Most suffering comes from confusing what we experience with who we are. This Mahavaakya teaches a simple inner skill: Learn to separate yourself from temporary labels.

(1) After retirement, a senior professional felt lost. For decades, he was known by his powerful designation. Suddenly, that label disappeared.

Slowly, through teaching, mentoring, and music, he realized: “I was never just my title. That was only one role I played.” Peace returned when identity expanded beyond one label.

(2) A person going through a difficult phase kept saying: “I am a failure.” Over time, with reflection, one truth became clear: I experienced failure ; I did not become failure.” That one shift reduced shame and restored self-respect.

Insight

• Clothes change, but the person wearing them remains the same.

• Roles, emotions, and situations change but you remain.

Essence

• You are more than your roles, emotions, and experiences.

• Detaching from labels reduces fear and anxiety.

• Inner freedom begins when identity becomes flexible.

Useful Tip

Thought for the Day

@@ 80 |  | Kaṭha Upanishad says | 
Kaṭha Upanishad says:

उत्तिष्ठत जाग्रत प्राप्य वरान्निबोधत

Uttiṣṭhata Jaagrata Praapya Varaan Nibodhata

Meaning: Wake up. Be alert. Take responsibility for your own growth.

Feeling stuck, lazy, or directionless,

• “I know what is right, but I don’t act.”

• ”I’ll start tomorrow.”

• ”Life is just passing me by.”

Life does not transform merely because we understand truth. Transformation begins only when awareness becomes lived action.

This mahavaakya is not a command ; it is a loving wake-up call. It is like a compassionate teacher gently shaking your shoulders and saying: “You are capable ; don’t sleep through the miracle of your own life.”

(1) A man ignored early health warnings ; constant fatigue, rising weight, and high sugar levels. Each day his body whispered, then spoke, then shouted. Still, he comforted himself: “I’ll take care later.”

One routine medical report landed like lightning, not to frighten him, but to awaken him.He began small: a morning walk, mindful meals, regular check-ups, slower breathing.

Within months, his eyes grew brighter, his steps lighter, his spirit steadier.

What truly changed?

Not knowledge but alert action.

(2) A professional felt stagnant for years, trapped in complaints about her boss, her company, and her age.She carried frustration like a heavy suitcase everywhere she went.

One quiet evening, instead of blaming the world, she chose courage. She enrolled in a short course, updated her skills, and reached out to old colleagues.

Doors did not swing open overnight , but small windows began to appear. Slowly, possibilities replaced helplessness.

What changed?

She stopped waiting and claimed responsibility for her destiny.

Insight

• An alarm clock ringing is useless unless you rise from the bed.

• Awareness without action is like hearing the alarm ; and hitting snooze on your own life.

Essence

• Life responds to alertness, not excuses.

• Small, conscious action builds powerful momentum.

• Waiting for perfect conditions keeps growth permanently “tomorrow.”

Useful Tip

• Identify one small action you have been postponing.

• Do it today , not perfectly, just sincerely.

Thought for the Day

I will act, not postpone.

@@ 79 |  | Maandukya Upanishad says | 
Maandukya Upanishad says:

अयम् आत्मा ब्रह्म

Ayam Atma Brahma

Meaning: This very self, the one living your daily life, is powerful and sacred.

Feeling unworthy, guilty, or disconnected from spirituality:

• “I am too flawed for spiritual growth.”

• ”My daily life is not spiritual.”

• ”I am not pure enough.”

Many people believe spirituality belongs only to monks, temples, or perfect people. This mahavaakya gently corrects that misunderstanding: Your ordinary life itself is sacred. Your daily self is not separate from the highest truth.

(1) A man regularly attended spiritual talks but felt guilty inside. He thought, “I get angry. I make mistakes. How can I think of myself as spiritual?”

One day, his teacher said,

“Do you stop cleaning your house because it gets dirty every day?”

That sentence changed him.

He understood: mistakes don’t make him unworthy; they make him human.

(2) A woman working long hours felt she had no “spiritual life.” No time for long prayers or retreats.

But she noticed something:

When she listened patiently, spoke honestly, and acted with care at work, people felt supported. She realized:

Spirituality was already happening through her actions.

Insight

• A muddy river is still water.

• Dust on a mirror does not destroy the mirror.

Your essence remains pure, even when life looks messy.

Essence

• You don’t need to escape life to be spiritual.

• Your daily self is not a problem; it is the starting point.

• Growth comes from acceptance, not rejection of yourself.

Useful Tip

Thought for the Day

My everyday life is sacred.

@@ 78 |  | One Life, One Strength, One Awareness, One Connection | 
Over the last four days, we reflected on the four great maha-vaakya of the Upanishads:

• Tat Tvam Asi - You are that; you belong to the whole.

• Aham Brahmasmi- Your real self is already complete and strong.

• Prajnanam Brahma - Clear awareness itself is the highest power.

• Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma - Everything is connected; nothing exists alone.

These may sound lofty, but they are meant for ordinary daily life, not only for philosophers.

Today, let us see how all four truths naturally unfold in one simple, real family situation that everyone can relate to.

There was a middle-class family in a city.

• Father (Ravi): Corporate employee, overworked, felt small in office.

• Mother (Suma): Managing home and children, felt invisible.

• Son (Arjun): College student, often angry and stressed.

• Grandfather: Retired, felt lonely and useless.

One difficult week

1. Ravi’s project was criticized in office.He came home thinking, “I am replaceable. I don’t really matter.”

2. Suma was exhausted managing everything. She felt, “Nobody notices my effort.”

3. Arjun got poor marks and reacted angrily at home.

4. Grandfather sat alone in his room, feeling unwanted.

That evening, a small crisis happened.Grandfather had sudden chest discomfort. The whole family rushed to the hospital. In that moment, something changed.

What happened inside them ?

1. Tat Tvam Asi — “You are part of the whole.”

At the hospital, Ravi suddenly realized. If I was really “insignificant,” why did my family panic when I wasn’t there? Why did my office call me? Why did my son need me?. He understood that Even if I feel small, my presence matters to many people.

Tat Tvam Asi in real life:

You may feel tiny ; but you are part of a larger human network.

2. Aham Brahmasmi - “Inner strength is already within.”

That night, Suma managed hospital paperwork, medicines, doctor talks, and family emotions , all calmly.Later she thought: “If I was truly weak, I would have broken today.”

She didn’t suddenly become strong.She recognized the strength already working inside her.

Aham Brahmasmi in real life:

You are stronger than you think ; your life itself proves it.

3. Prajnanam Brahma - “Awareness is the highest power.”

Arjun felt like shouting at everyone in panic.But seeing his grandfather ill, he paused. Instead of arguing, he asked calmly: “Doctor, what exactly should we do?”. That single pause prevented confusion and chaos.

Prajnanam Brahma in real life:

When you pause before reacting, clarity replaces anger.

4. Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma - “Everything is connected.”

In the hospital waiting room, strangers helped them ; a nurse brought water, another family shared seats.Ravi realized that We are not alone. Even strangers are connected in human care. Later, when grandfather recovered, he said softly, “Today I felt loved again.”

Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma in real life: Connection exists everywhere ; we only need to notice it.

What this means for daily life ?

From one real situation, all four truths appeared naturally:

1. Tat Tvam Asi - You matter more than you think.

2. Aham Brahmasmi - Strength is already inside you.

3. Prajnanam Brahma - Pause brings clarity.

4. Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma - You are never truly alone.

One practical daily habit

When stress comes:

Pause - Breathe - Remember- Connect

Silently say:

I belong. I am strong. I will see clearly. I am connected.

Thought for the Day

I belong to life, I trust my inner strength, I act with awareness, and I live in connection with all.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 77 |  | Chāndogya Upanishad says | 
Chāndogya Upanishad says:

सर्वं खल्विदं ब्रह्म

Sarvam khalvidam Brahma

Everything here is Brahman. Everything is connected. Nothing exists alone.

Sometimes we feel lonely. We feel, “I am facing everything alone.” We feel, “No one understands me.”

The Upanishads speak softly to us and say:

You are not separate from life. You are part of life itself. Loneliness does not always come because people are absent. It comes when we forget our connection with the world around us

The Upanishads do not scold us. They do not say, “Don’t feel lonely.” They simply remind us: Just like a wave belongs to the ocean, you belong to life. When we remember this, the heart becomes lighter.

(1) After retirement, a senior professional felt empty. No office calls. No meetings.No appreciation. Days felt long and silent.

One day, he started visiting a nearby school.He helped children read simple stories. Slowly,Children smiled at him. Conversations returned. His heart felt useful again

What changed?

Not the world.His connection with life returned.

(2) A woman lived in a busy apartment complex.Hundreds of people… yet she felt lonely. One morning, she did something small. She smiled at the security guard. She spoke to the lift operator. She called the vegetable vendor by name. Day by day, warmth grew.

What changed?

She realized that Connection was already there. She just had to notice it.

Insight

A mobile phone without a network feels useless. Once connected, even a simple phone becomes powerful. Human beings are the same. Connection gives meaning to life.

Essence

You are not alone, even when you feel lonely. Connection exists everywhere in people, nature, work, and service. When we recognise connection, fear and isolation reduce

Useful tip

• Each day, connect consciously with one person or one activity

• A smile, a greeting, or a small act of help is enough.

• Small connections create big warmth.

Thought for the Day

I am connected to life.

Dhanyosmi 👏🙏

@@ 76 |  | Mahavaakya from Aitareya Upanishad says | 
Mahavaakya from Aitareya Upanishad says:

प्रज्ञानं ब्रह्म

(Prajnanam Brahma)

Means “Clear awareness itself is the highest power.”

Not muscle power.Not speed. Not sharp talking.

The power to see clearly what is happening ; that itself is Brahman

Many of us feel: “My mind is overloaded.”………”I react emotionally and regret later.”……. “I know what is right, but I don’t do it.”……..”My mind never feels calm.”

The Upanishads say something very comforting. Life hurts us not because problems exist, but because we react without clarity. When awareness is absent, emotions take over, words come out harsh, decisions go wrong

Prajnanam Brahma reminds us: When awareness is present, even a difficult situation becomes manageable.

(1) A manager received a harsh email late at night. His first urge was to reply immediately which would have been angry and defensive. Then he remembered: “Let me first see clearly.” He paused. Next morning, he re-read the email calmly.

He understood: The anger was not personal. The client was under pressure. He replied with clarity, not emotion.The issue was resolved peacefully.

What worked here?

Not high IQ. Not authority. Prajnanam - clear awareness before action.

(2) During a family conversation, an elderly parent made a sharp remark. The immediate thought: “Why are they always criticizing me. Instead of reacting, the person paused and observed: The words were harsh. But the emotion behind them was fear and insecurity. He responded gently instead of arguing. The tension dissolved.

What changed?

Nothing outside. Only awareness replaced blind reaction. That awareness itself is Prajnanam Brahma.

Essence / Key Takeaways

• Awareness is greater than intelligence

• Clarity saves us from regret

• Peace is not created ; it appears naturally when awareness is present

That awareness is Brahman, the highest strength in life.

Useful Daily Practice

Before reacting: Pause for three deep breaths Ask, “What is really happening here?”. Respond only after the mind becomes clear. This small pause is Prajnanam in action.

Thought for the Day

When awareness rises, peace follows. Prajnanam Brahma - awareness is my greatest power.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 75 |  | Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upanishad says | 
Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upanishad says:

अहं ब्रह्मास्मि (Aham brahmasmi)

Means “My real ‘I’ is complete, whole, and powerful ; not limited by fear, weakness, or situations.”

In daily life, we often hear people say: “I am not good enough”, “Others are better than me”, “I cannot handle this”.

These thoughts come when we identify only with our role, body, mind, or temporary failures.

This mahavaakya does not teach arrogance. It reminds you of a deeper truth you often forget: You are not just your thoughts, emotions, mistakes, or past failures. Behind all of them is a steady awareness that has already seen many ups and downs and remained intact. That inner awareness itself is strength.

(1) A senior professional was asked to lead a critical meeting after many years of staying in the background. His first thought was, “I am not as sharp as others anymore.”

But once the discussion started, his experience, calmness, and clarity guided the team through a tough decision. Younger colleagues later told him, “Your presence changed the whole meeting.”

What changed? Nothing outside. He stopped identifying himself only with comparison and doubt, and allowed his inner clarity to function.

(2) A woman went through a difficult phase with health issues, family pressure, and emotional exhaustion. She kept thinking, “I cannot handle this.”

One day, after managing yet another hospital visit, paperwork, and family responsibility, she realized: “If I was truly weak, I would have broken by now.”

She did not become stronger overnight. She simply recognized the strength that had been quietly operating all along.

Essence / Key Takeaways

• Self-doubt comes from forgetting your deeper, steady self.

• Confidence is not becoming someone new; it is remembering who you already are.

• You have faced difficult days before, and the same inner strength is available today.

Useful Tip

• When self-doubt arises, recall one situation you handled despite fear or uncertainty.

• Silently remind yourself: “ I am more than my thoughts. Strength is already within me.”

Thought for the Day

I am grounded in inner strength and awareness.

@@ 74 |  | Chandogya upanishad says | 
Chandogya upanishad says:

तत् त्वम् असि (Tat Tvam Asi)

Tat Tvam Asi - “You are that. You are part of everything.”

Many of us feel small, powerless, overwhelmed, or insignificant in life i.e., at work, in family, or in society.

Even the greatest sages remind us: your value is not measured by size, title, or achievements. Even when you feel powerless, you are connected to the bigger whole, and your presence matters more than you think.

(1) Ramesh is a team leader in a corporate office. Deadlines are tight, emails keep piling up, and he feels overwhelmed and small. He thinks: “I am just one person ; how can I make a difference?”

One day, a small suggestion he made prevented a project mistake. The team met the deadline, the client was happy, and his contribution made a real impact.

Lesson : Sometimes your small actions ripple outwards, affecting the whole. You may feel insignificant, but you are part of something bigger.

(2) Anita is a mother managing work and home. She often feels stressed and invisible. One evening, her teenage son struggled with a homework problem. She sat down patiently and explained it. He solved it successfully and felt confident. Anita realized: her calm presence and guidance had made a real difference.

Lesson: Even simple support or presence in everyday life matters deeply. You are not “small”; your influence is significant.

Insight

• A drop of water in the river may feel tiny, but it is made of the same water as the river.

• A leaf on a tree may flutter alone, but it is connected to the strong trunk and roots.

Essence / Key Takeaways

• Feeling small or overwhelmed is natural; remember your connection to life and others.

• Your small actions ripple outwards and can create big change.

• Awareness of your role in the bigger picture reduces stress, fear, and self-doubt.

Useful Tip

• When life feels heavy → pause → remind yourself: “I am connected. I belong. My actions matter.”

• Acknowledge one small positive action today, no matter how minor.

Thought for the Day

I belong. I make a difference.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 73 |  | Bhaja Govindam – The Essence of All the Verses | 
Adi Shankaracharya did not write Bhaja Govindam to scare us. He wrote it to wake us up gently like a loving alarm clock, not a fire siren .

The core reminder of all 31 verses is this:

“You are not here to accumulate things.You are here to awaken.”

REMEMBER THE POWERFUL ‘mantra’ : G.O.V.I.N.D.A

This is the 31 verses takeaway in one sacred word:

G – Go inward daily

Don’t outsource your peace to people, salary, children, or WhatsApp messages. Sit quietly for a few minutes every day.

O – Observe the mind

Watch thoughts like traffic signals. You don’t have to jump at every green light the mind shows.

V – Vanish attachment

Use the world, don’t let the world use you. Enjoy relationships but don’t chain your happiness to them.

I – Invoke the Divine Name

Chanting is not for God’s benefit.It is mental hygiene like brushing your teeth, but for the mind.

N – Neutralise desire gently

Desires are not enemies.

But don’t hand them the steering wheel of your life.

D – Do your duty cheerfully

Work sincerely, serve lovingly, act responsibly without ego, without anxiety.

A – Awaken to who you truly are

You are not the body that will age.You are not the mind that worries. You are the unchanging witness.

Bhaja Govindam = Live G.O.V.I.N.D.A every day

Life is like a typical Indian morning at home.

• The alarm rings ; you press snooze.

• It rings again ; five more minutes.

• Suddenly it’s late, the tea is cold, the bus/train/meeting is waiting, and the mind is already irritated.

Bhaja Govindam says: “Life’s alarm has been ringing since youth. Old age is not a new alarm ; it is the final reminder.”

How we actually live:

• In youth: “Let me enjoy first.”

• In middle age: “Too many responsibilities.”

• In old age: “If only I had time and health.”

Shankaracharya smiles and says gently, “You never lacked time. You only kept pressing snooze.”

Life is like a mobile phone

• When battery is full → we waste it on reels, gossip, overthinking

• When battery is at 5% → suddenly we panic and search for a charger

Shankaracharya says, “Chant when the battery is full,

not when the phone is about to switch off.”

HOW THIS TIES ALL 31 VERSES TOGETHER

• Youth, beauty, wealth, relations = temporary battery charge

• Desire, anger, pride = background apps draining energy

• Chanting, reflection, service = power-saving mode

• Self-knowledge = knowing you are not the phone, but the user

Don’t live life on snooze mode.

Wake up now while the body is strong and the mind is clear.

Old age does not bring wisdom automatically. Awareness does. And awareness can begin today ,even before your morning tea.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 72 | Bhajagovindam – Verse 31 | मूढः कश्चिन् वैयाकरणो | 
डुकृङ्करणाध्ययन धुरीणः ।

श्रीमच्छङ्करभगवच्छिष्यैः

बोधित आसीत् शोधनकरणैः ॥ 31॥

Moodhah kashchin vaiyakaranah

Dukrinkaranadhyayana dhurinah

Shrimachchankara bhagavat shishyaih

Bodhita asit shodhana karanaih

Shankaracharya points to a real situation:

There was a man deeply learned in Sanskrit grammar. He spent his entire life mastering complex rules like “Ḍukrn” . He was respected for his intelligence. But his mind was restless and his heart was empty. He knew many rules; but he did not know himself.

Seeing this, Shankaracharya’s disciples gently corrected him

and showed him the higher truth.

• Learning is valuable

• But without inner awareness and devotion, it remains incomplete

Raju worked in a big city office. He was famous for his intelligence. He knew all company policies. He knew every shortcut in Excel. He knew how to impress seniors. He even corrected others’ mistakes. People said, “Ask Raju; he knows everything.”

But every evening, He returned home irritated. He snapped at family. He lay awake worrying. His mind never rested

One day, while returning late from work, his phone battery died. He stood on the road, confused. A small tea shop owner asked, “Sir, where is your home?”

Raju froze. He suddenly realised; He knew office systems, but at that moment, he couldn’t even find his way home. That night, the thought struck him deeply: “I know so much…but I don’t know how to be peaceful.”

From the next day, He sat quietly for a few minutes daily. He reduced unnecessary arguments. He worked the same job, but with awareness

Nothing outside changed. But inside, something softened.

Just like the grammar scholar in this verse, Raju realised:

• Knowledge can make you efficient

• But only self-awareness makes you peaceful

Essence

Shankaracharya is not against learning. He gently reminds us: You may have degrees; You may have titles; You may have sharp intellect

But if you do not understand: why you get angry ; why fear controls you; why desire never ends; why the mind keeps running, then true wisdom has not yet begun.

Real wisdom is not information.

It is inner clarity.

Useful tip

Today, pause once and ask honestly: “I know many things…but do I know my own mind?”

One-minute practice:

1. Sit quietly

2. Take one slow breath

3. Say gently inside: “I am more than my thoughts.Peace is already within me.”

This one minute cleans the mind more than many books.

Thought for the Day

Information sharpens the mind. Self-knowledge softens the heart.

Dhanyosmi 😊🙏

@@ 71 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 30 | तुम्बुरु नारद के गाना में | 
शिव शंकर का स्मरण में ।

भव भय हारक नाम जपो रे

भज गोविन्दम भज गोविन्दम ॥

Tumburu Narada ke gana mein

Shiva Shankara ka smarana mein

Bhava bhaya haraka nama japo re

Bhaja Govindam Bhaja Govindam

Adi Shankaracharya now speaks like a caring elder holding your hand. He says:

Even the greatest sages like Narada and Tumburu spend their lives singing God’s name

Why?

Because God’s name Removes fear, Gives courage, Brings peace to a restless heart. When life becomes heavy, don’t think too much, just chant.

One night, there was a sudden power cut. No lights. No fan. No mobile signal. Complete darkness

A small child started crying. The mother pulled the child close and softly said: “Don’t be afraid.” Then she began chanting quietly, “Govinda… Govinda…”

The child slowly stopped crying. His breathing became normal.He fell asleep in her lap. The darkness did not go. The power did not return. But fear disappeared.

Shankaracharya says:

God’s name does not remove darkness outside first. It removes darkness inside. And once fear is gone, everything becomes manageable.

An old man walks with a stick. The stick does not remove stones from the road. The stick does not stop the rain. The stick does not shorten the journey. But with the stick, He feels confident. He feels steady. He does not fall easily

God’s name is that walking stick for life. Life will still have Problems, Loss, Uncertainty. But you will not collapse under them.

Essence

• Fear comes from feeling alone; Chanting God’s name removes loneliness

• When the name flows on the tongue, the heart remembers, I am not alone

You don’t need Deep philosophy; Long rituals; Perfect conditions. Just need sincerity.

Useful tip

• When fear comes → chant once

• When worry comes → chant once

• When confusion comes → chant once

• When courage is needed → chant louder

No rules. No qualification. No preparation.Just call out.

Thought for the Day

God’s name does not change the storm,it changes the heart that walks through it.

Dhanyosmi 😊🙏

@@ 70 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 29 | गुरुचरणाम्बुज निर्भर भक्तः | 
संसारादचिराद्भव मुक्तः ।

सेन्द्रियमानस नियमादेवं

द्रक्ष्यसि निज हृदयस्थं देवम् ॥

Guru charanambuja nirbhara bhaktah

Samsarad achirad bhava muktah

Sendriya manasa niyamad evam

Drakshyasi nija hridayastham devam

Shankaracharya gives a quiet but powerful assurance:

• Hold firmly to the Guru’s guidance

• Discipline your senses and mind gently

• Don’t run wildly behind the world

Then something magical happens:

• You stop searching outside

• You see God inside your own heart

Liberation is not far away. It is closer than your breath.

In a house, there was a small locked room. The owner kept searching everywhere:In temples, In pilgrimages, In books, In rituals

One day, an elderly visitor asked: “Have you checked that locked room in your own house?” The owner replied: “Oh, that room?. The key has always been with me. I never bothered to open it.” They opened it. Inside was: Light, Fresh air, Calm silence.

Shankaracharya says:

• The Guru does not bring God to you

• The Guru only says: “Open what is already yours.”

Discipline is the key. Devotion is the courage to turn it.

A radio station is broadcasting all the time. If the radio is Uncontrolled, Noisy, Mis-tuned, you hear only noise. The signal was never missing. The tuning was.

Shankaracharya says:

Guru gives the right frequency

Discipline tunes the senses. Calm mind removes disturbance Then suddenly…You hear the divine music from within

Essence

• Guru bhakti gives direction

• Discipline gives stability

• Sense control prevents leakage of energy

• Mind control brings clarity

God is not hiding. Our restlessness is hiding Him. When the mind becomes quiet,

the heart reveals its secret.

Useful tip

No effort. No struggle. Just sincerity. Today, practice:

• One act of obedience to a good teaching

• One moment of sense control

• One minute of quiet remembrance

That is enough to begin inner freedom.

Thought for the Day

The Guru shows the door, discipline opens it, and God is found waiting inside.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 69 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 28 | प्राणायामं प्रत्याहारं | 
नित्यानित्य विवेक विचारम् ।

जाप्यसमेत समाधिविधानं

कुर्ववधानं महदवधानम् ॥

Pranayamam pratyaharam

Nityanitya viveka vicharam

Japyasameta samadhi vidhanam

Kurvavadhanam mahad avadhanam

Shankaracharya now becomes like a loving life coach.

He says:

• Don’t wait for problems to explode.

• Train your mind daily, just like your body.

How?

• Breathe slowly

• Pull the mind back from useless distractions

• Know clearly what is permanent and what is temporary

• Chant God’s name

• Sit quietly with full attention

This is not for monks only. This is mental hygiene for everyone.

A man proudly said, “My car runs fine. I never service it.”

Months passed.

One day, in heavy traffic. Engine overheated. Smoke came out. Car stopped suddenly

The mechanic later said, “The problem didn’t come today.

It was building quietly every day.”

Shankaracharya says:

The mind is the same.

• Small stress ignored

• Small anger ignored

• Small restlessness ignored

One day, it bursts as

• Anxiety

• Depression

• Burnout

• Broken relationships

Daily spiritual practice is preventive maintenance, not emergency repair.

Every house has a water tank on the terrace.

If you don’t clean it, Water still comes, But slowly it becomes dirty. Smell develops. Health suffers

Mind is your inner water tank.

Breathing cleans it. Withdrawal from distractions protects it. Discrimination filters it. Chanting purifies it.

Neglect it and life suffers quietly.

Essence

• Pranayama calms the nervous system

• Pratyahara stops mental leakage

• Viveka gives clarity in decisions

• Japa and meditation anchor you inside

Do this before life shakes you.

Spirituality is not emergency medicine. It is daily nutrition.

Thought for the Day

Neglect the mind and it breaks you.Train the mind and it carries you.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 68 | Bhaja Govindam - Verse 27 | गेयं गीता नाम सहस्रं | 
ध्येयं श्रीपति रूपमजस्रम् ।

नेयं सज्जनसंगे चित्तं

देयं दीनजनाय च वित्तम् ॥

Geyam Gita nama sahasram

Dhyeyam Shripati rupam ajasram

Neyam sajjana sange chittam

Deyam dinajanaya cha vittam

Shankaracharya now becomes very practical. He tells us how to live daily life wisely:

• Let your mouth chant God’s name.

• Let your mind remember God’s form.

• Let your company be good people.

• Let your money help those in need

This is not renunciation.This is right living.

In every house, there is a switchboard.

• One plug goes to: TV (noise)

• One to mobile charger (distraction)

• One to mixer (restlessness)

Everything is plugged in. One day, the electrician said, “Sir, current is fine. But wrong devices are drawing all the power.”

Shankaracharya says:

Your life energy is limited. Where are you plugging it ?. Mouth plugged into complaints?. Mind plugged into worries?. Company plugged into negativity?. Money plugged into ego?

Change the plugs; life will glow differently.

Every week your phone shows:

“Screen Time Report” It tells you: Hours wasted, Apps overused, Energy drained

Imagine a life report:

• How much time went into God’s name?

• How much time went into noble thoughts?

• How much time went into good company?

• How much wealth eased someone’s pain?

Shankaracharya gives the ideal life dashboard in one verse.

Essence

• Chanting purifies speech

• Meditation steadies the mind

• Good company protects values

• Charity melts ego

These four together:

• Clean the inside

• Simplify the outside

• Create quiet joy

Spiritual life is not escape. It is alignment.

Thought for the Day

Sing to clean the tongue,

remember to calm the mind,

choose people wisely,

and let money serve love.

Dhanyosmi 😊🙏

@@ 67 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 26 | कामं क्रोधं लोभं मोहं | 
त्यक्त्वात्मानं पश्यति सोऽहम् ।

आत्मज्ञानविहीना मूढाः

ते पच्यन्ते नरकनिगूढाः ॥

Kamam krodham lobham moham

Tyaktvatmanam pashyati soham

Atmajnana vihina moodhah

Te pachyante naraka nigoodhah

Shankaracharya speaks without mercy, but with great compassion:

Lust, anger, greed, and confusion are not outside enemies; they are fires burning inside us.

He says, “Drop them, Step back, Look within. The moment you see who you really are, these fires lose their power”.

Hell is not somewhere else. Hell is a mind on fire.

Every day in Meena’s house: Morning rush, Late office, Angry words, Silent resentment, Night exhaustion.

Inside her, emotions kept boiling: Desire for more, Anger at family, Greed for control, Confusion about life.

One evening, the pressure cooker whistle broke. Steam burst out. Food spilled. Kitchen filled with smoke.

Her mother calmly said: “The cooker is fine. The pressure had no outlet.”

That night, Meena realised: “My life is the same cooker”.

Shankaracharya says:

• Desires build pressure

• Anger increases heat

• Greed locks the lid

• Ignorance removes the whistle

• Chanting God’s name is the safety valve.

Imagine your phone overheats. Apps running nonstop. Battery draining. Screen burning.

What do you do?

You don’t punish the phone. You close the apps.

Shankaracharya says:

• Lust is an app

• Anger is an app

• Greed is an app

• Delusion is an app

You forgot to close them. Self-knowledge is not adding more apps. It is switching off what is unnecessary.

Essence

• Desire promises happiness, but creates restlessness

• Anger promises power, but creates damage

• Greed promises security, but creates fear

• Ignorance keeps the cycle running

Without self-knowledge, a person suffers inside, even in comfort.

With self-knowledge, even simple life feels free.

Useful tip

• Observe one strong desire today ; don’t act immediately.

• When anger rises, pause and chant God’s name once.

• Reduce mental clutter before adding spiritual practices.

• Remember, Peace comes not from getting more, but from burning less.

Thought for the Day

Desire, anger, and greed create hell inside. Self-knowledge opens the door and walks you out.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 66 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 25 | शत्रौ मित्रे पुत्रे बन्धौ | 
मा कुरु यत्नं विग्रह सन्धौ ।

सर्वस्मिन्नपि पश्यात्मानं

सर्वत्रोत्सृज भेदाज्ञानम् ॥

Shatrau mitre putre bandhau

Ma kuru yatnam vigraha sandhau

Sarvasminn api pashyatmanam

Sarvatra utsruja bhedajnnam

Shankaracharya gives a direct warning:

Do not waste your life energy fighting Enemies, Friends, Children, Relatives.

He says: Today’s enemy can become tomorrow’s friend. Today’s loved one can become tomorrow’s conflict

If your peace depends on people behaving as you wish,

you will never be peaceful.

Two brothers lived in the same house. One argument started over: “Who should pay the electricity bill?” . Voices rose. Days passed.Egos hardened. They stopped speaking. They avoided eye contact. Tension filled the house.

One night, a short circuit caused a fire. Neighbours rushed. Fire was controlled. Standing outside, watching smoke rise, one brother whispered: We fought over bills… and almost lost the house.”

Shankaracharya asks, “How many homes, hearts, and lives

are burning because of small differences?

Imagine your right hand fighting your left hand. Right hand says: “I am right!” Left hand says: “I am right!. Both belong to the same body. Who is foolish?

Shankaracharya says: Seeing others as separate from you

is ignorance. When the body fights itself, it suffers.

Essence

Most conflicts are born from ego, not truth. You fight because you think: “I am separate”……”I am right”. Spiritual wisdom is seeing: Same life, Same breath, Same Divine presence

This does not mean: You accept wrong. You tolerate injustice. It means: “You respond with clarity, not hatred.

Useful tip

• Before reacting, pause for 10 seconds. Ask yourself: “Is this worth my peace?

• Reduce one argument today deliberately.

• When anger rises, chant God’s name quietly.

• Peace is a daily practice, not a theory.

Thought for the Day

Most battles are not with others…they are with our own untrained mind.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 65 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 24 | त्वयि मयि सर्वत्रैको विष्णुः | 
व्यर्थं कुप्यसि मय्यसहिष्णुः ।

भव समचित्तः सर्वत्र

त्वं वाञ्छस्यचिराद्यदि विष्णुत्वम् ॥

Tvayi mayi sarvatraiko Vishnuh

Vyartham kupyasi mayy asahishnuh

Bhava samachittah sarvatra

Tvam vanchasy achirad yadi Vishnutvam

Shankaracharya makes a statement that shakes the ego:

The same God is in you, me, and everyone. Then why are you angry? Why the hatred, impatience, comparison, and intolerance?

If you truly want to experience God, he says, “Learn to keep your mind balanced everywhere”.

God is not somewhere else. God is right in front of you, wearing different faces.

A man once stood in front of a mirror. He was angry. He frowned ; the mirror frowned back. He shouted; the mirror shouted back. He raised his hand ; the mirror did the same.

Finally, exhausted, he smiled. The mirror smiled back instantly. Suddenly he understood: “The mirror never fought me. It only reflected what I gave.”

Shankaracharya says:- The world is a mirror of the Divine. Your anger is meeting your own reflection.

In your house: The bulb in the hall The fan in the bedroom. The fridge in the kitchen. They all look different. They do different jobs. But remove electricity, everything stops. God is that electricity.People differ. Personalities clash. Opinions fight. But the same power runs through all. Then whom are you really fighting?

Essence

• Anger does not punish the other person. it burns you.

• Intolerance comes from forgetting the Divine presence.

• Seeing God everywhere does not mean approving wrong actions. It means responding without hatred

• Equanimity is not weakness. It is spiritual strength.

Useful tip

When anger rises, pause for 5 seconds. Ask quietly: “Is the same life not breathing in them too?”

Chant God’s name when irritated , not when calm.

Practice respect even in disagreement.

This is how divinity grows inside daily life.

Thought for the Day

When you fight the world, you fight God in disguise. When you calm your mind, God reveals Himself.

Dhsnyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 64 | Bhaja Govindam - Verse 23 | कस्त्वं कोऽहं कुत आयातः का मे जननी को मे तातः । | 
इति परिभावय निज संसारं सर्वं त्यक्त्वा स्वप्न विचारम् ॥

Kas tvam ko’ham kuta ayatah

Ka me janani ko me tatah

Iti paribhavaya nija samsaram

Sarvam tyaktva swapna vicharam

Adi Shankaracharya asks questions that shake us deeply:-

Who are you really? Who am I truly? Where did I come from ? Who are my real parents?

Then he gives a stunning insight :-

This entire life that we take so seriously is like a dream we are inside. Important while it lasts… but not permanent.

At the airport immigration counter, a man panicked. The officer asked: “Who are you?”

He immediately gave His name, His nationality, His passport number. The officer stamped and returned the passport.The man walked away safely.

Later he realised: “If this small book was missing, I would be nobody here.Shankaracharya’s question goes deeper: If one booklet defines you to the world, who are you without documents, roles, labels?

In a dream, You cry, it feels real… You fear, it feels real…..You lose , it feels unbearable…..

Then the alarm rings. Suddenly, No fear……No loss…..No pain…..Life, says Shankaracharya, is similar.

Wisdom is not running away from life, it is remembering that one day we will wake up.

Essence

Most suffering comes from wrong identity .We think: “This is my body”……..”This is my role”………”This is my world”…. Reflection loosens this grip. Remembering God turns fear into clarity. You still live, love, work, but without panic.

Useful tip

When disturbed, ask: “Will this remain forever?”…….Do your duties fully, but lightly.

Sit silently for 2 minutes today. Chant God’s name and remember: I am more than what is happening to me.

Thought for the Day

Life feels solid while dreaming ; wisdom begins when you question the dream.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 63 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 22 | रथ्या चर्पट विरचित कन्थः | 
पुण्यापुण्य विवर्जित पन्थः ।

योगी योग नियोजित चित्तः

रमते बालोन्मत्तवदेव ॥ 22 ॥

Rathya charpaṭa virachita kanthaḥ

Puṇyāpuṇya vivarjita panthaḥ |

Yogī yoga niyojita chittaḥ

Ramate bālonmatta-vad eva ||

Adi Shankaracharya paints a beautiful picture of a true yogi:

Life moves like a road,sometimes smooth, sometimes broken. The yogi walks this road without getting trapped in good or bad.

His mind is firmly anchored in Yoga and devotion. Such a person lives with the carefree joy of a child ; free, playful, and contended.

When the mind is rightly engaged, life itself becomes joyful, no matter what comes.

Raju was constantly tense with office deadlines, financial worries, family pressures

His little niece Meera, however, was always laughing turning everything into a game.

One day, Raju asked helplessly: “Meera, how are you always happy? Nothing special is happening!”

She smiled and said: “I make everything a game. Even sweeping the floor is a race!”

That moment struck Raju deeply. A child does not change the situation; she changes how she engages with it.

Essence

Shankaracharya’s teaching is profound yet simple: Life will never be free from ups and downs. Peace does not come by rearranging circumstances. It comes by retraining the mind.

When the mind is absorbed in God’s Name, anchored in Dharma, softened by surrender

• Even struggles lose their weight.

• Life begins to feel like Leela; divine play.

Namasankeerthanam does exactly this; It turns seriousness into serenity, burden into bliss.

Useful tip

Anyone can live this verse: Treat life as God’s playground, not a battlefield. When stress rises, pause and chant a divine name. Do ordinary actions with remembrance, not resentment.

Cooking, cleaning, walking, let everything become worship. Joy is not in what you do, but in how the mind meets it.

Thought for the Day

Walk life’s rough roads like a child at play; chant like a yogi and the heart will smile, no matter the path.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 62 | Bhaja Govindam – Verse 21 | पुनरपि जननं पुनरपि मरणं | 
पुनरपि जननी जठरॆ शयनम् ।

इह संसारॆ बहु दुस्तारॆ

कृपयाऽपारॆ पाहि मुरारॆ ॥ 21 ॥

Punarapi jananam, punarapi maranam

Punarapi janani jathare shayanam

Iha samsare bahu dustare

Kripaya aparé pahi Muraré

Shankaracharya reminds us gently:

We are caught in a cycle:

born → struggle → worry → fall sick → die → born again.

Life keeps repeating like a loop, unless we wake up. The world is not easy; problems come one after another. Only God’s grace and remembrance can help us cross this ocean of life.

In one line:

Life is tough ; God’s compassion is the only rescue.

Ramesh bought a new washing machine. He loaded clothes, added soap, pressed Start…

It began:

Wash → Rinse → Spin → Repeat

His daughter asked: “Appa, why are the clothes going round and round again?”

Ramesh smiled: “That is how the machine works, kanna.”

Then it struck him ; his own life was like this:

Office → Stress → Sleep → Worry → Repeat

That night, he sat quietly and chanted God’s name for 5 minutes. Nothing outside changed, but inside, something slowed down.

For the first time he felt: “I am not the washing machine. I am the one watching it.”

Essence

• Life without awareness becomes a never-ending cycle. Problems don’t stop ; our reaction can.

• Remembering God does not remove duties, it removes the burden from the heart.

• God’s name is like a pause button in a fast-forward life

Useful tip

When life feels heavy or repetitive:

1. Pause for 30 seconds

2. Take a slow, deep breath

3. Chant any divine name you love (Rama, Krishna, Shiva, Govinda…)

4. Remind yourself softly: “I am not alone.”

Problems stay outside. Peace enters inside.

Thought for the day

Life may keep repeating its cycle, but God’s name helps you step out of the spin.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 61 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 20 | भगवद्गीता किञ्चिदधीता | 
गङ्गा जललव कणिका पीता ।

सकृदपि यॆन मुरारी समर्चा

क्रियतॆ तस्य यमॆन न चर्चा ॥ 20 ॥

Bhagavad Gita Kinchid Adhita Ganga Jalala Lavan Kanika Peeta

Sakridaapi Yen Murari Samarcha

Kriyate Tasyam Yamena Na Charcha

Shankaracharya says:

A person may have read the Bhagavad Gita once, or sipped holy Ganga water, But if they never worship Lord Krishna (Murari) with devotion, All their rituals and knowledge cannot protect them from the cycle of birth and death.

In short,Knowledge and rituals alone are not enough; devotion is essential.

Meet Ramesh ; He reads a lot of holy books. He can quote verses and explain philosophy beautifully. One day, he poured holy water from the Ganga on his plants, thinking it would bring him blessings.

Yet, he never paused to offer a simple prayer to Krishna, never chanted His name from the heart.

His wise grandmother said: "Ramesh, reading and rituals are like filling a basket with water. Without devotion, it leaks away. The heart’s offering is what counts.”

Ramesh realized, humorously: "All this knowledge, and I forgot the simplest thing; just saying His name!”

Essence

• Spiritual practices and knowledge are tools, not goals.

• True protection and peace come from devotion and surrender.

• Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) is simple, powerful, and accessible to everyone.

Useful tip

• Don’t just read or perform rituals mechanically. Add love and devotion.

• Chant God’s name daily, even 5-10 times. Let your heart connect to the Divine.

• Knowledge plus devotion = lasting peace and protection.

Thought for the Day

Reading holy books and sipping Ganga water is nice, but if you forget to call God from your heart… you are still thirsty!

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 60 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 19 | यॊगरतॊ वा भॊगरतॊ वा | 
सङ्गरतॊ वा सङ्गविहीनः ।

यस्य ब्रह्मणि रमतॆ चित्तं नन्दति नन्दति नन्दत्यॆव ॥ 19 ॥

Yogarato Va Bhogarato Va Sangarato Va Sangavihinah

Yasya Brahmani Ramate Chittam

Nandati Nandati Nandatyeva

Shankaracharya explains:

It doesn’t matter whether a person is meditating, enjoying life, attached, or detached. If the mind is focused on God, they are truly happy and content.

Happiness comes from where your mind rests, not from external circumstances.

They are two friends Ravi & yogi . Ravi, a yogi, spends hours meditating every morning. Suresh, a chef, enjoys food, parties, and life’s pleasures.

One day, a traveler asked: “Who is happier?”. Ravi smiled calmly, eyes closed, peaceful. Suresh laughed heartily, serving sweets to neighbors, dancing, and singing.

The traveler was confused: “One is quiet, the other is loud… who is truly happy?”

A wise elder said: “Both! Ravi’s mind rests on God in meditation. Suresh’s mind also rests on God in joy and sharing. Happiness is not in silence or noise ; it is in connection to the Divine.”

Suresh added: “Even if I drop a ladle on my foot, I still laugh, because my heart is light!”

Essence

• True joy is independent of life circumstances.

• Focusing on God in meditation, work, or play brings peace that lasts.

• Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) and surrender cultivate this constant inner delight.

Useful tip

• Wherever you are at work, home, or alone, try spending 2-3 minutes remembering God.

• Focus on doing your duty with love and surrender.

• Notice the difference ; a few moments of divine focus bring smiles that money or fun cannot buy.

Thought for the Day

Meditating on God or laughing with friends, both are blissful if your heart rests on the Divine.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 59 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 18 | सुरमन्दिर तरु मूल निवासः | 
शय्या भूतलम्-अजिनं वासः ।

सर्व परिग्रह भॊगत्यागः

कस्य सुखं न करॊति विरागः ॥ 18 ॥

Suramandira Taru Mool Nivasah

Shayya Bhootalam Ajinam Vasah

Sarva Parigraha Bhogatyagah Kasya Sukham Na Karoti Viragah

Shankaracharya explains:

• Even a simple monk living under a tree, on the ground, with nothing can feel inner peace and happiness.

• Those who have freed themselves from attachment and greed can enjoy true comfort.

• Happiness does not come from possessions, but from letting go.

Meet Hari ji. Hari left the city and moved to a small hut under a banyan tree. He had no TV, no phone, no savings, only a mat to sleep on. Villagers thought he was crazy: “How can someone be happy with nothing?”

But every morning, he smiled at the sunrise, fed birds, shared water with travelers, and chatted joyfully with children passing by.

One day, a rich businessman visited him, complaining about stress and debts. Hari laughed softly: “You have all the wealth, but no peace. I have nothing, but I sleep like a king.”

The businessman realized: “All my money can buy comfort, but not contentment!”

Essence

• Real happiness comes from detachment and letting go, not material accumulation.

• Freedom from desire makes life light, joyful, and fearless.

• Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) and surrender help cultivate inner richness that possessions can never give.

Useful tip

• Start simplifying desires. Do you need that extra gadget, that unnecessary upgrade, or that one more sweet?

• Spend more time in gratitude and giving than acquiring.

• Chant God’s name daily to train the mind to be content and peaceful.

• Happiness is portable; you can carry it anywhere, unlike money or things.

Thought for the Day

Sleep on the ground, under a tree, with nothing but a mat and your heart full of God. That is true luxury.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 58 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 17 | कुरुतॆ गङ्गा सागर गमनं | 
व्रत परिपालनम्-अथवा दानम् ।

ज्ञान विहीनः सर्वमतॆन भजति न मुक्तिं जन्म शतॆन ॥ 17 ॥

Kurute Ganga Sagara Gamanam

Vrat Paripalanam Athava Danam

Jnana Vihinah Sarvamatena Bhajati Na Muktim Janma Shaten

Shankaracharya explains:

A person may perform great pilgrimages, fasts, or charity, But if they lack knowledge and devotion to God, these acts cannot give liberation.

External deeds without inner understanding are like empty shells ; impressive to see, but hollow inside.

There was a man, Raghav. He traveled to the Ganga, the ocean, and famous temples, performing rituals meticulously.

People admired him: “What devotion! He has visited all holy places!” Yet, Raghav was always worried about his next business deal, counting money even in the temple, and grumbling at his family for small mistakes.

One day, a priest asked him gently. “Raghav, all these journeys are beautiful, but where is your heart? Your mind is still chained to greed and worry.”

Raghav laughed awkwardly. He realized: “I went across oceans, climbed mountains, and still forgot the ocean of peace inside me!”

Essence

• External rituals and charity are good, but inner knowledge and devotion are essential.

• Real liberation comes from knowing God and chanting His name.

• Without God-consciousness, even a hundred lifetimes of pilgrimage won’t free you.

Useful tip

Don’t just follow rituals blindly; check your heart. Begin every act with God in mind.

Daily chanting or remembering God is more valuable than any long pilgrimage.

Focus on inner purity, not outer show.

Thought for the Day

Travel the world, give alms, fast… but if your heart forgets God, you are a tourist in life, never home.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 57 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 16 | अग्रॆ वह्निः पृष्ठॆ भानुः | 
रात्रौ चुबुक समर्पित जानुः ।

करतल भिक्षस्-तरुतल वासः

तदपि न मुञ्चत्याशा पाशः ॥ 16 ॥

Agre Vahnih Prishthhe Bhanuh Ratrau Chubuk Samarpita Januh

Karatal Bhiksas-Tarutal Vasah Tadapi Na Munchati Asha Pashah

Shankaracharya explains:

No matter how much a person performs rituals, worships, or gives charity, If the mind is still clinging to desire and greed, all actions are useless for liberation.

External devotion alone cannot free you; your heart and mind must let go of attachment.

Meet Ramesh-ji. Every day, he would light lamps at the temple, donate fruits, and even offer food to stray dogs.

Villagers admired him, saying, “He is so pious!”. But at home, he would complain if his son ate a sweet without asking and grumble if his neighbor had a bigger mango.

One festival day, a wise priest gently told him, “Ramesh-ji, all your offerings and rituals are like pouring water into a bucket full of holes. Your mind is still thirsty for more!”

Ramesh-ji paused. Humorously, he realized: “I have been feeding lamps, dogs, and charity boxes… but my own heart is still hungry and greedy!”

Essence

Rituals and charity are good, but without detachment and humility, they do not cleanse the mind.

True devotion is letting go of greed and desire.Chanting God’s name purifies the heart effortlessly.

Useful tip

• Observe your mind; Am I still craving more while I give?

• Offer not only materials but also your ego and attachment.

• Begin or end the day with God’s name to free the mind from endless desire.

• Let actions come from love, not from a hidden agenda.

Thought for the Day

Light lamps, give alms, chant prayers… but if your heart is still greedy, you are carrying sand in a sieve. Let God’s name fill your heart instead.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 56 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 15 | अङ्गं गलितं पलितं मुण्डं | 
दशन विहीनं जातं तुण्डम् ।

वृद्धॊ याति गृहीत्वा दण्डं

तदपि न मुञ्चत्याशा पिण्डम् ॥ 15 ॥

Angam Galitam Palitam Mundam Dashana Vihinam Jatam Tundam

Vruddho Yati Grihitva Dandam Tadapi Na Munchati Asha Pindam

Shankaracharya explains:

• When the body becomes old, weak, and frail, all physical strength, teeth, hair, and energy disappear.

• Yet, some people still cling to greed, desire, and worldly attachment.

• Material possessions and pride cannot follow you in old age or at the time of death.

There was an elderly man, Dinesh ji

• He lived with his family and always bragged: “When I die, my children will fight over my treasures!”

• Even at 80, he counted money, jewelry, and stocks every morning, worrying constantly.

• His grandchildren played outside, laughing and running around, while he sat bent, holding a walking stick and muttering about wealth.

One day, his neighbors invited him to a temple festival. Reluctantly, he went. He watched the children, the priests, and devotees singing, laughing, helping each other, and suddenly realized: “All my wealth won’t give me this joy or peace. I have been chasing shadows while real happiness was here!”

Humorously: Holding onto wealth at that age is like carrying a heavy bag while the bus is already at the stop ; pointless and exhausting!

Essence

• Life is temporary; the body will weaken, and possessions cannot travel with you.

• True comfort comes from detachment, devotion, and God-consciousness.

• Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) gives a lasting treasure that even old age and death cannot take away.

• Reflect daily: Am I holding too tightly to wealth, pride, or desires?

• Share time, love, and simple joys instead of obsessing over material things.

• Begin chanting God’s name, especially as you feel life passing, to keep your mind peaceful.

• Prepare your heart, not just your bank account.

Thought for the Day

Teeth fall, hair fades, energy disappears but God’s name in your heart is eternal. Carry that, not gold.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 55 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 14 | जटिलॊ मुण्डी लुञ्जित कॆशः | 
काषायान्बर बहुकृत वॆषः ।

पश्यन्नपि च न पश्यति मूढः

उदर निमित्तं बहुकृत वॆषः ॥ 14 ॥

Jatilo Mundi Lunjit Keshah Kashayaan Bar Bahukrit Veshah

Pashyannapi Cha Na Pashyati Moodhah

Udara Nimittam Bahukrit Veshah

Shankaracharya warns:

• People may look holy or wear religious robes, but inside they may be foolish or greedy.

• External appearance doesn’t guarantee inner purity.

• True spirituality is what you feel and practice in your heart, not what you show on the outside.

There was a man named Hari in a village. Hari wore orange clothes, tied his hair in a big knot, and carried a walking stick. People saw him and said, “Ah! He must be a very great saint!”. They bowed to him and spoke softly in front of him.

But inside… Hari was not so nice.

• He cheated people when he sold things.

• He spoke badly about others when no one was watching.

• He thought only about money and food, not about God.

One day, a small child looked at Hari and asked innocently, “Uncle, why do you look so good outside, but act so bad inside?”. Hari felt very ashamed. For the first time, he understood the truth.

He said to himself, “Oh! These clothes did not make me good.

My hair did not make me pure.

Only a kind heart and good actions can make me holy.”

Humorously, Wearing holy clothes without a pure heart is likeputting a lion’s cape on a cat. It may look funny…but everyone knows it is still a cat!

Essence

• God does not look at our clothes. ;God looks at our heart

• Being good means:

• speaking truth

• being kind

• helping others

• remembering God with love

Saying God’s name every day slowly cleans our heart, just like soap cleans our hands.

Useful tip

Every day, ask yourself:

• Was I kind today?

• Was I honest?

• Did I remember God with love?

Let God’s name be your real dress. That dress never gets dirty.

Thought for the Day

Holy robes can’t hide a naughty heart. Let God’s name dress your heart truly.

@@ 54 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 13 | का तॆ कान्ता धन गत चिन्ता वातुल किं तव नास्ति नियन्ता । | 
त्रिजगति सज्जन सङ्गतिरॆका भवति भवार्णव तरणॆ नौका ॥ 13 ॥

Ka Te Kanta Dhana Gata Chinta Vatula Kim Tava Nasti Niyanta

Trijagati Sajjan Sangatireka Bhavati Bhavarnava Tarane Nauka

Shankaracharya explains:

• When wealth is gone, family worries you, and desires increase, life becomes unstable.

• Nobody can control fate or fix everything.

• Only association with good, wise people is like a boat across the ocean of life.

In other words, in the ups and downs of life, good friends and spiritual company are your real support

Imagine Raju:-He was traveling alone in a small rickety bus during a huge storm.Rain poured, roads were slippery, and the bus swayed dangerously. Passengers panicked. But an elderly, calm man sat beside him, guiding and reassuring everyone: “Hold tight, we’ll cross safely. Don’t panic.”

Raju realized: “I was terrified alone, but with this wise person beside me, I felt safe. Life is like this stormy bus we need good company to survive the storms.”

Humorously: Life without wise friends is like trying to swim across a river in flip-flops.

Essence

• Wealth, family, and fame are unstable and uncertain.

• Real safety comes from spiritual company and wise friends.

• Surround yourself with people who encourage goodness, God-consciousness, and calm.

• God’s name + good company = double protection in life’s storms.

Practical Takeaway

• Spend time with wise, kind, and spiritual people.

• Avoid company that pulls you into greed, anger, or worry.

• Daily chanting or spiritual discussion with friends strengthens your mind like a boat in a storm.

• Remember: one good friend can be your lifeboat across the ocean of life.

Thought for the day

Life is an ocean; wealth and family are just leaves. Wise friends and God’s name are your sturdy boat.

@@ 53 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 12 | दिन यामिन्यौ सायं प्रातः |

तदपि न मुञ्चत्याशावायुः ॥ 12 ॥

Din Yaminau Sayam Pratah

Shishir Vasantau Punarayatah

Shankaracharya is explaining how time never waits:

• Days, nights, seasons - everything keeps moving.

• Life is like a game; your age is ticking away.

• Even if you wish and hope, time doesn’t pause for anyone.

The message:- use your time wisely. Don’t waste it chasing illusions.

A little boy, Kiran, saw a honeybee buzzing around flowers. He asked, “Bee, why are you always running?” The bee replied, “I have only so many days to collect nectar before winter. I cannot stop to play.”

Kiran laughed, realizing: “Even a tiny bee doesn’t waste time. And I complain about hours while scrolling my phone!”

Life is like a train & if you sleep, it will pass without you!

Essence

• Time is relentless and unstoppable. Wasting life on greed, anger, or illusions is pointless.

• True wisdom is to spend time on God, self-improvement, and service.

Chanting God’s name daily helps you stay focused and steady, instead of being carried away by the rush of time.

@@ 52 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 11 | मा कुरु धनजन यौवन गर्वं |

मायामयमिदम्-अखिलं हित्वा

Ma Kuru Dhana Jana Yauvana Garvam

Shankaracharya is warning by saying :-

• Don’t be proud of wealth, youth, or family. Time passes in the blink of an eye everything you cling to can vanish.

• The world is an illusion; real life is connecting with the Divine (Brahman).

• It’s a call to focus on what lasts, not what is temporary.

Ramesh was 25, rich, handsome, and popular. Every day, he showed off his car, his watch, and his new gadgets. His friends teased, “Wow! You’re the king of the world!”

One day, he slipped on wet stairs and broke his leg. He couldn’t drive his car or go to the office. His friends got busy with their lives.

Ramesh realized: “All my pride, my youth, my money ,none of this protects me from life’s accidents. Only my faith in God gives me strength.”

Humorously: Being proud of youth and money is like bragging about ice cream on a hot day ; it melts fast!

Wealth, youth, family, and pride are temporary and unreliable.

Real strength and protection come from inner connection with God.

The sooner you let go of illusions (maya), the sooner you attain lasting peace.

Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) anchors your heart while everything else changes.

Useful tip

• Check yourself daily, Am I proud of things that will vanish?

• Practice humility and gratitude.

• Spend a few minutes chanting God’s name, especially when life feels unstable.

• Remember youth and wealth are gifts, not guarantees.

Thought for the Day
