@@ 51 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 10 | वयसि गतॆ कः कामविकारः | 
शुष्के नीरॆ कः कासारः ।

क्षीणॆ वित्तॆ कः परिवारः

ज्ञातॆ तत्त्वॆ कः संसारः ॥ 10 ॥

Vayasi Gate Kah Kama Vikarah

Shushke Neere Kah Kasarah

Kshine Vitte Kah Parivarah

Jnate Tattve Kah Samsarah

Shankaracharya is telling us that nothing in this world stays forever.

• When old age comes, strong desires automatically go away. What we once ran behind no longer attracts us.

• When water dries up, the pond becomes useless. Spoiled milk cannot be used again.

• In the same way, when money goes, even relatives may stop caring.

• When true wisdom comes, the pull of this world disappears.

This verse is a soft reminder: don’t hold tightly to things that will not last.

Think of a simple village man called Mohan. When he was young, he wanted a big house, money, name, and respect. He worked hard, earned money, got married, and raised children.

Slowly, time passed. His body became weak, savings reduced, and his children became busy with their own lives.

One day, Mohan saw an old well in the village fully dried up. He thought, “Just like this well, money, family support, and desires can also dry up. But the peace I got by chanting God’s name is still with me.”

In a light way, life is like a water balloon ; enjoy it, but remember it will burst one day.

Money, relationships, desires, and pleasures do not stay forever. Only self-knowledge and God’s name stay with us till the end.

When we understand that everything is temporary, we naturally turn towards what really matters is peace inside and remembrance of God.

Useful tip

• Every day, think for a few minutes that nothing in this world is permanent.

• Don’t waste your life running behind endless wants.

• Chant God’s name daily ; it is the only support that never changes.

• Keep your mind away from things that will disappear, and your heart connected to the eternal.

Thought for the Day

Money, family, and desires all dry up like old water. Only God’s name remains fresh forever.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 50 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 9 | सत्सङ्गत्वॆ निस्सङ्गत्वं | 
निस्सङ्गत्वॆ निर्मॊहत्वम् ।

निर्मॊहत्वॆ निश्चलतत्त्वं

निश्चलतत्त्वॆ जीवन्मुक्तिः ॥ 9 ॥

Satsangatve Nissangatvam

Nissangatve Nirmohatvam

Nirmohatve Nishchalatattvam

Nishchalatattve Jeevanmuktihi

Shankaracharya is explaining the path to true freedom step by step, like guiding a child by holding the hand:

1. Satsanga - Spend time with good and wise people who remind you of God and goodness.

2. Nissanga - Slowly stop depending on things to make you happy.

3. Nirmoha - Let go of confusion, false hopes, and endless desires.

4. Nishchalatattva - Your mind becomes steady, like a lamp that does not flicker in the wind.

5. Jeevanmukti - You are free here and now, even while living in this world.

This is not a jump. It is a ladder ; each step naturally leads to the next.

Imagine a simple man named Anand who wanted peace in life:

• First, Anand began spending time with kind, calm, spiritual people (satsanga). He noticed his thoughts becoming cleaner.

• Slowly, he stopped running behind money, gadgets, and constant upgrades (nissanga).

• Then, jealousy, anger, and “why don’t I have what others have?” started losing their grip (nirmoha).

• Over time, even praise and blame could not shake him (nishchalatattva).

One day, friends came running to him with many problems.

Anand smiled gently and said,

“I will help you ; but my mind is no longer a prisoner of worry.”

That calm smile itself was Jeevanmukti.

Essence

Satsanga: Good company does not change you loudly ; it changes you silently.

Nissanga: You can use things, but don’t let things use you.

Nirmoha: When false ideas fall, clarity rises naturally.

Nishchalatattva: Joy and sorrow come and go ; you remain steady.

Jeevanmukti: Freedom is not after death; it is freedom from fear and craving while alive.

Namasankeerthanam (chanting God’s name) gently supports every step. Without force, it loosens attachments and steadies the mind ; like a mother calming a restless child.

Thought for the Day

Climb the ladder of satsanga, drop illusions along the way, stay steady inside, and live free with God’s name softly beating in your heart.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 49 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 7 · Verse 8 | बाल स्तावत् क्रीडासक्तः · का तॆ कान्ता कस्तॆ पुत्रः | 
तरुण स्तावत् तरुणीसक्तः ।

वृद्ध स्तावत् चिन्तामग्नः

परमे ब्रह्मणि कोऽपि न लग्नः ॥ 7 ॥

Bāla stāvat krīḍā-saktaḥ

Taruna stāvat taruṇī-saktaḥ

Vṛddha stāvat cintā-magnaḥ

Parame brahmaṇi ko’pi na lagnaḥ

Adi Shankaracharya points out a simple truth: In childhood, we are absorbed in play. In youth, we are caught in attraction and enjoyment. In old age, we are weighed down by worries.

Sadly, through all these stages, most people fail to remember God or the eternal Truth.

With deep compassion, Shankaracharya observes human life and notes a common pattern: people are always busy, yet rarely peaceful.

Life moves from childhood to youth to old age, but the mind never rests, because it keeps chasing something outside.

Once there was a boy named Raju. As a child, he carried a bag full of toys and said, “I have no time for anything else. I must play!” . As a young man, he dropped the toy bag and carried a bag of dreams : friends, relationships, fun, name, and status. He said, “I don’t have time for God now. Later.”

In old age, Raju carried a heavy bag of worries : health, money, children, and fear of death. He wondered, “Why is my mind never peaceful?”

One day, a wise man smiled and said, “Raju, all your life you kept changing bags, but you never put them down and looked within.” This is the heart of Shankaracharya’s teaching.

Life’s stages change, but attachment continues. Play, pleasure, and worry are not wrong; forgetting God is. When the mind loses connection with the Eternal, it keeps running. Remembering God brings balance, peace, and clarity at every age.

Essence

Life itself is not the problem. Forgetfulness of God is. You need not escape the world; just do not forget the One who is beyond it.

Useful Tips

Whatever your age, spend a few minutes with God every day. Chant a simple name : Govinda, Rama, Shiva, or your Ishta Devata.

Teach children devotion along with education.

Do not wait for old age or crisis to remember God. Let God walk with you while playing, working, and resting.

Thought for the Day

Childhood runs after play, youth runs after pleasure, old age runs after worry; the wise one pauses, remembers God, and walks peacefully.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

Bhajagovindham – Verse 8

का तॆ कान्ता कस्तॆ पुत्रः

संसारॊஉயमतीव विचित्रः ।

कस्य त्वं वा कुत आयातः

तत्वं चिन्तय तदिह भ्रातः ॥ 8 ॥

Ka Te Kanta Kas Te Putrah Samsaro Uyamateeva Vichitrah

Kasya Tvam Va Kut Ayatah Tattvam Chintaya Tadih Bhratah ||8||

Shankaracharya is gently shaking us awake. He asks: “Who is your wife? Who is your child?” Not to deny love, but to question our blind attachment. This world, he says, is truly strange and confusing when we forget the deeper truth of who we really are.

Instead of getting trapped in labels and relationships, he urges us to pause and reflect: Who am I? Where did I come from? What is the truth behind this life?

Ravi once visited a railway station to receive his close friend. Suddenly, an announcement was made: “All passengers, please identify your luggage carefully.” Ravi looked around ; bags everywhere looked similar. For a moment, he panicked. Then he smiled and said, “Only when I know which bag is mine, I can move forward.”

At that moment, his friend arrived and laughed, “Ravi, in life you are running around carrying everyone’s expectations , but have you identified yourself?”

At home, the same Ravi is pulled in many directions. His mother calls, “Son, help me. His wife asks, “Did you remember the payment?”

His children shout, “Papa, look at me!” His boss messages, “Urgent!”

Ravi feels overwhelmed and wonders silently, “Am I only a son, husband, father, employee… or am I something more?”

Shankaracharya would compassionately say: “Do not get lost in the noise. First know who you are. When the center is clear, all roles fall into place naturally.”

Humorously, life is like acting in many dramas on the same stage but forgetting that you are the actor, not the costume you wear in each scene.

Life becomes heavy when we cling only to names, roles, and relationships. Peace dawns when we recognize our true identity ,the eternal Self beyond body and mind. Relationships are beautiful, but temporary. Self-knowledge alone is lasting.

Namasankeerthanam , chanting God’s name slowly peels away confusion and brings us back to our true center.

Useful Tip

Spend a few quiet minutes daily asking yourself: Who am I beyond my duties and titles?

When life feels overwhelming, pause and chant God’s name ,it brings instant grounding.

Perform your roles with love, but stay rooted in the eternal Self within.

Thought for the Day

When you know who you truly are, life’s confusion loses its power. God’s name is the lamp that helps you recognize your own bag in the crowded station of life.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️

@@ 48 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 6 | यावत्-पवनॊ निवसति दॆहॆ | 
तावत्-पृच्छति कुशलं गॆहॆ ।

गतवति वायौ दॆहापायॆ

भार्या बिभ्यति तस्मिन् कायॆ ॥ 6 ॥

Yavat-Pavano Nivasati Dehe Tavat-Prichchhati Kushalam Gehe

Gatavati Vayau Dehapaye Bharya Bibhyati Tasmin kaye

Shankaracharya says attention and care from others last only as long as your body is healthy.

When your body is strong and alive, family and friends ask, “Are you fine?” But the moment your health fails, some even fear or avoid you.

Life is like that ,respect and attention are temporary if based only on physical presence or usefulness.

Mr. Sharma was always fit and active. Neighbors and friends loved having him around. Everyone would check, “How are you?” or invite him to tea.

One day, he fell ill. Suddenly, the phone stopped ringing. Visitors came rarely. Even some relatives seemed uncomfortable around him.

Mr. Sharma laughed and said: “I was popular when I could walk and talk, but now I’m invisible. Only God’s love can never leave me.”

Essence

Don’t depend on others’ attention for happiness. Build a connection with the eternal.

Human care is often conditional, depending on your health or usefulness. Fear, avoidance, or neglect is natural when life or health declines.

True peace comes from inner devotion, not outer attention. Chanting God’s name daily keeps your heart strong and content, regardless of others’ actions.

Useful tip

• Appreciate when others care, but don’t rely on it for peace of mind.

• Strengthen your inner faith and devotion ; they are permanent.

• Be kind and helpful, but don’t be upset if people avoid you in weakness.

• Daily simple prayer or God’s name makes you fearless and steady.

Thought for the Day

When your body is strong, people care. When it falters, only God’s name holds you tight.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 47 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 5 | यावद्-वित्तॊपार्जन सक्तः | 
तावन्-निजपरिवारॊ रक्तः ।

पश्चाज्जीवति जर्जर दॆहॆ

वार्तां कॊஉपि न पृच्छति गॆहॆ ॥ 5 ॥

Yavad-Vitto Parjana Saktah Tavan Nija Parivaro Raktah

Pashchaj Jeevati Jarjara Dehe Vartam Ko’pi Na Prichchhati Gehe

Shankaracharya is telling us a simple truth: As long as a person earns and helps, people stay close. When age, illness, or weakness comes, attention slowly reduces. This happens in many homes quietly, without bad intention

So he reminds us: “Don’t depend fully on people for happiness.” Because people change. God doesn’t. Shankaracharya states a simple and honest truth about life.

In a small apartment, there lived a grandfather. When he was younger, he went to office every day; paid school fees ; bought groceries ; helped everyone in the house ; Whenever he came home, people asked: “Did you eat?….”Are you tired?”…..”How was your day?”…..Years passed.

Grandfather retired. His legs became weak. He sat quietly on the same chair near the window every day. Now in the house: Everyone was busy with phones ; Children had online classes ; Adults had office calls ; Nobody was rude ; Nobody shouted ; But nobody asked: “How are you feeling today?”

Grandfather noticed something. Earlier, people needed his money and strength. Now he needed only one kind word. That day he smiled and said to himself: “People are not wrong… this is how the world works.”

So he began to chant God’s name softly, sitting on his chair. That gave him peace even when no one noticed him.

Essence

Money brings attention; Youth brings importance; Strength brings respect. But all three will go one day. Only God’s name stays with us in childhood, adulthood, and old age.

Thought for the day

When people become busy, God becomes our companion.

Dhanyosmi 🙏😊

@@ 46 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 4 | नलिनी दलगत जलमति तरलं | 
तद्वज्जीवित मतिशय चपलम् ।

विद्धि व्याध्यभिमान ग्रस्तं

लोकं शोकहतं च समस्तम्

Nalini-dala-gata jalam ati-taralam

Tadvat jīvitam ati-śaya capalam |

Viddhi vyādhi-abhimāna-grastaṁ

Lokaṁ śoka-hataṁ ca samastam

Adi Shankaracharya compares human life to a water droplet resting on a lotus leaf. It shines beautifully, yet it can slip away without warning.

In the same way, Life is chanchalam , extremely unstable . The world is gripped by illness (vyadhi), ego (abhimana), and sorrow. Clinging to what is temporary only deepens suffering

There once lived an old monk near a quiet lotus pond. Every morning before sunrise, he would sit in silence by the water.

One day, a young man approached him and said: “Swami, people say life is fleeting. But I am healthy, successful, and strong. Why should I think about impermanence now?”

The monk smiled and said nothing. Instead, he asked the young man to come again the next morning.

At dawn, the lotus flowers were in full bloom. On one petal, a single dew drop shimmered like a jewel. The monk remained silent. As the sun rose and a soft breeze passed, the droplet trembled… rolled… and disappeared into the pond.

Then the monk spoke gently: “Did you notice how precious it looked?” “Did you notice how effortlessly it vanished?”

The young man nodded. The monk continued: “That droplet did not fall because of a storm.

Not because of violence. Just a small movement of life itself was enough.” He paused and said “That is your body. That is your health. That is your pride. That is your certainty about tomorrow.”

Sankara is not trying to frighten us. He is awakening us gently. Life is beautiful, but not secure. Disease comes quietly. Ego grows unnoticed. Sorrow accumulates silently

Suffering arises not because life is impermanent,but because we forget that it is.

Sankara does not say: “Reject life.” He says: “Do not lean on life for permanence.” That is why he says , Bhaja Govindam.

When the heart holds on to the Eternal, the slipping of the temporary no longer terrifies us.

Essence

Live fully, but hold lightly. Love deeply, but do not cling. Care for the body, but do not worship it. Anchor the mind daily in God’s name, not as ritual, but as inner stability

Thought for the Day

Life is a droplet on a lotus leaf, not meant to be held,but meant to remind us of the Eternal.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 45 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 3 | नारी स्तनभर नाभीदॆशं | 
दृष्ट्वा मा गा मॊहावॆशम् ।

ऎतन्मांस वसादि विकारं

मनसि विचिन्तया वारं वारम् ॥ 3 ॥

Naari Stanabhara Nabheedesham

Drishtva Ma Ga Mohavesham

Etan Maamsa Vasadi Vikaram Manasi Vichintaya Varam Varam

Shankaracharya is teaching about attachment to the body and senses, especially lust. He says: “Don’t be fooled by temporary beauty or flesh. Our attraction to bodies is shallow and fleeting, like mist in the morning.”

Falling in love with appearances leads to distraction, suffering, and bondage, while the mind remains restless.

Vikram was scrolling social media late at night. He stopped at a glamorous post of a movie star. His mind was instantly captivated: “Wow! Such beauty! I need this in my life!”

Next day, Vikram meets someone at work who is kind, smart, and genuine. But his mind still compares: “She is not like the celebrity…” Weeks pass. He realizes that chasing appearances made him miss real connections and peace.

Shankara would say: “Vikram, why be crazy about what fades? Flesh and looks change. Beauty is temporary, devotion lasts forever.”

Essence

Physical attraction is temporary; the body changes over time.

Lust is a trap ; it clouds judgment and creates suffering.

True fulfillment comes from nurturing the heart and mind, not chasing bodies.

Spiritual growth requires seeing beyond the external, focusing on the eternal.

Thought for the Day

Don’t chase the sparkling wrapper; enjoy the sweet inside. Bodies fade, but the heart that loves wisely stays forever.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 44 | Bhajagovindam – Verse 2 | मूढ जहीहि धनागम तृष्णां | 
कुरु सद्बुद्धिं मनसि वितृष्णाम् ।

यल्लभसे निजकर्मोपात्तं

वित्तं तेन विनोदय चित्तम् ॥

Moodha Jahi Dhanagama Trishnam

Kuru Sadbuddhim Manasi Vitrishnam

Yallabhase Nijakarmopattam Vittam Tena Vinodaya Chittam

Shankara now looks at us and says: “My dear friend, stop running behind money with endless greed. Earn what you need, but don’t lose your peace for it.”

Desire is like fire. If you feed it more wood, it burns bigger… not smaller. Shankara wants us to light the lamp of good sense instead of the fire of greed.

There was a young man who first bought a small bike. After two months he said: “This is too basic… I deserve a bigger one.” He bought the bigger one. Three months later: “No no… this is nothing… my neighbour bought an even bigger bike.” So he upgraded again. Then after a year: “This bike is fine… but maybe a car will make me truly happy.” He bought a car. Six months later: “My friend bought a better model… my happiness is gone.”

One day his father quietly said: “Son… your happiness has more gears than your bike.

It keeps shifting all the time.”

The boy laughed, but that night he realised: “If my happiness changes every time someone buys something, then the problem is not outside. It is inside.”

We all carry this same disease. “When I get that… then I will be happy.” But the finish line keeps moving!

Essence

Greed never ends ; it only grows. Peace does not come from money; it comes from contentment. Earn honestly. Enjoy what comes to you through your own karma. Don’t compare your life with others ; comparison destroys peace. The heart that is satisfied becomes God’s temple.

This verse is actually pushing us gently toward Namasankeerthanam and surrender, because only devotion can cool the burning mind.

Thought for the Day

Chase God a little more… and chase money a little less. Only one of them follows you even after retirement.

@@ 43 | Bhajagovindham – Verse 1 | भज गोविन्दं भज गोविन्दं | 
गोविन्दं भज मूढमतॆ ।

सम्प्राप्तॆ सन्निहितॆ कालॆ

नहि नहि रक्षति डुक्रिङ्करणॆ ॥ 1 ॥

Bhaja Govindam Bhaja Govindam

Govindam Bhaja Moodhamate

Samprapte Sannihite Kale

Nahi Nahi Rakshati Dukrinkarane

Adi Shankaracharya once saw an elderly man struggling to memorize Sanskrit grammar. The man’s hands trembled with age, yet he clutched the book tightly and recited grammar rules like a devoted student.

Shankara gently said: “Brother, at this stage of life, memorizing grammar won’t save you. Instead, remember Govinda & chant God’s name. That alone will help you at the end.”

This verse is a gentle wake-up call to convey that worldly knowledge, wealth, or accomplishments alone cannot protect you at the time of death ; only devotion and remembrance of God can.

Think of our modern life: There’s a grandpa in a metro city.. Every day he writes down all his passwords in a little notebook: Bank password, Phone lock,ATM PIN, Wi-Fi password, Even Netflix password!

His grandchildren tease him:

“Thatha, why do you write the same passwords again and again?” He replies earnestly: “If I forget, I am finished!”

One night, he suddenly falls ill. As the family rushes him to the hospital, he cries: “Bring my password book! I can’t manage without it!”

The doctor shakes his head with a sad smile: “Sir, no password will help now. Only God’s name can give you peace at this moment.”

We spend our entire lives memorizing trivial things, but forget the one thing that truly protects us which is devotion.

Essence

Don’t wait until the last moment to remember God. Knowledge, money, and worldly accomplishments are temporary. Your heart should know God’s name as naturally as your phone remembers your fingerprint.

Remembering God is protection. Not just knowledge, not just rituals , a heart that connects with the Divine is what truly matters.

Thought for the Day

Keep God’s name saved in your heart… don’t wait to Google Him at the last minute.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 42 |  | What is Karma? | 
Think of your life like a school bag. Inside this bag, there are three types of notebooks.

1. Sanchita Karma - The Cupboard of Old Notebooks. This is all the homework from all your earlier classes. A big storage of all old actions from this life and past lives.

You don’t have to do all of it today. It is only stored, like a cupboard full of old notebooks.

Example: You have 500 old notebooks in a cupboard. You don’t carry them every day. That is Sanchita.

2. Prarabdha Karma - Today’s Homework Notebook

From that big cupboard,one notebook is taken out and put in today’s school bag. This notebook is your current life: your family, your body, your basic nature, your main challenges, your main gifts

You only work with this one notebook today. Life never gives all the old homework at once.

Example:Teacher gives today’s maths homework, not the whole year’s work. That is Prarabdha.

3. Agami / Kriyamana Karma- The Homework You Create Today

This is the new writing you do now. Your current actions: how you talk, how you behave, how you handle anger, how kind or honest you are

These become tomorrow’s results.

Example: You practice piano today → you play better tomorrow. That is Agami.

Putting All Three Together

A boy starts a new school year.

He has:

1. Old notebooks in the cupboard → Sanchita

2. One notebook in today’s bag → Prarabdha

3. New writing he does now → Agami

The teacher never dumps the whole cupboard on him. Only one notebook per day. Life works exactly like that.

How Do We Reduce Karma?

You don’t burn the cupboard in one shot. But you can do 3 simple things:

1. Do Today’s Homework Calmly. Whatever life has given today, handle it with patience and dignity. This lightens Prarabdha.

2. Stop Creating New Wrong Notes. Avoid anger, harsh words, cheating, hurting others.This stops Agami.

3. Do Good Actions + Chant God’s Name . Goodness and chanting act like fire. They slowly burn the old Sanchita. This is why saints feel light and their cupboard becomes emptier each day.

The Most Beautiful Part

Even if your cupboard has 1000 notebooks, God puts only one in your school bag today. That one , you can definitely handle. And you are never alone.

Thought for the Day (TFD)

Karma is not a heavy load from the past. It is only today’s homework.Do it sincerely, and the whole past becomes light.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: please read the previous TFDs once again to get the recap of the conscious and sun conscious mind .

@@ 41 |  | Understanding Mind & Subconscious mind Through Vedanta | 
In the previous 2 TFDs, we read about conscious mind and sub conscious mind. Let us see the same through the lens of Vedanta.

Mind = Manas (the surface layer)

Subconscious mind = Vasana Storehouse (the deeper layer)

Vedanta says the mind has two layers:

1. Manas (Conscious Mind) - the part that thinks now: “What should I do? Why is this happening? Should I react?”

This is small, active, and easily disturbed.

2. Vasana Layer (Subconscious Mind) - the deeper storehouse of all impressions from many years (and many births): habits, fears, likes, dislikes, tendencies.

Whatever sits here becomes our automatic behaviour.

A simple example ; The House & the Storeroom

• The conscious mind is like your living room - what you see, arrange, clean daily.

• The subconscious with vasanas is the old storeroom filled with things you don’t see daily: old memories, hurt, anger, beliefs, ego, guilt, fears, attachments.

When a crisis happens…

The mind doesn’t react from the living room. It reacts from the storeroom, because that is where your vasanas live.

That’s why even when we “know” something, we still feel something else.

Knowing = conscious

Feeling = vasana layer

Another example; A person reads slokam every day (conscious effort).But someone comments something hurtful suddenly anger jumps out.

Where did it come from?

Not from the conscious mind.

It came from the vasana of anger sitting quietly inside for years.

So where does chanting / parayanam work?

Vedanta says vasanas are purified only by repeated sattvic inputs. Slokam chanting, Nama japa, satsang, any noble thought

drops one small clean impression into the storehouse.

One drop daily → slowly changes the water in the tank.

It is not instant. But it is very powerful.

A strong Vedantic comparison

• Conscious mind = daytime moon

• Subconscious / vasanas = actual moon

Daytime moon looks tiny, weak, almost invisible. But the real moon has huge gravitational power affecting tides.

Similarly, Your conscious mind looks powerful , “I will be calm, I will not get angry.” But your vasanas actually decide the tide of your emotions.

Then how do we purify vasanas?

Vedanta gives only two methods:

1. Abhyasa (repetition with awareness)

2. Vairagya (letting go of ego-driven reactions)

Chanting, slokam, japa, reading a shloka every morning , all this is Abhyasa. Slowly the deeper layer gets filtered.

Thought for the Day

Your conscious mind decides your actions. Your vasanas decide your reactions.

Purify the vasanas and life itself becomes lighter.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 40 |  | If our mind keeps jumping like a monkey, how does Namasankeerthanam/ Nama… | 
If our mind keeps jumping like a monkey, how does Namasankeerthanam/ Nama chanting train it?

Because the mind listens only to what is repeated again… and again… and again. And nothing repeats more sweetly and stubbornly than God’s Name.

What actually happens inside?

The conscious mind tries to focus for two seconds. By the third second it wonders: “Breakfast? Messages? Charger?”

But the subconscious quietly absorbs whatever is repeated like a sponge. Every time you chant, one drop of peace goes in, one layer of fear comes out.

Slowly the inner mind gets trained… gently, without any drama.

A Simple Analogy

Your mind is like a little monkey at home jumping on the sofa, opening the fridge, checking WhatsApp, then innocently asking, “Why am I stressed?”

Give the monkey a banana → it sits still. God’s Name is that banana. It gives the mind something safe to hold.

When milk is boiling, you look away for one second ,it overflows. The mind is exactly the same. Left unattended, it overflows with worry, comparison, irritation.

But when you chant even softly, even mentally your attention stays anchored. Milk stays inside. Mind stays inside.

Three Silent Benefits of Namasankeerthanam

1. Purifies the subconscious, old patterns lose grip.

2. Anchors the conscious and the mind finally gets a handle.

3. Builds a bridge to God and in crisis, even if the mouth forgets, the subconscious whispers the Name… and that whisper becomes your strength.

Essence

Your thoughts jump. Your emotions swing. Your mind wanders.But God’s Name stays.Hold the Name and the mind learns to hold you.

Thought for the Day

You don’t train the mind by fighting it. You train it by feeding it the right sound. Repeat the Name… and the mind slowly stops wandering and starts remembering.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 39 |  | If our mind has so much power, why does it become weak exactly in crisis time? | 
If our mind has so much power, why does it become weak exactly in crisis time?

Because we use only the tiny tip of our mind… the conscious part.

The real power lies with the subconscious mind which sits quietly underneath, like the huge portion of an iceberg.

The conscious mind is the small tip that plans, reads, and decides. The massive subconscious lies below, keep storing habits, fears, reactions, beliefs, past wounds, old memories

Whatever we repeatedly put into it… becomes our nature.

Imagine a small child sitting on a giant elephant.

Child = conscious mind

Elephant = subconscious mind

When the elephant walks calmly, the child thinks, “See? I am controlling it!” But if the elephant gets upset, even the child gets terrified and goes into panic mode. That is us. When the subconscious is peaceful, we look wise. When it is disturbed, wisdom runs away before we do.

A man lit a diya daily and complained, “My light looks so dull!” A friend wiped the window.Instant brightness.

The light = your soul

The window = your subconscious

The cleaning = spiritual practice

Your inner light is always bright. Only the window needs cleaning.

Why sub conscious mind Matters in spiritual angle?

In crisis, the conscious mind panics. But a trained subconscious automatically remembers God ,like a child automatically calling “Amma!” when scared.

Daily chanting, prayer, gratitude, and noble thoughts slowly purify the subconscious (Chitta Shuddhi). Then faith becomes your natural reflex.

Useful tip

First input of the day becomes the day’s mood. Don’t start your day with reading the news and TV or Internet . Start your day by chanting /prayers or reading something that kindles your spiritual thoughts. Two calm breaths slows the elephant and calms the child.

Essence

The conscious mind remembers God. The subconscious mind relies on God.

When your inner mind becomes calm and clean, you no longer “search” for God in crisis, you stand on Him, because He is already rooted inside you.

Thought for the day

Strengthen the part of the mind you cannot see. When the subconscious is filled with faith, God becomes your reflex, not your reminder.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 38 |  | Why is it that when a real crisis hits us, we forget God exactly at the… | 
Why is it that when a real crisis hits us, we forget God exactly at the moment we need Him the most?

Let me tell you a very simple story.

A small boy was learning to ride a cycle. Every evening, his father would hold the back of the cycle and run with him. The boy used to say, “Appa, don’t leave me!” And the father would reply, “I am right here.”

One day, the boy went alone to the street to practise. Suddenly a fast lorry came from behind, honking loudly. The boy got scared, lost balance, and fell.

And instantly he shouted,

“Appa! Where are you? You said you are with me!”

But the father was actually standing two feet away. He came running and said, “I was here, kanna. When you got scared, you forgot my presence… but I never left.”

This is what happens to us.

When life is normal, we say,

“I trust God completely.”

But the moment a shock comes, our mind gets frightened,loses balance, and instantly forgets that God is still standing right beside us. There is nothing wrong with us. The mind behaves like that because it has no practice of remembering God during pressure.

So what should we do?

Just one tiny daily habit: Every morning or evening, for one minute, close your eyes and say softly: “God, whatever happens today, I know You are with me.” This one line repeated daily goes into the subconscious mind.

Then, when a crisis comes,

instead of shouting, “Why me?” your mind will automatically say, “Wait… God is here. I can handle this.”

Because you have practised it.

The boy who practised cycling daily could balance even when fear came. In the same way,

a mind that practises trust daily remains steady even in trouble.

Essence

Trust in God is not built during problems. It is built in calm times through small, daily reminders.

Thought for the Day

Let me practise remembering God in peaceful moments, so that in crisis, my mind does not forget His presence.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 37 |  | How can simply chanting or singing God’s Name help us surrender? Is it not… | 
How can simply chanting or singing God’s Name help us surrender? Is it not surrender something big…?

A child was climbing the stairs during a power cut. He held his father’s hand tightly. The father said, “Don’t be scared, I am with you.” The child replied, “Appa, I am not holding you. You are holding me.”

This is what Namasankeerthanam quietly does. We think we are chanting the Name. But slowly we realise that the Name is carrying us.

Another day, a man was driving in heavy rain, gripping the steering wheel too tightly. His friend said, “Relax your hands. The road is the same… only your fear has increased.”

When we chant the Name,

our inner grip loosens. The mind stops shaking. Fear slowly melts.

And just like a traveller who hears a familiar tune and feels instantly safe, our heart settles when we hear or chant the Divine Name. The mind becomes less interested in controlling everything… and more ready to trust.

Surrender doesn’t come by force. It comes when the heart softens. The Name softens us.

Essence

When the mind repeats the Name, the heart remembers it is not alone. And in that gentle remembrance… surrender happens naturally.

Thought for the Day

The more you hold the Divine Name, the more life holds you and surrender becomes a natural response, not a spiritual effort.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 36 |  | What should be our approach towards family which could help us in the path… | 
What should be our approach towards family which could help us in the path of surrender?

Think of family like the people sitting in our car while we drive. We love them, we care for them, and we want them to feel safe. But if everyone starts telling us how to drive like “Go faster!” “Slow down!” “Take left!” “No, take right!” , we get stressed, and the journey becomes tiring.

In the same way, when we allow everyone’s expectations, opinions, and reactions to sit on our head, we lose our peace. And when we try to control their life completely, they lose their peace.

But when we simply say, I will drive calmly. You be comfortable. I will do my best, and the rest is in God’s hands, both the car and the relationships move smoothly.

Family life becomes a burden only when we shift from caring to controlling. When we care with love, but leave space for everyone to be themselves, our mind becomes light and surrender becomes natural.

Essence

Love them, support them, be there for them but don’t try to control every outcome. This lightness brings peace.

Thought for the Day

Let me hold my family with love, not pressure ,so my heart stays free for surrender.

@@ 35 |  | How can I change my daily habits so that “surrender”becomes easier for me? | 
How can I change my daily habits so that “surrender”becomes easier for me?

Why do I start sincerely… but stop halfway?

A man wanted to climb a hill.

On the first day, he rushed up with full enthusiasm but got tired quickly. On the second day, he tried again… and stopped halfway. On the third day, he almost gave up.

An old shepherd watching him said, “You are climbing with excitement, not with rhythm.

Take smaller steps. Walk slowly. But don’t break the flow.”

The next day, the man followed his advice by following small steps, slow pace, steady rhythm. He reached the top peacefully.

Surrender also needs rhythm, not force. Small habits, not big jumps.

Some simple steps:

Do very small spiritual actions that you can continue daily.

• Not 30 minutes of chanting. Start with 1 minute. Small habits don’t scare the mind. They keep the connection alive.

Replace “I must finish” with “Let me just begin”

• When you focus on beginning, the pressure reduces.The mind is more relaxed… and surrender becomes possible.

Use “micro-pauses”

• Before starting the car, opening an email, or entering a stressful meeting, just pause for two seconds and say: “You guide me.” This tiny pause shifts the mind from control → trust.

Drop one small worry each day

• Not all worries.Just one.

This slowly teaches the mind to release.

Keep one anchor practice

• Even if everything else fails in the day, do one tiny thing , a short Nama chanting, a prayer, or a deep breath. This keeps the heart connected.

Essence

Surrender doesn’t come by doing big things once. It comes by doing small things consistently. Small steps create a soft mind. And a soft mind surrenders naturally.

Thought for the Day

Take smaller steps, stay steady, and let the heart soften because surrender grows quietly in simple, daily habits.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 34 |  | Two very different ways of surrender - Monkey & kitten type | 
A monkey clings tightly to its mother as she jumps from branch to branch. If the mother lets go, the baby monkey would fall. The baby survives because it holds on with all its might. This is the first type of surrender : clinging surrender. We see it in children holding their mother’s hand when learning to cross the street,

or a nervous person holding tightly to their routines, needing reassurance. It is sincere, but the grip is tight, anxious, and dependent.

The second type is like a kitten surrendering to its mother. The kitten doesn’t cling desperately; it relaxes completely, trusting that the mother will carry it, feed it, and keep it safe. The kitten’s surrender is calm, effortless, and full of trust. It doesn’t struggle or panic; it simply lets go. This is the higher surrender, letting go of fear and control, knowing we are supported.

In life, we often start as monkeys: tight, anxious, holding onto habits, expectations, and outcomes.

With practice, devotion, and trust, we learn to become kittens: relaxed, gentle, and confident that life and God are guiding us.

Essence / Do’s and Don’ts

Do:

Trust life, little by little. Practice small acts of letting go each day. Observe your tight grips and release gently.

Don’t Force surrender ; it grows naturally. Don’t Confuse clinging with surrender ; holding too tight blocks trust. Dont’s Judge yourself when your mind slips back to monkey-mode.

Thought for the Day

May my heart start as a monkey clinging, and slowly learn to be a kitten fully relaxed, fully trusting, fully surrendered.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 33 |  | If God is taking care of this whole universe effortlessly, why is surrender… | 
If God is taking care of this whole universe effortlessly, why is surrender so hard for us?

This question sits quietly in every heart. Belief is easy. Letting go is difficult , not because God is far, but because our mind holds too tightly.

A man once crossed a river in a boat with a heavy stone on his head. Someone asked, “Why not put it down?” He said, “If I drop it, the boat may sink.” The other man smiled: “The boat carries you. It can carry your stone too.” Just like that, we carry fears, guilt, and expectations as if life cannot handle them. Surrender is simply placing the stone down , not throwing it away but just loosening the mind.

Another day, a father taught his son to drive. The boy held the steering wheel so tightly that his knuckles turned white. The father whispered, “Relax your grip. The car listens better to calm hands than to frightened hands.” This too is surrender, still driving, but with trust instead of tension.

And like a small child learning to cycle shouting, “Don’t leave me!”, the father says gently, “I’m right here.” Surrender is that gentle trust.

What surrender really means, Not giving up effort, Not stopping work, Not abandoning responsibility. Surrender means doing your best without fear tightening your heart. It is breathing out after holding your breath too long.

A small shift… yet a huge relief.

Essence

Surrender is not dropping responsibilities. It is dropping the unnecessary fear inside the responsibility.

Thought for the Day

Real surrender begins the moment you stop gripping life with fear and start trusting the hands that are already holding you.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 32 |  | If Sattva, Rajas and Tamas quietly sit inside all of us… why do we loudly… | 
If Sattva, Rajas and Tamas quietly sit inside all of us… why do we loudly debate which vegetable carries which label?

Some people follow strict rules:”No onion, no garlic, no this, no that.”

Others live more relaxed: “Eat well, pray well, sleep well… life is simple.”

Both groups have tradition. Both have logic.Both have sincerity. And both often forget one gentle truth given by our own scriptures: Gunas are mainly in the mind… food only supports what is already inside.

Our tradition never said that food alone decides your spiritual level. It only said: “Choose what helps your mind stay light and clear.”

Sattva, Rajas, Tamas are not enemies.They are simply energies: (1) Sattva is clarity; (2) Rajas is movement; (3) Tamas is rest. All three are needed.All three live within us. Even saints had all three just in balance.

So instead of asking, “Is this potato sattvic or not?”, a more helpful question is, “How is my mind right now?”. This is not against tradition , this is the deeper layer of tradition.

How do we see our own gunas?

Very simple:

When your thoughts are calm → Sattva

When your thoughts are fast → Rajas

When your thoughts are dull → Tamas

When your words soothe → Sattva

When your words rush → Rajas

When your words hurt → Tamas

This inner checking is what our saints meant when they said: “Lift yourself from within.”

There is a beautiful story. A devotee asked a Guru: “Swamiji, is garlic tamasic?. The Guru smiled: “Yes. But ego cooked in any oil is more tamasic.” The devotee understood: First clean the mind, then the menu.

Essence

Respect the rules as they are sacred. Respect the differences that each body and mind is unique.Respect the gunas as they guide our inner journey.Respect bhakti as it purifies everything it touches.

Above all, remember: Spirituality is choosing what brings Sattva into your mind, not what brings arguments to your dining table.

Thought for the Day

Eat with awareness. Live with balance. Act with clarity. Love with fullness. That is true Sattva.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 31 |  | Have you ever wondered how the same song can feel completely different in a… | 
Have you ever wondered how the same song can feel completely different in a Carnatic concert and in a Namasankeerthanam?

This is not about which is better. It is simply a reflection on how two lovely traditions touch different corners of our inner world.

A Carnatic concert is like admiring a painting.

Namasankeerthanam is like stepping into the painting. Same composition. Different doorway.

A concert aims for excellence in raga, tala, sangatis, and manodharma. Namasankeerthanam invites experience i.e., bhava, surrender, shared singing, and sensing the saint’s heartbeat behind the words. One is performance. The other is participation.

At the centre of Namasankeerthanam is bhakti which is a simple, honest turning of the heart towards the Divine.Music without bhakti touches only the ear; music with bhakti touches the soul.

And bhakti does not arrive through perfect sangatis. It arrives through sincerity humility, gratitude, and an open heart. When the heart softens, devotion naturally flows into the music.

Sometimes, in our enthusiasm, we turn Namasankeerthanam into a small exam: “Is the raga exact? Did I miss a sangati?”

Learning well is good, but saints didn’t compose for marks. They composed to melt hearts. A lyric without bhava is like a recipe without salt which may be correct, but incomplete.

The moods differ too. In a concert, we sit quietly and admire the musician. In Namasankeerthanam, we sing, sway, clap, forget ourselves sometimes forget tala too, yet feel closer to God. One refines your musical taste. The other refines your inner being.

Tyagaraja reminds us: “Music without bhakti is just sound.”

Namasankeerthanam is that same sound with a heart added. And when Carnatic music is offered with humility, it becomes worship too.

No higher, no lower , just two different climbs up the same sacred mountain.

Essence

Carnatic concert is music for the mind. Namasankeerthanam is music for the soul. Bhakti is the gentle thread that turns both into a bridge to the Divine.

Thought for the Day

A saint’s song truly enters us not when the raga is perfect, but when the heart opens.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 30 |  | Am I growing inside… or just running around outside? | 
Many of us really love spirituality. Morning yoga, Gita class, chanting, many online classes for shlokas and parayana, learning bhajan kirtans, temple visit, nama writing… and of course a phone full of spiritual WhatsApp messages and TV channels like Sankara TV, Bhakthi TV etc . It shows a very sincere heart that wants to stay close to God throughout the day.

But sometimes, without realising, the mind becomes very busy with spiritual things… instead of becoming peaceful because of spiritual things.

So it helps to ask ourselves gently: “Is all this making me calm… or just keeping me occupied?”

There is nothing wrong in doing many practices. In fact, it shows devotion and enthusiasm. But spirituality is not a competition of how much we do; it is a journey of how deeply we feel.

You may attend ten programs in a day… but the real question is: “Did at least one moment touch my heart today?”

The Gita says yoga is steadiness, not speed. Shankara reminds us that depth matters more than quantity. Tyagaraja shows that devotion should melt the heart, not overload the timetable.

Once a devotee happily told a saint, “Swami, I attend so many classes every day!” The saint smiled and said, “Very good. Now just check… did your mind become a little softer today?” That one question itself created inner awareness.

That is the power of self-reflection: it never scolds; it gently guides.

Essence

Spiritual activity is wonderful. But spiritual absorption is even more beautiful. Let your mind become calm, your heart become tender, and your thoughts become clear. Even one sincere moment with God can do what many busy moments cannot.

Thought for the Day

Do as many good things as you enjoy… but let at least one of them take you inward. A peaceful mind is the real sign of spiritual growth.

@@ 29 |  | How to Love Fully and Live Lightly? | 
Many elders spend their whole life raising children by packing tiffin, stopping fights, running behind school, and adjusting to everything. After all this, one truth slowly comes out: THE HEART NEVER RETIRES.

So naturally, the same love that ran behind small children now runs behind grandchildren… and if God is very kind, even great-grandchildren. Not because someone demands it, but because a heart that has cared for 40 years cannot suddenly say, “From today, I will think only of myself.” If only life was that simple!

In most homes, a funny pattern happens. Just when life becomes a little free… new worries quietly knock on the door.One child’s small exam…One grandchild’s tiny cough…One relative’s big emotional flood…

People change, but the “worry basket” fills up again every day. Like temple lamps, it never goes off. And honestly, this is beautiful. Loving is beautiful. Helping others is dharma.

But one small mistake happens. The mind forgets to take care of itself.

A saint once asked a devotee, “You pray daily for 14 people. Wonderful! But where is your name in the list?” The devotee smiled shyly and said, “Swami, I removed myself long ago.” We all laugh… because we all know this story is about us.

Great saints never said, “Stop loving your family.” They only said, “Love them fully, but don’t forget God. And don’t forget yourself. Ask for wisdom and devotion and those are the only things that travel with you even after all duties are over.

Family responsibilities will slowly reduce one day. But inner peace starts today, when the mind gets even a few minutes to breathe for itself.

Slowly shift from: “Let everything go well for them… to …..Let me also grow inside.”

Your blessings can continue. Your support can continue. Your love can continue. Only the worry needs early retirement.

Essence

Love your family deeply. Serve with joy. But keep your mind free and light, and remember God. When love stays and worry goes, a normal householder becomes a spiritual seeker.

Thought for the Day

Love with your whole heart. But keep one small corner of your mind for God, and for your own soul. Life becomes softer when your prayers are not only for others… but also gently for you.

@@ 28 |  | Money, Comforts, and Spirituality - A Gentle Reminder | 
In every generation, as students, working professionals, homemakers, businesspeople, and elders we all carry one silent belief: “If I have a little more money, I will finally feel safe.”

But strangely even when money increases, the fear rarely reduces.

A young professional thinks:

“Let me earn well first… then I will live peacefully.”

A middle-aged parent thinks:

“Let me settle the children… then I can relax.”

A retired person thinks:

“Let me save this for emergencies… what if something happens tomorrow?”

Different ages… same fear… same tight fist.

A college student once said, “After exams I will relax.” After exams came placements. After placements came workload. After workload came EMIs. Relaxation kept shifting like a moving target.

A software engineer shared, “I earn ten times more than my first job. But my mind worries fifteen times more.” Money changed. But the mind didn’t.

A mother once received a small gift voucher.She wanted to buy a dress she liked… but said, “Let me use it for the children.”The children used and bought a dress for her. She cried and said, “I forgot that I also deserve something.”

He had saved all his life for travel. After retirement he said,

“Maybe next year… what if expenses come?” Three years passed. His health changed. Finally he said, “I saved for travel, but I forgot to travel.”

The Human Pattern

At any age, money quietly becomes a security blanket. We hold it tightly as if Lakshmi Devi will run away the moment we loosen our grip.

A saint humorously said:

“Some people hold money so tightly that Lakshmi Devi wonders, “If they don’t let me move, how will I bring more?’”

Another wise teacher said:

“If you hold water tightly, it escapes. If you hold it gently, it stays.”

Spirituality never says don’t earn or don’t enjoy comforts. It simply says: Let money be in your hand, not in your heart.

The Upanishads declare:

“tyagenaike amritatvam anashuh” . Only through letting go can we taste true joy.

Letting go doesn’t mean irresponsibility.It means trust. It means remembering that:

Abundance is not only in the account. It is in the attitude. Peace does not come from money. It comes from how we relate to money.

The Essence

Saving is wise.Planning is good.Comforts are not wrong. But living in constant fear of “not enough” shrinks our life. Life should not become a museum of worries. Life should be a garden of freedom. When you give, even a little, your heart feels full. When you treat yourself with simple joy, your mind relaxes. When you trust life, life supports you.

Thought for the Day

Don’t hold money as if it is your life. Hold life as if it is your greatest wealth.When your hand opens to give, your heart opens to receive abundance.
