@@ 27 |  | Why do I keep slipping back into my old habits even after I honestly try to… | 
Why do I keep slipping back into my old habits even after I honestly try to change? Why do I still get angry, even after so many satsangs?

Have you noticed this funny pattern?

We decide, “From tomorrow, no anger.” Next morning, someone tests us and the vow dissolves before breakfast.

We promise, “I will wake up early.” But at 5 am, the blanket becomes a sacred cave of comfort.

We think, “I will stop overthinking.” Five minutes later, we are overthinking about why we overthink.

This happens even to sincere seekers. Even saints have smiled at this universal human struggle.

Habits are like employees who joined 20 years ago and they don’t retire because you gave one motivational speech!. The mind takes its old route like a bus that refuses to change its usual road. This doesn’t mean you are weak and it means your habits are very experienced.

Change always happens in layers, not leaps. Growth is never like : “Today decision → Tomorrow enlightenment.”

It is always like : try → slip → try again → slip less → try again → slip rarely

Even climbing a hill means

one step up and two deep breaths down. But you are still climbing. The mind loves the familiar even if it is foolish. The mind says:

“Anger? Ah, I know this room.”

“Worry? Very comfortable sofa.”

“Laziness? Home sweet home.”

New habits feel risky. Old habits feel “safe.” So the mind pulls you back like an overprotective, outdated security guard. Not to punish you , just out of old loyalty.

Gita says : “The mind is friend when trained, enemy when untrained.”

Upanishads say : “As you practice, so you become.”

Tamil saints say : “Old habits trouble; new habits uplift.”

No scripture promises instant change. All scriptures promise possible change. Slipping is actually progress. Only those who walk would fall. People who never move, never slip.

Earlier you got angry 20 times a day. Now only 5 times.

You call it failure. A saint calls it evolution. The very fact that you notice your slip means your awareness has grown.

How to handle old habits gently?

• Don’t scold yourself. Self-judgment is fertilizer for the old habit.

• Reduce frequency, don’t chase perfection. One step less is still victory.

• Replace the habit; don’t fight it. Darkness goes by bringing light.

• Celebrate tiny wins. Encouragement grows the mind.

• Invite grace. Your effort + Divine support = transformation.

Essence

You slip because the habit is old. You rise because you are evolving. Growth is a dance: one step forward, one step back but always moving upward.

Thought for the Day

Falling is not failure. Rising again is spiritual victory. Each rise creates a new future inside you.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 26 |  | Why is forgiveness (Kshama) so difficult ? Even when I know I should… | 
Why is forgiveness (Kshama) so difficult ? Even when I know I should forgive, why does the hurt stay inside me? Why can’t I forget what someone said or did, even when my mind says ‘let it go’?”

This is not weakness.This is human nature. Imagine a thorn pricks your foot. You remove the thorn immediately.But the wound remains tender for a few days. If someone touches that spot, even lightly,it hurts again. Forgiveness works exactly like this.

The thorn is the hurtful event. Forgiveness is removing the thorn.But the emotional wound takes time to heal. Just because the wound is still tender does not mean

you have not forgiven. It only means healing is still happening.

Many people feel guilty that they have not forgiven. But in reality, they have removed the thorn and the wound simply needs time. Forgiveness is not a one-minute action. It is a gradual healing.

The Bhagavad Gita says:

“Kshama, forgiveness, is the strength of the strong.”

Our saints say:

“Forgiveness is not forgetting the past.It is refusing to let the past control your peace.”

The Upanishads tell us:

“Whatever you hold in your heart, you relive again and again.”

Holding anger is like carrying a burning coal, hoping the other person will feel the heat.But only our hand burns. Forgiveness releases this burning coal so that our heart can cool down.

Why is forgiveness so hard ? Because, We replay the incident many times. Ego wants an explanation or apology. The mind holds on to “I am right, they are wrong”. We mix today’s hurt with old memories. We think forgiveness means weakness. We expect the other person to change. All of this keeps the wound open.

Forgiveness becomes easier when we realise that Forgiveness is not for the other person. It is for our peace.

We don’t forgive because they deserve it.We forgive because we deserve peace.

How to forgive in real life?

Stop replaying the incident ,each replay keeps the wound fresh. Say in your heart: “I release this for my peace.” Don’t wait for them to apologise and forgiveness is your decision. Separate the person from the behaviour and sometimes good people make mistakes. Pray for clarity and not for their change. Give time as healing is not instant. Slowly, the wound becomes lighter. Then smaller. Then disappears.

Forgiveness does not happen in one day but peace return step by step.

Essence

Forgiveness does not mean forgetting the hurt. It means choosing not to carry it. You remove the thorn, and then give the wound time to heal.

Thought for the Day

Forgive not because the past deserves it… but because your peace deserves it. Forgiveness is the gift you give yourself.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 25 |  | I keep hearing about karma… but what does it really mean in my daily life? | 
I keep hearing about karma… but what does it really mean in my daily life?

And if everything is ‘my karma’, then what is God doing in all this?

This question troubles everybody, even those who never talk about it openly.

Imagine you have a bank account that you yourself created over many years.

Every day, knowingly or unknowingly, you:

• deposit (good actions, kind words, honest behaviour)

• withdraw (anger, hurting someone, selfishness, carelessness)

Now one day you look at your balance and feel upset: “Why is there so little money?” But the bank is not at fault. The balance reflects our own past deposits and withdrawals.

Karma is exactly like this account. Life gives us the “balance” we ourselves have built over past actions, words, intentions, and choices. Not as punishment, but as natural consequence.

When we help someone, we deposit peace. When we hurt someone, we withdraw peace. When we act selflessly, we deposit blessings. When we act carelessly, we create dues.

Karma is not God sitting with a punishment book. Karma is like gravity neutral, automatic, and universal.

So where does God come into this?

This is the most important point.Karma gives the situation. God gives the strength.Karma gives the exam paper. God gives the courage, clarity, and grace to write it well.Karma brings the rain.

God gives the umbrella.Karma brings the challenge. God walks with you through it. Karma is the law. Grace is the support. We are never alone.

The Bhagavad Gita says:

“Past actions create circumstances; present actions create your future.”

How does this help us in daily life?

When we understand karma, We stop blaming God, We stop feeling “Why me?”, We start taking responsibility, We realise we can change our future by today’s choices, We become more careful with words and behaviour, We become calmer when difficulties come, We start depositing more peace into our inner account. Karma is not about fear. Karma is about awareness.

Every thought, word, and action is like a small transaction creating our emotional, mental, and spiritual balance sheet.

Essence

Life is not random. What comes to us is karma; how we face it is grace; and what we create next is our choice. Karma shapes the path. Grace holds our hand.

Thought for the Day

Don’t be afraid of karma but understand it. Each moment is a fresh chance to create new harmony, new goodness, and a new future. Your past may explain your present, but your present will write your tomorrow.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 24 |  | How does satsang really help me in my spiritual evolution? | 
And can Namasankeerthanam truly cure the problems I face in daily life?

We attend satsangs… we listen… we feel uplifted for a while… but then daily life pulls us back. So what is actually happening inside us?

Satsang is the ‘company of truth’ and company shapes character Even a quiet person becomes noisy if they stay in a noisy place. Even a restless person becomes calm if they spend time in a peaceful place. Satsang works the same way.

Satsang means, minds seeking truth ; hearts seeking peace ; people seeking goodness ; thoughts flowing upward

When you sit in such a vibration, your mind automatically absorbs purity just like the body absorbs sunshine.

You don’t always “learn” in satsang. Sometimes… you simply catch the energy.

Satsang weakens the negative tendencies silently. You don’t see it immediately, but slowly: anger reduces; ego softens ; fear loses grip ; jealousy drops ; restlessness becomes lighter ; clarity increases

Satsang does not change you dramatically. It changes you naturally, like seasons.

One day you realise: “I don’t react like before. Something has shifted.” That “something” is Satsang

Without Satsang, the mind quickly becomes heavy. The mind is like a room. If you don’t open the windows, dust settles, air becomes stale, light becomes dull. Satsang is the opening of the windows. Fresh air enters. Light comes in. Dust flies out. Even 1 hour of satsang cleans 10 hours of stress.

Can Namasankeerthanam really cure problems?

The simple answer: Yes but not by changing the problem, but by changing you first.

—> The Name touches a part of you that worries cannot reach

A thought enters the mind. A mantra enters the heart.Worry agitates. Nama settles.

When the mind repeats the Divine Name: breath slows, heartbeat softens, stress drops, thinking becomes clear, panic dissolves

Nama does not remove the storm outside, but it removes the storm inside. When the inner storm calms, the outer storm becomes manageable

—> Nama breaks negative mental patterns

Anger → Nama → gap

Fear → Nama → gap

Overthinking → Nama → gap

That “gap” is where transformation happens.When you chant,your mind cannot simultaneously overthink. Nama is the switch that turns off mental noise.

—> Nama pushes divine grace into daily life

Saints say: “Wherever the Name resides, Grace flows naturally.”

Problems shrink. Paths open. Confusion reduces. Help comes at the right time. Nama is not magic.Nama is magnet. It makes your mind a magnet

that attracts God’s timely support.

—> Nama is the ultimate medicine because it treats the real cause which is the restless mind

Every problem has 2 parts: The external situation & The mind’s reaction to it

Nama melts the reaction.Once the reaction softens, the situation becomes solvable. That is why saints say: “All problems become smaller in front of the Name.”

The Bhagavata says: “Kirtanam is the purifier for this age.”

The Gita says:

“Among sacrifices, I am Japa.”

Bodendra Sadguru says: Nama is the boat that crosses all obstacles.”

Thyagaraja Swami says:

“Nama removes sorrow even before sorrow arrives.”

Our tradition is clear. Nama is not a technique . Nama is the path and the protection.

Essence

Satsang refines the mind. Nama dissolves the mind’s heaviness. Both work together like soap and water:

Satsang = soap that lifts the dirt . Nama = water that washes it away. A mind that is regularly in Satsang and regularly in Nama cannot remain in pain for long.

Thought for the Day

Satsang lifts you up; Nama holds you there. Together, they make the mind light, the heart pure, and life itself manageable.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 23 |  | A learned bhagavatha sent me a guiding input through my friend :- Please… | 
A learned bhagavatha sent me a guiding input through my friend :- Please explain upanishad vakyam “Sarvam kalvidham brahma” .. this can benefit everybody to understand the basics of spirituality.

I further expanded his question “if the Upanishads say “Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma”, which means everything is God, then why do I feel like just a normal person dealing with daily mundane life? If everything is divine, then why does my life still feel like ordinary day-to-day living? Who am I really in all this?”

A question that seems simple,

but carries the heart of all Vedanta.Let us understand it through three famous analogies (which we all remember from our Vedanta class) which many of us can relate to.

1. The clay and the pots

2. The gold and the ornaments

3. The ocean and the waves.

The Clay and the Pots

Many pots sit in a workshop.Each pot thinks, “I am tall… I am small… I am colourful… I am plain.” A child taps them and says, “You are not the pot.You are the clay inside every pot.”The pot may break, but the clay remains the same untouched. Just like our body, moods, age, success and failure change but the real “I” , the inner presence does not change.

The Gold and the Ornaments

A bangle thinks it is round.A chain thinks it is long.A ring thinks it is tiny. But a goldsmith laughs: “All of you are only gold. Your shapes differ, but your substance is the same. Our roles differ as parent, student, employee, friend.But our essence, the divine spark inside is the same.

The Ocean and the Waves

A wave rises proudly and falls fearfully. It cries, “I am born… I live… I die.” The ocean lovingly says, “You are not the wave. You are the water in every wave.” Our thoughts rise and fall just like waves. But the awareness behind them is steady like the ocean.

How does this apply to us in our daily life?

Very simple.Every day you wake up as a person with tasks, routines, deadlines, hopes, worries.The clay–pot story reminds us that “You are more than your outer life. You are the inner substance.” When someone compares themselves with others, “She is better than me… he is richer than me…”the gold-jewellery story whispers:Shapes differ, but the gold is the same in all.

You don’t have to compete. When life gives ups and downs some days peaceful, some days stressful, the wave-ocean story tells you: Your experiences change,but the real You remains calm and untouched.

These stories are not meant to stay a philosophy.They are mirrors showing you that beneath the everyday noise of life, there is a quiet, divine presence in you that never gets old, never gets hurt, never gets lost.

This is the meaning of Sarvam Khalvidam Brahma : There is only One Essence appearing as many lives, many bodies, many minds including yours.

Essence

You are not the outer pot; you are the clay. You are not the changing ornament; you are the gold.You are not the rising and falling wave; you are the water.Forms change.Essence stays.And that essence is you.

Thought for the Day

You may live an ordinary looking life,but the One Divine Reality lives in you as your true Self.When you remember this,

even your simplest day becomes sacred, and every step becomes light.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 22 |  | Someone asked me: “Why do we make such a big deal of 60, 70, 80, 90 and 100… | 
Someone asked me: “Why do we make such a big deal of 60, 70, 80, 90 and 100 Are these numbers spiritual… or just cultural celebrations?”

To answer this, our tradition points to the powerful story of King Yayati in the Mahabharata.

Yayati lived life to the fullest with power, pleasure, achievement, everything. But when old age hit him suddenly, he was shaken. After deep reflection, he realised something brutally honest: “Pleasure has a limit. Desire does not” That one realisation changed him. He accepted ageing, and said life actually has five inner turning points, not defined by age but by clarity.

Interestingly, these five match our Indian milestones of 60, 70, 80, 90 and 100. Let me put this in words that every one of us can relate to.

At 60 - Shashti : The Mind Shifts From Accumulating to Understanding. Something changes around 60. Not in the body but in the priorities. You no longer ask, “How much more can I get?”. You quietly start asking, “What actually matters now?”. A subtle turning inward begins. You don’t need noise anymore. You don’t need applause. You want clarity.It is not old age. It is maturity catching up with ambition.

At 70 - Bheemaratha Shanthi: You Realise Peace Is More Powerful Than Proving. Your 40s and 50s were spent telling the world who you are. At 70, something surprising happens: You stop reacting so quickly. Arguments don’t tempt you. You would rather keep friendships than win fights. You finally understand that being right is less important than being peaceful. This shift is why 70 is celebrated.

At 80 -Sathabhishekam: Your Presence Becomes Medicine. By 80, people don’t come to elders for advice.They come for something deep which is the reassurance that life can be survived,digested,and understood. At 80, your presence itself becomes a blessing. You don’t have to speak much.Your very being tells others, “It is okay. Life works out.” This is why the 80-year milestone is sacred.

At 90 -Navathi: The Ego Quietly Retires. By 90, something rare happens.You no longer feel the urge to correct people.You don’t cling to opinions.You don’t take things personally. You don’t get offended easily. Not because you are weak, but because you have seen too much of life to waste energy on the small stuff.A quiet softness appears.This humility itself is spirituality.

At 100 -Shatamanam; Life Feels Larger Than Personal Stories. Reaching 100 is not about the number of years. It is about reaching a state where you see the bigger picture. You realise that most things you worried about were not worth worrying about. People you loved mattered more than anything else. And life was always held together by something mysterious and kind.At 100, one becomes less of a “person” and more of a presence.

Essence

Our rishis did not celebrate age.They celebrated the inner shifts that age brings.

• 60 ,Priorities change

• 70 ,Peace becomes strength

• 80 ,Presence becomes healing

• 90 ,Ego becomes quiet

• 100 Life becomes complete

Age is not decline. Age is distillation.

Thought for the Day

Growing older doesn’t reduce life.It refines it into wisdom, softness and grace.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

PS: I have read something interesting that at the age of 60, people start their inner realisation and attain brahma swaroopam at 70 , Rudra swaroopam at 80 and Vishnu swaroopam at 90..

will share more on this in future TFD.

@@ 21 |  | One of our young satsang team member sent me this beautiful question: “If I… | 
One of our young satsang team member sent me this beautiful question: “If I surrender to God, should I stop wanting anything? And if I stop wanting, how will I work towards my goals?”

This is not just her doubt. It is the confusion with many of us. Let us look at it simply.

Surrender does NOT mean stopping all desires .If a seed has no desire to grow, will it become a tree? If a child has no desire to learn, will he go to school? If a devotee has no desire for a good life, why would he even pray?. Desire is not the problem. The problem is the anxiety behind the desire.

Saints never told us to stop wanting. They told us to stop worrying. Andal longed for Perumal. Meera longed for Krishna.Thyagaraja longed for Rama. Their desire did not block surrender.Their desire BECAME surrender. They wanted but with devotion, not desperation.

Surrender means: “I will do my best, and I trust God for the rest.” A farmer ploughs, sows, waters…but knows he cannot pull the crop out of the soil. Sunshine and rain are not in his hands.Life works the same way. We put effort.We keep goals.We work sincerely. But the final result we leave in His hands. That “letting go” is surrender.

Without desires, you cannot walk. Without surrender, you cannot breathe peacefully. Desire gives direction. Surrender gives peace. Both are needed.

Desire without surrender becomes stress. Surrender without desire becomes laziness.But desire WITH surrender becomes devotion.

A boy once told his Guru,

“Swami, I am confused. Should I run towards my goal or surrender to God?” The Guru smiled and said, “Run towards your goal…but let God hold the steering wheel.” That is the balance we all need.

God never asked you to stop wanting. He asked you to stop fearing. When you surrender, you don’t give up your dreams. You give up your tension. You still work hard. You still plan.You still aspire. But deep inside, you whisper: “Bhagavan, You know what is best for me. Guide me in the right direction.” And that trust moves mountains.

Essence

Surrender does not cancel effort.It removes the fear behind effort.Desire moves your feet; surrender calms your heart.Together, they take you to God’s plan beautifully.

Thought for the Day

Surrender is not giving up your goals. It is giving up your anxiety about the result. Work with devotion, want with clarity, and leave the outcome gently in God’s hands.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 20 |  | If we truly trust God, why do we see horoscopes, look for good times, or do… | 
A devotee asked me a very practical question: “If we truly trust God, why do we see horoscopes, look for good times, or do parihara? Isn’t that contradictory?”

A beautiful doubt which every seeker carries silently. Trusting God and using astrology are not opposites. They are different stages of the same journey.

When a child is small, the mother holds his hand. When he grows, she walks beside him. When he matures, she trusts he will walk on his own. Our faith develops exactly the same way.

When trust is young, we look for outer guidance like horoscopes, poojas, timings. As trust deepens, we feel God within, and dependence on outer supports naturally reduces.

The Vedas call astrology Jyotisha Shastra, the “eye of the Vedas.” It is like a weather report for life. If rain is predicted, you don’t curse the rain but you carry an umbrella. Astrology shows challenges;

Parihara is that umbrella. It cannot stop the rain, but it helps us walk through it with balance and grace. And the real power is not in the ritual, but in the bhava ,the feeling of surrender. A calm heart receives God’s grace much faster than a fearful one.

Our acharyas said: “Daivam anukoolam, purushakaram pradhanam.” God’s grace is always present, but effort done with devotion completes the journey.

A simple example: When you go on a journey, you pray, “God, keep me safe.” That is faith. You also check the road and carry a spare tyre. That is wisdom. Faith does not mean ignoring intelligence and it means using both.

How should a devotee approach this? Use astrology for guidance, not fear. Do parihara with surrender, not anxiety. Let faith be stronger than doubt. Move gently from ritual to inner remembrance.

Saints beautifully said: Karma brings the fruit, but Bhakti brings the sweetness in the fruit. Even when we do remedies, let the heart whisper: “Bhagavan, I trust You to do what is best.” That trust itself becomes the highest worship.

Essence

Horoscopes and remedies are outer helps. Faith and surrender are inner strength.

When both join, life becomes smooth. When faith deepens, even the stars bow down.

Thought for the Day

Faith in God is not ignoring guidance ; it means using guidance with trust, not fear. Astrology may show the map, but God decides the road. And a surrendered heart walks safely in all times.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 19 |  | I keep hearing people say “I am not religious, I am spiritual”. Some say… | 
A satsang member asked me :

“I keep hearing people say “I am not religious, I am spiritual”. Some say being religious means rituals, and being spiritual means freedom. I am a bit confused… what is the real difference between being spiritual and being religious?”

This is a beautiful question because both religion and spirituality are like two sides of the same coin. One is the outer journey, the other is the inner journey.

Religion teaches us how to start the journey with prayer, rituals, discipline, and moral values. Spirituality shows us how to live that journey with love, awareness, and surrender. Both are needed. Like a lamp and its flame ; religion is the lamp, spirituality is the light inside it.

Many people think religion is about rules and spirituality is about freedom. But the truth is religion gives structure, spirituality gives spirit. Religion teaches us to bow our head before God. Spirituality teaches us to open our heart to God. Religion helps us move from chaos to discipline. Spirituality helps us move from discipline to devotion. Religion is the path, spirituality is the destination. Religion is the body, spirituality is the soul.

Once a devotee asked his Guru, “Why do we need rituals if God is in the heart?”. The Guru smiled and said, “When a child learns to write, he uses four lines note book. Later, he doesn’t need them. But without those four lines, he could never have learned to write properly.” In the same way, religion gives the lines, spirituality gives the wisdom to write beautifully. The Vedas say, “ Yajnad bhavati parjanyah”, rituals and prayers bring discipline and purity. The Gita says, “Manmana bhava madbhakto” fill that purity with love for God. So, first we clean the vessel (through religion), and then we fill it with nectar (through spirituality).

How to Balance Both religion and spirituality in Daily Life

Respect Rituals, but Go Beyond the Form: When you light a lamp, don’t just do it because it is a habit. Feel that you are lighting the lamp of wisdom in your heart.

Chant God’s Name with Feeling: Don’t chant just to complete a count. Chant because you love Him. Even one sincere “Govinda” said with tears is greater than a thousand said mechanically.

Practice Values, Not Just Customs: Following fasting or visiting temples is good but following kindness, truth, and patience is greater.

Connect Head and Heart: Religion teaches the right actions. Spirituality teaches the right attitude. When both meet, life becomes sacred.

A beautiful saying explains it simply: Religion is when you believe in God. Spirituality is when you feel God. Religion tells you where to look for God.

Spirituality helps you see Him everywhere.

Essence

Religion brings you to the temple. Spirituality brings the temple into your heart. Religion makes you follow God’s path.

Spirituality makes you walk with God Himself.

Thought for the Day

Don’t choose between being religious or spiritual. Be both and follow the discipline of religion, and fill it with the love of spirituality. Then every ritual becomes real, and every moment becomes divine.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 18 |  | I read your posts every day. I sing Namasankeerthanam daily. When I sing, I… | 
A friend asked me lovingly:

“I read your posts every day. I sing Namasankeerthanam daily. When I sing, I feel lost in joy like I am not even here. But I don’t do formal rituals, I don’t chant many slokas, and I don’t read the Gita or Bhagavatam regularly. I just love singing Bhagwan’s name for hours. Am I really spiritual? Should I be doing something more?”

This is such an honest and beautiful question.

The answer lies in your own experience. When your heart melts while singing the Lord’s name, that melting itself is spirituality. Spirituality begins when the heart turns to God, not when the mouth follows rules. Our scriptures say clearly: Harer namaiva kevalam In this age, Nama alone is enough. Saints like Namdev, Tukaram, and Chaitanya never sat with textbooks. They sang. Their tears became their Gita. Their music became their tapas. Rituals, slokas, scriptures these are all beautiful, but they are different doors to the same home. If your door is Namasankeerthanam, then walk through it with your whole heart.

When you sing the Lord’s name with love, you are already doing bhakti yoga, the highest path of devotion. The purpose of all scriptures is to bring the same love that you already feel. Sri Ramakrishna Paramahamsa said: “If you cry for God sincerely even for one day, it is worth more than reading a thousand scriptures without feeling.” The real measure of spirituality is bhav that softness, that longing, that inner pull toward God.

So yes , you are truly spiritual, because spirituality is not about how many rituals you perform; it is about how deeply you remember Him.

What can you do further?

You don’t need to do more. You only need to go deeper. In my humble opinion, here are a few gentle ways to enrich your Nama bhakti:

Before singing: Close your eyes for a moment and think,

“O Krishna, these songs are my flowers for You.” This turns singing into offering, not performance. After singing: Sit quietly for two minutes. Let the vibration settle. That silence is God’s presence. Sometimes read one verse or story from the Gita or Bhagavatam. Don’t study it like a student. Read it like Krishna is speaking to you. It adds depth to your bhakti. Chant softly during daily activities: While cooking, walking, or even bathing, whisper “Govinda… Govinda…” Slowly the joy of bhajan spreads into the whole day.

Once a devotee told a saint, “I don’t know Sanskrit or rituals. I just love singing God’s name.”

The saint smiled: “When sugar melts in your mouth, do you need a chemistry book to enjoy it?” When the Name melts your heart, God is already with you.

Essence

Rituals, chanting, scriptures are all paths. But the goal is one: to feel God’s presence with love and surrender. If your heart dances when you sing His name, you are already walking the highest path, “Prema Bhakti”.

Thought for the Day

You don’t have to do everything to reach God. Just do what fills your heart with His presence. If Namasankeerthanam makes you forget yourself and remember Him, that is true spirituality.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 17 |  | A friend asked me, “If God is everywhere, why do I need a Guru? | 
Many people have this doubt but feel shy to ask. It is a very natural and honest question.

Inside all of us, three small voices talk:

Voice 1: “I can manage life. Why do I need anyone’s help?”

Voice 2: “What if I trust the wrong person?”

Voice 3: “I want guidance…but I don’t know whom to trust.”

This confusion is not a problem. It is actually the first sign that our heart is growing.

There is a story of a traveler carrying a lantern. He tells a lost man, “I can show only your next few steps. When the sun comes, you won’t need this lantern.” This is what a Guru does. He helps us walk safely until our own inner light becomes strong.

A young man went trekking. He felt confident because he had Google Maps and a compass. But suddenly fog came. His compass stopped working. GPS failed. He got scared. Then he saw an old shepherd walking calmly and the shepherd said, “Just follow my footsteps. I know this place.”

In life too, when our mind becomes confused, a Guru is like that shepherd.

A child once asked his father, “Why do we need a mirror?”

The father said, “Without a mirror, you can wash the whole world, but you cannot see dirt on your own face.” A Guru is that mirror. He shows us our blind spots gently and without hurting us. Because the mind hides our mistakes.Because emotions confuse us.Because sometimes we feel spiritually alone. A Guru becomes that quiet support who understands us even when others don’t.

Ramana Maharshi said: “When the student is ready, the Guru appears inside or outside. Kabir said: “Guru is greater because he shows you the God you cannot yet see.”

Vivekananda said: “The teacher wakes up what is already inside you.”

Do we need to search and run behind a Guru? No. When the heart is ready, guidance comes naturally sometimes as a person,sometimes as a book,

sometimes through an experience, and sometimes as a sudden inner realisation.We don’t choose the Guru. The Divine chooses the Guru for us.

A man got angry when another boat hit his boat. He shouted, “Who is this careless person?”

Then he saw the boat was empty. He laughed at himself, because he understood the problem was not outside and his mind created unnecessary anger. A Guru helps us see such truths early, before they trouble us.

Essence

A Guru is not someone who controls us or demands anything. A Guru is simply a light that shows the next few steps when we feel lost. He removes the blocks so our own inner wisdom can shine.

Thought for the Day

You don’t find the Guru. When your heart becomes peaceful and sincere, the Guru finds you exactly when you are ready.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 16 |  | After one of our bhajan sessions, a devotee shared | 
“We both recently retired. All these years were full of work, family responsibilities, and children’s education. Now life is quiet, children are well settled abroad. I finally have time… but I often feel empty. I miss my parents deeply. When they needed me the most in their last years, I was not available. I had my own struggles then with work stress, family issues, so many things to handle. I could not give them enough time or care. That guilt keeps coming back. Even though I pray, attend satsangs, and do chanting, that sadness returns again and again especially during ceremony. What should I do?”

His words were simple, but they carried so much truth. Many of us feel exactly like this in our hearts but we just don’t say it aloud.

Let us understand what is really happening.

For years, our mind was fully occupied with job pressure, responsibilities, deadlines, children’s future. There was no time or space for emotions. Now that life has slowed down, all those feelings that were buried inside are rising to the surface.

This is not weakness but it is natural. It is a kind of emotional cleansing. When a river flows fast, we cannot see the mud at the bottom. When it slows down, the mud becomes visible. But once it starts flowing again in the right direction, toward God ,the water becomes clear again.

We are not sad only because of the past. We are sad because our love which once flowed toward parents, children, and work now has no place to go. And God gently says, “Turn that love toward Me.”

The Bhagavad Gita says:

“Na shochati, na kankshati” :

The wise neither grieve for the past nor long for the future. They stay calm, seeing God in everything.

We often feel guilty because we think, “I should have done more.” But remember, at that time, you did the best you could, with the strength and understanding you had. Life does not allow us to go back, but it always allows us to make peace now.

When you help someone who is lonely, when you chant sincerely, or when you spread kindness ,that love reaches your parents too. The Shastras say, “Pitrudevo Bhava” our parents are divine. So don’t carry guilt; transform it into gratitude.

Here are a few simple ways to find peace and healing:

Talk to your parents in prayer.

Sit quietly and say, “Amma, Appa… I know you needed me more toward the end. I was caught up in my own struggles. Please forgive me. I did what I could with the strength I had. Bless me now to live with peace and compassion.” You will feel lighter. Love never ends and it only changes its direction.

Offer your bhajans and chanting to them. Whenever you sing or chant, say in your heart, “Let this Nama reach my parents and make them happy.” Every time we do Namasankeerthanam, our love travels beyond words.

Use your time to serve. Visit an old-age home, speak kindly to someone who feels forgotten, or share food with love. When you do that, your parents’ blessings flow through you again.

See this phase as a blessing.

God has slowed life down not to make it empty, but to make it deeper. This is your time to move from duty to divinity.

A devotee once told his Guru, “I miss my mother every day. I wish I had done more for her. The Guru said, “When you chant God’s name, include her name in your prayer.” After a few days, the devotee said, “Now I feel she is smiling beside me whenever I chant.” The Guru smiled and said, “Because when love becomes pure, it turns into devotion. And in devotion, no one is ever lost.”

Essence

What you feel is not weakness it is love searching for peace. Your parents gave you life; now, through remembrance and bhakti, you can give them peace. Don’t see this feeling as guilt but see it as God’s gentle way of expanding your heart.

Thought for the Day

When memories bring tears, turn them into prayers. When guilt arises, turn it into gratitude. When loneliness visits, know that it is God coming to sit beside you quietly, with love.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 15 |  | Is it true that spirituality means becoming almost a sanyasi? People say if… | 
A friend from USA messaged me : “Is it true that spirituality means becoming almost a sanyasi? People say if someone is spiritual, they should not care about money, family, or work. Is that what the Vedas really say?”

This is a very common question. Many people think being spiritual means leaving everything like family, job, comfort and going to the forest or ashram. But that is not what our Vedas or saints have taught.Spirituality is not about running away from life , it is about living your life in the right way.

When Arjuna told Krishna, “I don’t want to fight, I’ll give up everything,” Krishna smiled and said, “You are speaking like a wise man, but you don’t understand.” He told Arjuna, “Do your duty with the right attitude.” So, spirituality is not stopping your work but it is working with peace and surrender in your heart.

It doesn’t mean giving up money , it means not being controlled by money. It doesn’t mean leaving family but it means seeing God in your family.

A saint once said, “A lotus grows in muddy water, but it stays clean and untouched.” In the same way, a true devotee can live in the world, do all duties, but remain peaceful and pure inside.

Even the Vedas say: “Na karmana, na prajaya, dhanena tyagenaike amritatvam anashuh.” This means , not by work, not by family, not by wealth but by letting go of attachment one attains peace. It doesn’t say to throw away everything, but to drop the clinging from the heart.

A businessman once told his Guru: “I want to leave everything and go to the Himalayas.” The Guru smiled: “If you can’t stay calm with two clients here, how will you stay calm with two monkeys there?”

The Guru meant changing the place won’t change the mind. Renunciation means changing attitude, not address.

When you cook for your family with love, it becomes seva (service). When you earn money honestly, it becomes yajna (a sacred offering). When you feed someone hungry, it becomes pooja (worship). When you care for your parents or children thinking of God, it becomes bhakti (devotion). So, you don’t have to leave your home and just bring God into your home.

What our scriptures tell about our living as :-

Work as Worship: Whatever you do whether office work, cooking, or cleaning , start by saying softly, “Krishna arpanam astu” (I offer this to Krishna). Do your best and leave the result to Him.

Enjoy with Gratitude: When you eat, thank God for the food. When you rest, thank Him for the day. Gratitude itself is prayer.

Do Without Worry: Work sincerely, but don’t overthink the result. Krishna said in the Gita: “Karmanye vadhikaraste, ma phaleshu kadachana”. You have the right to do your duty, but not to control the results.

See God in All: See your family, friends, and even people who trouble you as forms of the Lord. Every situation is a chance to grow in patience and love.

The Vedas never said, “Don’t live.” They said, “Live rightly.”

The Gita never said, “Don’t earn.” It said, “Earn righteously.” Our saints never said, “Don’t love.” They said, “Love everyone as Krishna.”

A home where God’s name is remembered with love is a bigger ashram than any forest.

A heart filled with bhakti is a greater temple than any shrine.

Essence

Spirituality is not about leaving your home but it is about bringing light into it. It is not about rejecting the world but it is about seeing God in everything around you.

Thought for the Day

You don’t have to give up family, wealth, or work to be spiritual. Just fill them with God’s name, love, and remembrance. When every act becomes an offering, your whole life becomes a temple.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 14 |  | Why do we always feel that money is not enough to feel secure? | 
What we often hear:

“Whatever I earn, still feels less.”

“If I could save a little more, then I will relax.”

“What if I lose my job? What if health goes wrong? What if something unexpected happens?”

“It is not greed… it is fear.”

Even people with good income feel insecure. Even people who have more than they need, still worry. Why? Because money can buy comfort, but not certainty. Money is important. We need it for family, education, health, responsibilities.

But the mind makes one mistake. It asks money to give something it can never give which is, permanent security.The world works with money. The heart works with faith. When faith becomes weak, no amount of money feels enough.

A wealthy man once told a saint, “Swamiji, I have everything, but I live in fear. What if I lose it? What if my children waste it? What if life changes?”. The saint opened his empty palm: “When my hand is empty, I am free. When my hand is full, I am careful. When it overflows, I am scared.” “Security does not come from possession… it comes from surrender.”

If wealth alone gave security,

the richest people would sleep peacefully. But many are the most anxious. Money can protect the body, only God can protect the mind. Money can build a house,only God can bring peace inside it. Money is a tool. Faith is the foundation. Without foundation, even a palace feels unsafe.

A Simple Technique to Reduce Fear & Build Abundance

Every night, before sleeping, do this for just 2 minutes: Close your eyes and recall 3 things that went well today. Even small things like a meal, a conversation, a smile, a payment, a blessing. This teaches the mind to see what it has and not what it lacks.

Say softly: “I have enough for today. Tomorrow will take care of itself. God has never left me empty.”

Thank God for whatever is in your life. Gratitude turns scarcity into abundance.

In a few days, the mind becomes calmer. In a few weeks, fear starts reducing. In a few months, trust becomes natural. A fearful mind says: “What if…?”. A faithful mind says: “Even if… God is with me.”

Does this mean we should not earn? No. Earn with honesty.Save with discipline. Enjoy with gratitude. Share with compassion. But do not worship money. Worship the One who gave it.

Purandara Dasa said: “However much water flows into the ocean, the ocean never says ‘Enough’.” That is desire. But peace comes when desire becomes small and faith becomes big.

So how to feel secure? Earn money, but also earn God’s grace. Save money, but also save peace of mind. Trust your planning, but also trust His planning. Keep wealth in your hand, not in your heart. Real security is not having more. Real security is fearless trust.

Thought for the Day

Money is important, but faith is essential.A heart that trusts God sleeps peacefully , whether the wallet is full or not. Real security comes not from what you hold… but from whom you hold on to.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 13 |  | A gentle devotee messaged me saying : “I have No office, no pressure, no… | 
A gentle devotee messaged me saying : “I have No office, no pressure, no deadlines. I like bhajans because I love Carnatic music. I don’t have patience for long discourses. I spend most of my time with my grandchildren. My joy is in them. Days pass in cooking, feeding, telling stories, and playing with them.

I hardly get time to think about spirituality. I join bhajan classes only when I am free.. What should I do?”

Many people think spirituality comes only when we sit in a puja room with eyes closed, incense lit, and the world switched off. But real spirituality begins when we realize God does not ask for our time and we just have to bring Him into the time we already have.

Once a householder told Sri Ramakrishna paramahamsa:

“Master, I have children, family, responsibilities. Where can I find time for God?. Ramakrishna smiled:

“When a mother feeds her child, is she counting minutes?

When a grandmother plays with grandchildren, does she feel burdened?. Love does not need separate time. Just mix God in the actions you already love.”

A grandmother was cutting fruits for her grandson. While cutting, she softly chanted “Govinda… Govinda…” She did not sit for long puja. She did not read scriptures. Yet her eyes were filled with bhakti every time the child smiled because she saw Krishna in him.. One day someone asked her:”Aren’t you sad that you don’t have time for spirituality?” She laughed and said: “When my Krishna is sitting on my lap, why should I look for Him in a temple?”. That is not poetry. That is Vedanta. Where there is love, God is already present.

Three Small Shifts That Change Everything. You don’t need to change your life. Just add awareness.

Turn Bhajan into Bhakti. Right now you enjoy bhajans as music. Add one small thought:

“Every note is my offering to God.” Music becomes worship. Saint Tyagaraja didn’t sing for applause. He sang for Rama. That is why his songs still make hearts melt.

Turn Family Time into God Time. When you feed your grandchild, tell yourself: “I am feeding the Lord who has come as this child.” When you put them to sleep, whisper God’s name softly. Your lap becomes a temple. Didn’t Yashoda play with Krishna and scold Him too?. Her daily life was devotion.

Use Small ‘Pauses’ and You don’t need 1 hour. Use 10 seconds: Between two chores, say “Narayana.” While closing the kitchen, “Thank You, Krishna.” When the child laughs, say, “Your leela, Krishna.” Little drops fill the ocean.

Someone asked Kabir: “You don’t go to temple daily. Are you not a devotee?” Kabir replied: “When I weave cloth, I sing His name. When I cook, I remember His gift. When I sleep, I sleep in His lap. If God is in my breath, do I need a bell or lamp?”. Spirituality is not a schedule. It is a feeling.

What the Scriptures Say :”Yat karma karomi tat tad akhilam,

Krishna arpanam astu. Whatever I do, I offer to Krishna.You don’t need more time.You only need more remembrance.

Essence

Bhakti is not only sitting in meditation.Bhakti is living with awareness that God is with you, around you, and acting through you. If your heart melts while listening to music, that is devotion. If you serve your family with love, God is already present in your home..

The Techniques for a Devotee at Home : While cooking: chant a short nama ,”Govinda… Govinda…” While walking: “Ram Ram…” softly. Before sleeping, have 2 minutes of gratitude. While listening to bhajans, close your eyes for a few seconds, feel the lyrics, not just the raga. Slowly, music becomes meditation. Service becomes worship. Family becomes temple.

Thought for the Day

You don’t need to leave life to find God. You only need to invite God into the life you already live.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 12 |  | One of our satsang friends asked: “My mind is full of work pressure. When I… | 
One of our satsang friends asked: “My mind is full of work pressure. When I sit for prayer or bhajan, the mind runs everywhere. I cannot even sit for 10 minutes. How can I be spiritual in such a life?”

What most people think: “Let life become calm… then I will pray.” But life never becomes fully calm. Something will always be there. No one has ever said: “I was totally peaceful and problem-free, so I started praying.” Life never becomes empty for devotion. Devotion is what makes life light.

A devotee said to Bhagwan Ramana Maharishi : “Bhagavan, I want to meditate… but I have so many thoughts.” Bhagavan smiled:

“You don’t have to fight your thoughts.Sit with them.Slowly the thoughts get tired, but the Self does not.” You don’t defeat the mind but you simply stop fearing the mind.

What is the Principle ? How do we that ?:

Be a Witness. You don’t have to stop thoughts. Just notice them.

Example 1 : Cricket Match

You watch a match on TV.

Virat Kohli is batting.You feel tense, you shout, you react.But still you know: “I am only watching. I am not the player.”In the same way, let thoughts run. Just watch them.Don’t enter the field.

Example 2 : WhatsApp Notifications

Your phone keeps giving notifications:Ting… Ting… Ting…Do you open every message? No. Thoughts are like notifications.Let them come. Just say: “Thought noted,” and continue your work or prayer.

Example 3 : Listening to a Bhajan

You are listening to a peaceful bhajan.Suddenly the mind thinks: “Email… bill… work…”

Don’t chase the thought. Don’t get angry. When a child runs away, the mother gently brings the child back. In the same way, bring your mind back to the bhajan. Even if it runs 10 times, bring it back 11 times. That is devotion.

What We Should avoid ?

Sometimes people read a sloka, chant a mantra, or sing a kirtan only with the mouth. The eyes are on the book, the voice is speaking, but the mind is somewhere else. Body is in front of God. Tongue is chanting. But the mind is thinking of office, bills, phone, food…. This is like starting the car and leaving it in neutral.The engine makes sound, fuel is used, but the car doesn’t move even one inch.

Spirituality is not just sound but it is attention. Imagine you are reading a book.Your eyes are moving line by line, but suddenly you realize: “I read three pages… but I don’t know what I just read!” The reading happened automatically, but the mind was not there. In the same way,if we chant a sloka only with the mouth,and the mind is travelling everywhere,the chanting becomes mechanical.

Why This is a Problem?

When the mouth chants and the mind wanders: The heart does not connect. Peace does not come. Bhakti does not grow. It is like calling someone on the phone and putting the phone down while they are talking. You are “technically connected,”but you are not really listening.

What To Do Instead?

Don’t try to chant faster. Don’t force the mind. Just bring the attention back gently.Even if the mind runs 20 times, bring it back 21 times. That effort is bhakti. That returning is devotion.That awareness is sadhana.

Thought for the Day

Don’t chase every thought. Don’t fear every worry. Just stay where you are and watch. When the mind returns to you, that is prayer.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 11 |  | Why does pious and good-hearted person face so many difficulties in life… | 
A question from one of my satsang friend : Why does pious and good-hearted person face so many difficulties in life, despite doing so many good deeds?

What we often hear:

“Why do honest people face more problems?”

“People who cheat seem to succeed, but the sincere ones struggle.”

“Even devotees go through illness, failure, loss… why?”

Many of us silently ask this question. Sometimes we don’t say it aloud, but the heart wonders.

A devotee once asked his Guru, “Why does God give more difficulties to good people?” The Guru smiled and pointed to a potter working with clay. The potter was shaping beautiful pots. He tapped each pot gently. Some he put aside. Some he tapped harder again and again. The devotee asked, “Why are you hitting some pots more?”The potter said, “Only the strongest pots can handle the tapping. Those are the ones that will go into the fire and become the best. The weak ones will break even before the fire touches them.”

The Guru looked at the devotee and said, “God tests only those He plans to strengthen.” When floods come, a weak tree falls. A strong tree bends, but survives. Suffering does not break good people but it shines them.

In Hindu tradition, every metal becomes divine only after fire. Sandalwood releases fragrance only when rubbed. Sugarcane gives juice only when crushed. Gold shines only when heated. Diamonds are formed only under pressure. So why feel punished?. Maybe we are being polished.

Does this mean God wants us to suffer?. No. Saints say, Difficulties are not punishment, they are preparation. Not to reduce life…but to resize the ego and increase faith.

When a mother takes her child to the doctor for vaccination, the child cries, “Why are you hurting me?” but the mother knows it’s for protection. Likewise, God gives us what our soul needs, not what our mind demands.

Andal conveys : “எது நடந்தாலும் அது நாராயணன் அருள்” “Whatever happens is Narayana’s grace.”

Grace does not always come as comfort. Sometimes grace comes as courage. So next time life hits you hard, tell yourself: “Maybe I am a strong pot. Maybe He is preparing me for something sacred.”

Thought for the Day

Good people don’t suffer because God is unfair. They suffer because their heart is strong enough to grow. Pain is temporary and grace is permanent.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 10 |  | We remember God only when we have problems. Why don’t we remember God when… | 
One friend asked : We remember God only when we have problems. Why don’t we remember God when things are going well?

What we often hear when a young couple gets married: “First let the family settle…then we will think of spirituality.”

When children are born: “Once they grow up, we will get time.”

When kids go to school: “Big responsibilities now… their future is important.”

When kids become youth: “College, career, marriage… no peace at all!”

When kids get married: “Now we have in-laws, new responsibilities, new tensions.”

When grandchildren arrive: “Let them grow… since my son and daughter in law are working, I need to take care of the grand kid.. spiritually we will focus later”

And after retirement: “We thought this is the time for satsang and peace… but now health issues, money worries, BP, sugar, heart, hospital…and grandchildren’s studies, marriage, visas… and what if something happens to us?”

The whole life goes in waiting for peace…but peace never comes. Because worries don’t end; they change shape.

A wise old master once said something shocking: “Most people don’t die , they just get exhausted.” A devotee asked, “Exhausted from what?”. The master said, “Exhausted from worrying about a life they never lived.” We spend life preparing to live…but never actually live. We plan for peace…but never experience peace.

We want to nourish the soul…but every day the mind says: “Not today, tomorrow.”, “After this responsibility.” “After this problem.” “After everything is perfect.”But life is never perfect.If we wait for a problem-free day to remember God,we will be worshipping only at our own funeral.

Saints remind us: Spiritual life does not begin after worries end.Spiritual life begins while worries exist. Why?….Because devotion is not a luxury for peaceful days,It is a medicine for stressful days. If we wait for retirement to touch God, we will reach God tired, anxious, and empty.

Satsang, bhajan, pooja ,they are not rituals.They are oxygen for a suffocating mind.But if they become mechanical, it means: The lips are moving, but the mind hasn’t arrived. The body is sitting in pooja,but the heart is still in worries.

One elderly devotee told a saint: “I spent my whole life worrying about my children.

Today they are settled abroad.

Now I sit alone and instead of God, only fear sits with me.”The saint said gently:”All your life, you watered the garden outside. Now water the garden inside.Because after a certain age,flowers inside are the only ones that stay.”

The truth we rarely speak: By the time we look for peace,

the body is weak,sleep is shallow,mind is scared,and life is almost over. We prepare for our children’s future,but forget to prepare for our own eternity. We insure our house and car,

but not our soul. We build deposits for emergencies, but forget the biggest emergency, our restless mind.

So what should we do? Not run away from responsibilities. But along with responsibilities, we must: Spend even 10 minutes daily in silence. Chant with feeling, not speed. Listen to bhajans with eyes closed. Replace worry with “Govinda… Govinda…”. Feed the mind with trust, not fear

Spirituality should not be the last chapter. It should be the background music of the entire book.

Thought for the Day

Don’t postpone God till life becomes peaceful. Life becomes peaceful only when God comes in.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 9 |  | I am scared about getting old . what if I become a burden to others? | 
Question: I am scared about getting old . what if I become a burden to others?

What we often hear : “These days, I feel like I am slowing down. I forget things, get tired easily, can’t do what I used to. Children are busy with their lives . I don’t want to trouble anyone. Sometimes I wonder what if I fall sick? Who will take care of me? I don’t want to become a burden. I get scared when I see a few of my friends of my age leaving this world after illness”

How often this worry sits quietly in our minds , not spoken, but always present, especially at night.

Our saints remind us gently growing old is not a fall, it is a return. When the body slows, the soul finally gets space to rest. When the outer roles fade as a parent, worker, caretaker while the inner role begins: being a child of God again.

Just like the moon looks smaller after full moon, but still carries the same light, aging only removes the noise, not the worth. God doesn’t see wrinkles, He sees devotion.

Once, an old devotee came to Ramakrishna Paramahamsa and said tearfully, “Swami, I can no longer sit long for meditation. I forget mantras. I feel useless.”

Ramakrishna smiled and said,

“When a child learns to walk, the mother holds its hand.

When the child grows tired, the mother carries it. So too ,when your strength fades, let God carry you.”

That is the essence of spiritual aging , it is not losing control, it is learning to be carried by faith.

When your hands tremble and you can’t do long pooja, just whisper His name ,that one word is enough. When your eyes can’t read the shloka, listen to bhajans . He hears your heart, not your pronunciation. When you can’t fast or climb the temple steps, offer your smile, your kindness, your forgiveness. A quiet heart is the highest offering.

Think of an old veena ,maybe the strings are worn, but the music it carries is deeper, softer, full of bhava.That is how a life of prayer sounds in old age.

Saint Purandara Dasa sang:

“When we can’t carry life by our strength, He comes to carry us by His grace.

Saint Meera said “When all have left, my Giridhari stays.”That’s the quiet faith that sustains old age , the company that never leaves.

Don’t fear becoming a burden.

God is quietly shifting you from doing to being. You cared for others all your life , now let Him care for you. Dependence is not weakness; it’s divine surrender.

Thought for the day

When your body grows weak, let your faith grow strong. The hands that once held others are now held by Him.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 8 |  | What Is Bhakti and Why It Matters? | 
You might have heard people say, “Be devoted,” or “Have bhakti.” But what is bhakti, really? Is it lighting a lamp? Chanting a name? Going to temples? Or giving up everything to meditate?

Actually bhakti is none of these alone. And yet, it is hidden inside all of them. Bhakti means love pure, unconditional love for the Divine.

It is the same love that makes a mother care for her child endlessly, the same surrender that makes you close your eyes and say, “Whatever happens, I trust You.”

Bhakti is not about rules, fear, or show. It is that invisible connection between your heart and the One who created you.

It is love that doesn’t expect anything in return.

Bhakti is not for saints alone. You don’t need to renounce, wear robes, or go to the Himalayas. Bhakti starts right where you are in your kitchen, in traffic, in your office, at your bedside.

When you whisper “Rama,” “Krishna,” “Shiva,” “Devi,” whatever name you love and your eyes soften for a moment, that is bhakti.

When you feed someone hungry, or forgive someone who hurt you that is bhakti too.

Bhakti is the path of the heart.

It doesn’t ask: “How much do you know?” It asks: “How much do you love?”

Our scriptures say there are many paths to the Divine:

• Karma yoga - the path of right action,

• Jnana yoga - the path of wisdom,

• Raja yoga- the path of meditation, and

• Bhakti yoga- the path of love.

Of all these, the saints say bhakti is the simplest and most powerful especially in this age (Kaliyuga). Because when the mind is restless and the world is noisy, only love can quieten it.

Thyagaraja said:

“Without bhakti, even music is empty but with bhakti, even silence sings.”

Meera Bai danced and sang, saying:

“I have no rituals, no wisdom I just call His name, and that is enough.”

How to Begin

You don’t need a big plan. Just begin with one small step sincerely.

• Start with remembrance. Say His name once a day from the heart, not as a task. Even “Thank You” can be your first prayer.

• Make it personal. Talk to God like you talk to your closest friend. Laugh, cry, question, even complain ; He listens to everything.

• See Him in everything. The morning sunlight, a child’s laughter, your cup of coffee ; all are reminders of Him. Slowly, the line between life and worship starts to fade.

• Don’t aim for perfection. Bhakti is not about being flawless. It is about being real. Even one tear of honest longing is worth more than a thousand rituals without feeling.

The Essence

Bhakti is not about doing more, it is about feeling deeper. It is not what you offer in your hands, but what you offer in your heart.

Thought for the day

When you can’t control life, love the One who controls it.

That is where bhakti begins.

@@ 7 |  | I go to Vishnu temple on Saturdays, Shiva temple on Mondays, Devi temple… | 
One person asked, “ I go to Vishnu temple on Saturdays, Shiva temple on Mondays, Devi temple during Navaratri, Hanuman on Tuesdays… I sing about all gods. I listen to upanyasams, read puranas but deep inside, I still feel something is missing. I am doing everything right but where is that inner connection?”

This is not doubt but it is yearning. The soul asking softly, “I am surrounded by God everywhere and why don’t I still feel Him within?”

Our scriptures say : “Ekam sat, vipra bahudha vadanti” Truth is one; the wise call it by many names.

The purpose of bhakti is not to collect gods , but to dissolve the ego that separates “me” and “Him.” Whether you say Rama, Krishna, Shiva, Devi, or Ayyappa, it’s like calling water by different names and the thirst is the same.

Bhakti begins not when we find the “right” God but when we start feeling His presence in everything and everyone.

Once, Saint Namdev went to Pandharpur daily and prayed to Lord Vitthal. One day, he saw a dog running away with a piece of bread. He ran behind it shouting, “Hey Vitthal! You didn’t even add chutney to the bread!”

People laughed but Namdev saw no difference between God in the temple and God in the dog. That is when Vitthal appeared and said, “Now your bhakti is complete and when you see Me everywhere, not just in stone.”

You might sing about Shiva in the morning, listen to a Krishna bhajan in the car, and attend a Devi Stuti parayanam in the evening . Each form is a mirror for your love. But real bhakti shines when you start seeing divinity in the small things , the smile of a child, the care of your spouse, the patience of a colleague, the cry of a beggar.

Visiting temples purifies the outer path; seeing God in everyday life purifies the inner one.

Tukaram wrote:

“The same sugar takes many shapes such as lump, powder, syrup.But the sweetness is one.”

Thyagaraja Swami sang:

“Endaro Mahanubhavulu… andariki vandanamulu.”

“So many forms of greatness and I bow to all, for in each, my Rama lives.”

And Sri Ramakrishna Paramahamsa said:

“Many rivers flow in different directions, But all merge in the same ocean.”

Takeaway for us :

• Don’t worry about which god to pray to but pray with full heart to whichever name comes naturally. The devotion matters more than the direction.

• Instead of adding more rituals, add more love. When you offer water to a tulsi, think, “This is my moment with You, Lord.”

• See every form as His presence and not His competition.When your heart becomes simple, God Himself becomes near.

Thought for the day :

“Bhakti is not about choosing one form of God . It is about feeling one God in every form.”

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 6 |  | We got introduced to satsangs and namasankeerthanam only later in life… but… | 
A question from one of our friends: We got introduced to satsangs and namasankeerthanam only later in life… but our children don’t seem interested at all. They don’t come for any poojas or bhajans, they are busy with their gadgets and own world. Even when we try motivating them, they turn a deaf ear. What can we do? I feel helpless.

What people usually say:

“I tell my son to sit for 10 minutes of sloka , he says he is tired.”

“My daughter says, ‘God is in my heart, why do I need rituals?’

‘ I feel sad we never taught them this side of life early enough.’

This is something many of us quietly feel. But we must remember that bhakti does not grow by pressure; it blossoms by presence.

Let me share a small story I heard in a satsang.

A devotee once asked his Guru, “Swamiji, my son never joins me for pooja or bhajan. I have tried everything but nothing works.”

The Guru smiled and said, “When you light a lamp in your house, does it call your son to come and see the light?”

The man said, “No, Swamiji. The light shines on its own.”

The Guru said, “Then just keep your lamp burning. When darkness comes, he will come running to that light.”

How true! We didn’t turn towards bhakti because someone forced us. Something in life like a song, a saint’s story, a tear of surrender which in turn touched us deeply. That is when the seed of devotion sprouted.

Our children too will find that moment ,maybe not now, but someday. Our job is not to push, but to keep the house soaked in Bhagwan Nama, so that when their hearts are ready, they know where to return.

Sri Ramakrishna Paramahamsa beautifully said:

“Even if a seed lies buried for years, when the rain comes, it sprouts.”

Sant Tukaram also reminds us:

“Don’t push the parrot to chant the Name. Sing it yourself, and even the walls will echo it.”

So, let us keep our bhakti alive with joy, not worry. When our children see peace on our face, kindness in our words, and strength in our surrender, they will start wondering, “What gives them that calm?”

That curiosity itself is God’s grace beginning to work.

We can’t give bhakti by instruction ,we can only live it. Let our devotion be the fragrance that silently draws them closer to Him.

Thought for the day:

Don’t chase your children towards God. Just shine like His light and one day, they will find their way home.

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 5 |  | I chant slokas and sing bhajans sincerely but I don’t always know their… | 
A Question from another friend : I chant slokas and sing bhajans sincerely but I don’t always know their meaning. Is that still true bhakti?”

What we often say:

“I chant Vishnu Sahasranamam daily, but honestly, I don’t know what most of the names mean.”

“I sing bhajans with feeling, but someone told me that I should know the meaning . I may not know Sanskrit, but my heart feels peaceful when I chant or sing”

This is a very sincere doubt and it comes only from people who truly care. But here is the good news. God understands the heart, not the grammar.

Saints tell us: Meaning is for the mind; feeling is for the heart. When the heart is awake, even a single word “Rama” is enough.The Name itself carries the meaning.

Think of how a child calls, “Amma!” The child doesn’t know the meaning of the word but the mother runs instantly.

Why? Because it is not the word that matters , it is the love behind it.

That is how the Lord listens too. When we chant His name sincerely even without knowing it still reaches Him directly.

Once a scholar laughed at a poor devotee who mispronounced “Vishnu Sahasranamam.” He said, “You don’t even know what you’re saying!”The devotee replied humbly, “Sir, maybe I don’t know the meaning , but Vishnu does.”

That night, the scholar dreamt of Vishnu smiling and saying,

“The devotee who mispronounced My name reached Me faster than you because he called Me with love, not pride.” The next morning, the scholar touched the devotee’s feet.

Tukaram said:

“God does not count the words and He counts the tears.”

Namdev said:

“The Name itself contains the meaning. Say it with love, and it reveals its truth.”

Thyagaraja Swami sang:

“Endaro Mahanubhavulu andariki vandanamulu” —

saluting those saints who sang not to show knowledge, but to express pure longing for the Lord.

When you see a lamp and say “Deepam,” you may not know the full Sanskrit verse but the flame still gives you light. In the same way, chanting Nama or sloka even without meaning still gives you peace, because the power lies in the vibration and the devotion behind it.

But over time, when you also learn the meaning, the mind and heart unite and that’s when bhakti quietly matures into jnana (realization).

So don’t feel guilty let understanding grow naturally.

What matters most is shraddha (faith) and bhava (feeling).

Where to start practising?

• Chant with feeling, not speed. Even if you don’t know the meaning, say it with reverence.

• Learn one line a week. Slowly understand one sloka’s meaning at a time not as study, but as reflection.

• Before chanting, pray: “I may not understand every word, but You know my heart.

Understanding adds clarity but love adds life. Bhakti is not about how much you know , it’s about how deeply you feel.

Thought for the day:

“God listens not to your pronunciation but to your pulse. The Name spoken with love reaches Him faster than the scripture recited with pride.”

Dhanyosmi🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 4 |  | We do so many rituals like poojas, slokas, daily parayanam, temple visits… | 
A Question from one of my friend: We do so many rituals like poojas, slokas, daily parayanam, temple visits… what more does God expect from us?”

What people usually say:

“I’m doing all the right things like daily prayers, nitya parayanam, Ekadasi, temple seva, annadanam… still, I don’t feel peaceful. What more should I do for God to be happy with me? Sometimes it feels mechanical like duty, not devotion.”

It’s a genuine feeling that after years of practice, something still feels missing.We wonder : “Am I chanting correctly? Did I miss a rule? Is my devotion not enough?”

Rituals are like the outer shell ; they prepare us, discipline us, keep our mind clean. But the fruit is inside : bhava, the feeling behind it.

A lamp is only useful when it gives light and not just when it looks pretty. Similarly, all our poojas, shlokas, and visits are meant to light up one thing which is our heart’s awareness of Him.

Rituals are like knocking on His door. But love is when we finally open it.

Once, a great scholar visited Sri Ramakrishna Paramahamsa. He proudly said, “I can recite all the Vedas from memory!” Ramakrishna smiled and asked, “Very good. But has the Veda entered your heart yet?” The scholar was puzzled. Ramakrishna said softly, “It’s like counting how many times you’ve said ‘sugar’ it won’t make your mouth sweet. To taste sweetness, you must become it.”

Our rituals are like repeating “sugar.” Their goal is to make our heart sweet with humility, love, forgiveness, and surrender.

Think of two people making coffee every morning. One does it as a habit with the same cup, same routine. Another does it thinking of his wife’s smile when she drinks it.

Both did the same act, but only one was filled with love. That’s the difference between ritual and bhakti. You can chant Vishnu Sahasranamam but if your heart softens when you say “Govinda!”, if a tear falls unknowingly, that’s the pooja God waits for.

Tukaram wrote:

“You wash the stone, but forget the soul. God lives not in the idol, but in the feeling.”

Meera Bai sang:

“I have built temples in my heart. There, my Giridhari lives without ritual or rule.”

And Thyagaraja Swami, the bhakta of Rama, said:

“Endaro Mahanubhavulu… andariki vandanamulu”

“Many are the great souls ,bow to all, for in each one, my Rama lives.”

Continue your rituals and they are beautiful offerings. But alongside them, add these silent ones: To Forgive someone who hurt you. To Help someone quietly, expecting nothing. To Sit in silence and say, “I love You, Lord” without words, without reason. That’s the “extra” God asks and not another pooja, but another ounce of love.

Thought for the day:

“Rituals please the eyes of the world. But love pleases the heart of God.”

Dhanyosmi 🙇‍♂️🙏

@@ 3 |  | With all the stress and business of daily life, how do we find time to… | 
With all the stress and business of daily life, how do we find time to remember God or offer our prayers?

We often feel life is too full with work, family, worries, endless to-do lists, loans /EMI, children’s marriage & education, peer pressure etc. and spiritual time keeps slipping away.

But Sant Tukaram reminds us through his abhang “Janma vari kele dhyana” that we don’t have to step away from life to find God. He says, “My whole life itself has become meditation.”

Tukaram teaches that prayer is not separate from daily living.

When we do every task like cooking, driving, working, caring with love, awareness, and gratitude, that itself becomes prayer. God doesn’t need rituals; He lives in our every breath.

So how do we live this way in our busy world?

Here’s how in small, simple ways:

• When you wake up, take a quiet breath and whisper, “Thank you for this day.” That is prayer.

• While cooking, think, “May this food bring health and happiness to my family.” That thought is devotion.

• While driving, instead of getting irritated, hum God’s name or stay calm — that patience is meditation.

• At work, do your job sincerely and help someone if you can , that sincerity is worship.

• Before sleeping, recall one good thing that happened and say, “Thank you.” That remembrance is namasmaranam.

When we live like this, we don’t need to find time for prayer , life itself becomes prayer. Every breath, every act, every moment can be an offering of love.

Live with devotion, not just pray with words. God lives not in rituals, but in the heart that remembers Him through life itself.

Radhe krishna 🙏

@@ 2 |  | How do we walk the path of bhakti while fulfilling our worldly duties- I… | 
Another question from the same person to the above reply: How do we walk the path of bhakti while fulfilling our worldly duties- I have big family commitments to fulfill?

So beautifully said and your thoughts reflect what every sincere seeker feels in this stage of life.

Travel, family duties, health challenges all these are not distractions, but part of our spiritual path itself. When we do our daily karma with awareness and remembrance of the Lord, that itself becomes sadhana.

Satsang and Nama Smarana don’t ask us to leave samsara & they help us carry peace within it.

Even conflicts, anger, and restlessness are reminders to pause and remember the bhagwan nama slowly, it starts cleansing the mind like gentle rain washing the dust.

As the saints say, “Don’t run away from life bring Lord into every moment of it.”. That is the essence of bhakti and the simplest way toward inner peace. 🙏

@@ 1 |  | A question was asked - what is the difference between ‘satsang’ and… | 
A question was asked - what is the difference between ‘satsang’ and ‘Namasankeerthanam’?

Satsang is being in the company of those with Sattva Guna ie., noble thoughts and divine vibrations. As Adi Shankaracharya says, when we are in the presence of such souls, our thoughts become clear and pure, leading us to detach from material distractions and attain inner peace. This is the true spirit of Sajjana Sangha, the starting point of our spiritual journey.

From Satsang arises Namasankeerthanam , the joyful remembrance of the Lord’s name (Nama Smaranam), which purifies the heart and fills the mind with divine energy.

Through continuous Nama Smarana and Satsanga, one moves steadily towards Jeevan Mukthi ie., liberation while living , realizing the Paramatma within ourselves, needing no search outside.

May we all stay connected in divine company and chant the sacred name with love and devotion.

Dhanyosmi 🙏🙇‍♂️
